2^{11}\cdot 5\cdot 7\cdot 13
. It follows that
(L\cap B_{0})\cap Y=\mathrm{G}_{2}(4)
, and then
S\cong\mathrm{G}_{2}(4)
, which is impossible.

Assume that
(m,q)=(3,2)
. Then
|L\cap Y|
is divisible by
2^{5}\cdot 7
, and
L\cap B_{0}\cong 2^{9}{:}\mathrm{L}_{3}(4)
,
\mathrm{Sp}_{6}(2)
,
\mathrm{L}_{3}(4){.}2
,
\mathrm{U}_{4}(3){.}2
or
\mathrm{M}_{22}
. Noting that
S\preccurlyeq(L\cap Y)\mathbf{rad}(L\cap B_{0})/\mathbf{rad}(L\cap B_{0})
,
with the help of GAP, we search the subgroups of
(L\cap B_{0})/\mathbf{rad}(L\cap B_{0})
with order divisible by
2^{5}\cdot 7\over(2^{5},|\mathbf{rad}(L\cap B_{0})|)
. It follows that
S\cong\mathrm{L}_{3}(2)
or
\mathrm{L}_{3}(3)
, desired as in part (2) of this lemma.

For the left candidates for
L\cap B_{0}
, we have
\Phi^{*}_{2m}(q)\neq 1
, and
|L\cap Y|
has a divisor
q^{2m-1}\Phi^{*}_{2m}(q)
.
Let
\overline{B}=(L\cap B_{0})/\mathbf{rad}(L\cap B_{0})
and
\overline{Y}=(L\cap Y)\mathbf{rad}(L\cap B_{0})/\mathbf{rad}(L\cap B_{0})
.
Then
S\preccurlyeq\overline{Y}
, and
|\overline{Y}|
is divisible by
\Phi^{*}_{2m}(q)
.
By
[
31
, pp. 38, Proposition B]
, we conclude that both
|\overline{B}^{\infty}|
and
|\overline{B}^{\infty}\cap\overline{Y}|
are divisible by
\Phi^{*}_{2m}(q)
.
Thus Lemma
7.1
works for the preimages of

\overline{B}^{\infty}\cap\overline{Y}
and
\overline{B}^{\infty}
in
\mathrm{GL}_{m}(q^{2})
(or in
\mathrm{GL}_{2m}(q)
when
\overline{B}^{\infty}\cong\mathrm{PSp}_{2m}(q)
).
Then the lemma follows.
∎

Lemma 7.4
.

Let
S
be a nonabelian simple group with a subgroup of prime index
r
.
Let
q=p^{f}
for a prime
p
and integer
f\geq 1
,
L=\mathrm{P\Omega}^{+}_{8}(q)
and
\mathbf{soc}(G_{0})=L
.
Suppose that
G_{0}=A_{0}B_{0}
is a core-free maximal factorization,
A_{0}\cap L\cong\Omega_{7}(q)
and
G_{0}=A_{0}Y
for
Y\leq B_{0}
.
Suppose that
S
is the unique insolvable composition factor of
Y
, and
\pi(S)=\pi(Y)
.
Then either
S\cong\mathrm{L}_{2}(q^{2})
and
r=q^{2}+1
with
p=2
and
f
a power of
2
, or
(S,q)
is one of
(\mathrm{A}_{5},3)
,
(\mathrm{A}_{7},2)
,
(\mathrm{A}_{7},3)
and
(\mathrm{A}_{7},7)
.

Proof.

By
[
31
, Table 4]
,
L\cap B_{0}
is described as follows:

\tiny\begin{array}[]{l|l|l|l|l|l}L\cap B_{0}&(L\cap A_{0})^{\tau}&\mathsf{P}_{i}&{}^{\bf\hat{}}({q\mp 1\over e}{\times}\Omega^{\pm}_{6}(q)){.}2^{e}&(\mathrm{PSp}_{2}(q)\otimes\mathrm{PSp}_{4}(q)){.}2&\Omega^{-}_{8}(\sqrt{q})\\
\hline\cr&\mbox{triality }\tau&i\in\{1,3,4\}&e=(2,q-1)&q\mbox{ odd}&q\mbox{ square}\\
\hline\cr L\cap B_{0}&\mathrm{A}_{9}&(\mathrm{L}_{2}(4)\times\mathrm{L}_{2}(4)){.}2^{2}&(\mathrm{L}_{2}(16)\times\mathrm{L}_{2}(16)){.}2^{2}&\Omega^{+}_{8}(2)\\
\hline\cr&q=2&q=2&q=4,G_{0}\geq L{.}2&q=3\end{array}

It is easily shown that
S\preccurlyeq L\cap Y
, and
|L\cap Y|
has a divisor
{1\over o}|G_{0}:A_{0}|
, where
o=|G_{0}:L|
. Note that
|G_{0}:A_{0}|={1\over(2,q-1)}q^{3}(q^{4}-1)
.

Suppose that
q=3
and
L\cap B_{0}\cong\Omega^{+}_{8}(2)
.
Then
|L\cap Y|
has a divisor
2^{3}\cdot 3^{2}\cdot 5
.
Checking the orders of maximal subgroups of
\Omega^{+}_{8}(2)
,
it follows that
L\cap Y\lesssim\mathrm{Sp}_{6}(2)
,
2^{6}{:}\mathrm{A}_{8}
,
\mathrm{A}_{9}
,
(3\times\mathrm{U}_{4}(2)){:}2
or
(\mathrm{A}_{5}\times\mathrm{A}_{5}){.}2^{2}
. Inspecting the subgroups of
\mathrm{Sp}_{6}(2)
,
\mathrm{A}_{9}
,
\mathrm{A}_{8}
,
\mathrm{U}_{4}(2)
and
\mathrm{A}_{5}\times\mathrm{A}_{5}
, we conclude that
S\cong\mathrm{A}_{5}
or
\mathrm{A}_{7}
.
If
L\cap B_{0}\cong\mathrm{A}_{9},\,(\mathrm{L}_{2}(4)\times\mathrm{L}_{2}(4)){.}2^{2}
or
(\mathrm{L}_{2}(16)\times\mathrm{L}_{2}(16)){.}2^{2}
then we have
(S,q)=(\mathrm{A}_{7},2)
,
(\mathrm{L}_{2}(q^{2}),2)
or
(\mathrm{L}_{2}(q^{2}),4)
.

Next we deal with the left factorizations
G_{0}=A_{0}B_{0}
.

Case 1
. Assume that
L\cap B_{0}
is one of
\mathsf{P}_{i},\,{}^{\bf\hat{}}({q\mp 1\over 2}{\times}\Omega^{\pm}_{6}(q)){.}2^{e},\,(\mathrm{PSp}_{2}(q)\otimes\mathrm{PSp}_{4}(q)){.}2
and
\Omega^{-}_{8}(\sqrt{q})
.
Let
\overline{B}=(L\cap B_{0})/\mathbf{rad}(L\cap B_{0})
and
\overline{Y}=(L\cap Y)\mathbf{rad}(L\cap B_{0})/\mathbf{rad}(L\cap B_{0})
. Then
S\preccurlyeq\overline{Y}
, and
|\overline{Y}|
is divisible by
{1\over a}|G_{0}:A_{0}|
, where
a
is a divisor of
o|\mathbf{rad}(L\cap B_{0})|
.
Then
S\preccurlyeq\overline{B}^{\infty}\cap\overline{Y}
, and it is easily shown that
|\overline{B}^{\infty}\cap\overline{Y}|
is divisible by
\Phi^{*}_{4}(q)
(and by
\Phi^{*}_{8}(\sqrt{q})
if
q
is a square). If
\overline{B}^{\infty}
is neither
\mathrm{U}_{4}(q)
nor

\mathrm{PSp}_{2}(q)\times\mathrm{PSp}_{4}(q)
then, applying Lemma
7.1
to the preiamge of
\overline{B}^{\infty}\cap\overline{Y}
in
\mathrm{GL}_{4}(q)
(or
\mathrm{GL}_{8}(\sqrt{q})
), we conclude that either
S\cong\mathrm{L}_{2}(q^{2})
,
p=2
and
f
is a power of
2
, or
(S,q)
is one of
(\mathrm{A}_{5},2)
,
(\mathrm{A}_{5},3)
,
(\mathrm{A}_{7},2)
,
(\mathrm{A}_{7},3)
and
(\mathrm{A}_{7},7)
.

Assume that
\overline{B}^{\infty}\cong\mathrm{U}_{4}(q)
.
Then
|\overline{B}^{\infty}\cap\overline{Y}|
has a divisor
{1\over(2,p)}q^{3}\Phi^{*}_{4}(q)
. Checking maximal subgroups
of
\mathrm{U}_{4}(q)
(refer to
[
3
, Tables 8.10 and 8.11]
), we conclude that
\overline{B}^{\infty}\cap\overline{Y}\leq[q^{4}]{:}[a_{1}]{.}\mathrm{L}_{2}(q^{2}){.}[a_{2}]
, where
a_{1}a_{2}={(q-1)(2,q^{2}-1)\over(4,q+1)}
. Applying Lemma
7.1
to a suitable quotient of
\overline{B}^{\infty}\cap\overline{Y}
, we have
S\cong\mathrm{L}_{2}(q^{2})
,
p=2
and
f
is a power of
2
.

Now let
\overline{B}^{\infty}\cong\mathrm{PSp}_{2}(q)\times\mathrm{PSp}_{4}(q)
with odd
q>3
. Then
|\overline{B}^{\infty}\cap\overline{Y}|
is divisible by
q^{3}\Phi^{*}_{4}(q)
. Let
N
be the direct factor of
\overline{B}^{\infty}
with
N\cong\mathrm{PSp}_{2}(q)
.
Noting that
S\preccurlyeq\overline{B}^{\infty}\cap\overline{Y}
,
we have
\pi(S)=\pi(\overline{B}^{\infty}\cap\overline{Y})
.
It follows that
S\not\preccurlyeq N
, and so
S\preccurlyeq(\overline{B}^{\infty}\cap\overline{Y})N/N
.
Clearly,
(\overline{B}^{\infty}\cap\overline{Y})N/N

has order divisible by
\Phi^{*}_{4}(q)
.
Since
q^{2}+1
is not a prime as
q
is odd, it follows from
Lemma
7.1
that
q=7
and
S\cong\mathrm{A}_{7}
.

Case 2
.
Assume that
L\cap A_{0}\cong\Omega_{7}(q)\cong L\cap B_{0}
. Then
B_{0}=A_{0}^{\tau}
for some triality
\tau
.
By
[
24
, Proposition 3.1.1]
,

L\cap A_{0}\cap B_{0}\cong\mathrm{G}_{2}(q)
.
Considering the limitations on
S
, we know that
\mathrm{G}_{2}(q)\not\preccurlyeq Y
. Since
G_{0}=A_{0}Y
, we have
B_{0}=(A_{0}\cap B_{0})Y
.
By Lemma
2.7
, we have a maximal core-free factorization
G_{1}=A_{1}B_{1}
with
B_{0}^{\infty}\leq G_{1}
,
(A_{0}\cap B_{0})^{\infty}\leq A_{1}
and
Y^{\infty}\leq B_{1}
.
By
[
31
, Theorem A]
,
B_{1}
can be read out from
[
31
, Tables 2 and 3]
:

\tiny\begin{array}[]{l|l|l|l|l|l}B_{1}\cap B_{0}^{\infty}&[q^{5}]{:}({q-1\over 2}{\times}\mathrm{PSp}_{4}(q)){.}2&\Omega^{\pm}_{6}(q){.}2&({q\pm 1\over 2}{\times}\mathrm{PSp}_{4}(q)){.}2^{2}&\mathrm{Sp}_{6}(2),q=3&\mathrm{S}_{9},q=3\\
\hline\cr B_{1}\cap B_{0}^{\infty}&\Omega^{\pm}_{6}(q){.}2&[q^{5}]{:}({q-1\over 2}{\times}\mathrm{PSp}_{4}(q))&\mathrm{Sp}_{2}(q){\times}\mathrm{PSp}_{4}(q)\end{array}

For
\mathrm{Sp}_{6}(2)
and
\mathrm{S}_{9}
,
we have
S\cong\mathrm{A}_{5}
or
\mathrm{A}_{7}
by checking their subgroups.
For the left candidates for
B_{1}
, by a similar argument as in Case 1, either
S\cong\mathrm{L}_{2}(q^{2})
,
p=2
and
f
is a power of
2
, or
(S,q)
is one of
(\mathrm{A}_{5},2)
,
(\mathrm{A}_{5},3)
,
(\mathrm{A}_{7},2)
,
(\mathrm{A}_{7},3)
and
(\mathrm{A}_{7},7)
.
∎

Lemma 7.5
.

Let
S
be a nonabelian simple group with a subgroup of prime index
r
.
Let
q=p^{f}
for a prime
p
and integer
f\geq 1
,
L=\mathrm{P\Omega}^{+}_{2m}(q)
and
\mathbf{soc}(G_{0})=L
for some
m\geq 5
.
Suppose that
G_{0}=A_{0}B_{0}
is a core-free maximal factorization,
A_{0}\cap L=\mathsf{N}^{-}_{2}
and
G_{0}=A_{0}Y
for
Y\leq B_{0}
.
Suppose that
S
is the unique insolvable composition factor of
Y
, and
\pi(S)=\pi(Y)
.
Then one of the following holds.

(1)

S=\mathrm{L}_{m}(q)
,
r={q^{m}-1\over q-1}
,
m
is a prime and
f
is a power of
m
.

(2)

(m,q)=(6,2)
,
S\cong\mathrm{L}_{5}(2)
and
r=31
.

Proof.

By
[
31
, Tables 1-3]
,
|G_{0}:A_{0}|={q^{2m-2}(q^{m}-1)(q^{m-1}-1)\over 2(q+1)}
, and

B_{0}
is of type
\mathsf{P}_{m}
,
\mathsf{P}_{m-1}
,
{}^{\bf\hat{}}\mathrm{GL}_{m}(2){.}2
or
{}^{\bf\hat{}}\mathrm{GL}_{m}(4){.}2
.
Let
\overline{B_{0}}=B_{0}^{\prime}/\mathbf{O}_{p}(B_{0}^{\prime})
and
\overline{Y}=(Y\cap B^{\prime})\mathbf{O}_{p}(B_{0}^{\prime})/\mathbf{O}_{p}(B_{0}^{\prime})
. Then
S\preccurlyeq\overline{Y}
and

|\overline{Y}|
is divisible by
\Phi^{*}_{m}(q)\Phi^{*}_{m-1}(q)
.
Consider the preimage of
\overline{Y}
in
\mathrm{GL}_{m}(q)
, and note that
\pi(S)=\pi(\overline{Y})
.
If
\Phi^{*}_{m}(q)=1
then
(m,q)=(6,2)
, and thus
S\cong\mathrm{L}_{5}(2)
by Lemma
7.2
.
Assume that
\Phi^{*}_{m}(q)\neq 1
. Then
S
is described as in
Lemma
7.1
. For those
S
in Table
7.8
, we have
\Phi^{*}_{m-1}(q)\neq 1
and
(|S|,\Phi^{*}_{m-1}(q))=1
. Thus
L\cong\mathrm{L}_{d}(q^{m\over d})
,
where
d
is a prime and
mf
is a power of
d
.
The only possibility is that
m=d
, and the lemma follows.
∎

Lemma 7.6
.

Let
S
be a nonabelian simple group with a subgroup of prime index
r
.
Let
q=p^{f}
for a prime
p
and integer
f\geq 1
,
L=\mathrm{P\Omega}^{+}_{2m}(q)
and
\mathbf{soc}(G_{0})=L
for some
m\geq 5
.
Suppose that
G_{0}=A_{0}B_{0}
is a core-free maximal factorization,
A_{0}\cap L=\mathsf{N}_{1}
and
G_{0}=A_{0}Y
for
Y\leq B_{0}
.
Suppose that
S
is the unique insolvable composition factor of
Y
, and
\pi(S)=\pi(Y)
.
Then one of the following holds.

(1)

S=\mathrm{L}_{d}(q^{m\over d})
,
r={q^{m}-1\over q^{m\over d}-1}
,
d
is a prime and
mf
is a power of
d
.

(2)

(S,m,q)
is one of the following triples:

(\mathrm{A}_{7},6,3)
,
(\mathrm{A}_{7},6,5)
,
(\mathrm{A}_{11},10,2)
,
(\mathrm{A}_{13},12,2)
,
(\mathrm{A}_{19},18,2)
,
(\mathrm{M}_{11},10,2)
,

(\mathrm{L}_{3}(2),6,3)
,
(\mathrm{L}_{3}(2),6,5)
,
(\mathrm{L}_{2}(11),10,2)
,
(\mathrm{L}_{2}(11),5,4)
,
(\mathrm{L}_{3}(3),12,2)
,

(\mathrm{A}_{7},6,2)
,
(\mathrm{L}_{3}(2),6,2)
and
(\mathrm{L}_{3}(3),6,2)
.

Proof.

By
[
31
, Tables 1-3]
,
L\cap B_{0}
is described as follows:

\tiny\begin{array}[]{l|l|l|l|l|l|l}L\cap B_{0}&\mathsf{P}_{m}\mbox{ or }\mathsf{P}_{m-1}&{}^{\bf\hat{}}\mathrm{GU}_{m}(q){.}2&(\mathrm{PSp}_{2}(q){\times}\mathrm{PSp}_{m}(q)){.}c&{}^{\bf\hat{}}\mathrm{GL}_{m}(q){.}2&\Omega^{+}_{m}(4).2^{2}&\Omega^{+}_{m}(16).2^{2}\\
\hline\cr&&m\mbox{ even}&q>2,m\mbox{ even},c=(2,q-1)&G_{0}\geq L.2\mbox{ if}&q=2&q=4,m\mbox{ even}\\
&&&&m\mbox{ odd}&m\mbox{ even}&G_{0}\geq L.2\\
\hline\cr L\cap B_{0}&\Omega_{9}(q).a&&\mathrm{Co}_{1}\\
\hline\cr&m=8,a\leq 2&&m=12,q=2\par\end{array}

Note that
|Y|
is divisible by
|G_{0}:A_{0}|={1\over(2,q-1)}q^{m-1}(q^{m}-1)
,
S\preccurlyeq L\cap Y
,
and
|L\cap Y|
has a divisor
{1\over o}|G_{0}:A_{0}|
, where
o=|G_{0}:L|
.

Assume that
L\cap B_{0}\cong\mathrm{Co}_{1}
. Checking the maximal subgroups of
\mathrm{Co}_{1}
with order divisible by
2^{10}\cdot 3^{2}\cdot 5\cdot 7\cdot 13
, we have
L\cap Y\leq B_{1}\cong 3{.}\mathrm{Suz}{.}2
or
(\mathrm{A}_{4}{\times}\mathrm{G}_{2}(4)){.}2
. Clearly,
S\preccurlyeq(L\cap Y)\mathbf{rad}(B_{1})/\mathbf{rad}(B_{1})
. Checking
the subgroups of
B_{1}/\mathbf{rad}(B_{1})
with order divisible by
2^{8}\cdot 3\cdot 5\cdot 7\cdot 13
, we conclude that
(L\cap Y)\mathbf{rad}(B_{1})/\mathbf{rad}(B_{1})\succcurlyeq\mathrm{G}_{2}(4)
or
\mathrm{Suz}
. This implies that
S\cong\mathrm{G}_{2}(4)
or
\mathrm{Suz}
, a contradiction.

Case 1
. Assume that
L\cap B_{0}\cong\Omega_{9}(q).a
,

\mathsf{P}_{m},\,\mathsf{P}_{m-1},\,(\mathrm{PSp}_{2}(q){\times}\mathrm{PSp}_{m}(q)){.}c
or

{}^{\bf\hat{}}\mathrm{GL}_{m}(q){.}2
.
Let
B_{1}=(L\cap B_{0})^{\prime}
and
Y_{1}=Y\cap B_{1}
.
Then
S\preccurlyeq Y_{1}
and
|Y_{1}|
is divisible by
\Phi^{*}_{m}(q)
.
If
L\cap B_{0}\cong\Omega_{9}(q).a
then
Lemmas
7.1
or
7.2
work for
B_{1}
and
Y_{1}
, and thus
S\cong\mathrm{L}_{2}(q^{4})
and
r=q^{4}+1
with
p=2
and
f
a power of
2
, desired as in part (1) of this lemma with
d=p=2
and
m=8
. (Note
\Omega_{9}(q)=\mathrm{Sp}_{8}(q)
for even
q
.)
For the left cases, choose
N\unlhd B_{1}
such that
B_{1}/N
is almost simple,
S\preccurlyeq Y_{1}N/N
and
|Y_{1}N/N|
is divisible by
\Phi^{*}_{m}(q)
.
Then
Lemma
7.1
works for the preimages of
B_{1}/N
and
Y_{1}N/N
in
\mathrm{GL}_{m}(q)
. It follows that
S
is desired as in part (1) or (2) of this lemma.

Case 2
. Assume that
L\cap B_{0}\cong\mathrm{GU}_{m}(q){.}2
for even
m
. Noting that
B_{0}=(A_{0}\cap B_{0})Y
,
by Lemma
2.6
, we have a core-free factorization

\overline{B_{0}}=\overline{(A_{0}\cap B_{0})}\,\overline{Y}
,
where

\overline{B_{0}}=B_{0}/\mathbf{rad}(B_{0})
,
\overline{(A_{0}\cap B_{0})}=(A_{0}\cap B_{0})\mathbf{rad}(B_{0})/\mathbf{rad}(B_{0})
and
\overline{Y}=Y\mathbf{rad}(B_{0})/\mathbf{rad}(B_{0}))
.
It is easily shown that
|\overline{(A_{0}\cap B_{0})}|
is divisible by
\Phi^{*}_{2m-2}(q)
. By Lemma
2.7
, we may
choose a core-free maximal factorization
G_{1}=A_{1}B_{1}
such that

\mathbf{soc}(G_{1})\cong\mathrm{U}_{m}(q)
,
|A_{1}|
is divisible by
\Phi^{*}_{2m-2}(q)
,
\overline{Y}^{\infty}\leq B_{1}
and
G_{1}=A_{1}(B_{1}\cap\overline{Y})
. By
[
31
, Theorem A]
,
either
A_{1}
is a subgroup of
G_{1}
with type
\mathsf{N}_{1}
, or

(m,q)=(6,2)
,
A_{1}\cap\mathbf{soc}(G_{1})\cong\mathrm{M}_{22}
and
B_{1}\cap\mathbf{soc}(G_{1})\cong\mathrm{U}_{5}(2)
. By Lemma
7.3
, we conclude that
S
is desired as in part (1) (with
d=p=2
) or (2) of this lemma.

Assume that
(m,q)=(6,2)
,
A_{1}\cap\mathbf{soc}(G_{1})\cong\mathrm{M}_{22}
and
B_{1}\cap\mathbf{soc}(G_{1})\cong\mathrm{U}_{5}(2)
. In this case,
|G_{1}:A_{1}|=2^{8}\cdot 3^{4}
. It is easily shown that
S\preccurlyeq\overline{Y}\cap B_{1}\cap\mathbf{soc}(G_{1})
, and
|\overline{Y}\cap B_{1}\cap\mathbf{soc}(G_{1})|
is divisible by
2^{7}\cdot 3^{3}
.
Checking the maximal subgroups of
\mathrm{U}_{5}(2)
, we conclude that

\overline{Y}\cap B_{1}\cap\mathbf{soc}(G_{1})
is solvable, a contradiction.

Case 3
. Assume that
L\cap B_{0}\cong\Omega^{+}_{m}(q^{2}).2^{2}
for
q\in\{2,4\}
and even
m
.
Suppose that
m=6
. Then
(L\cap B_{0})^{\prime}\cong\mathrm{L}_{4}(q^{2})
.
It is easily shown that
S\preccurlyeq Y\cap(L\cap B_{0})^{\prime}
, and

|Y\cap(L\cap B_{0})^{\prime}|
is divisible by
{1\over 8}q^{5}(q^{6}-1)
.
Assume that
q=4
. Then
S
is given in Lemma
7.2
, and the only possibility is that

S\cong\mathrm{L}_{3}(16)
. Then
r={16^{3}-1\over 16-1}
, which is not a prime. Thus
q\neq 4
, and so
q=2
. Checking the subgroups of maximal subgroups of
\mathrm{L}_{4}(4)
, we get

S\cong\mathrm{L}_{3}(2)
or
\mathrm{A}_{7}
, which is included in part (2) of this lemma.

Next let
m\geq 8
. Similarly as in Case 2, we have a core-free maximal factorization
G_{1}=A_{1}B_{1}
such that

\mathbf{soc}(G_{1})\cong\Omega^{+}_{m}(q^{2})
,
|A_{1}|
is divisible by
\Phi^{*}_{m-2}(q^{2})
,
S\preccurlyeq\overline{Y}^{\infty}\leq B_{1}
and
G_{1}=A_{1}(B_{1}\cap\overline{Y})
, where
\overline{Y}=Y\mathbf{rad}(B_{0})/\mathbf{rad}(B_{0}))
.

Assume thta
m=8
. If
A_{1}
has a normal subgroup isomorphic to

\mathrm{Sp}_{6}(q)
then
S\cong\mathrm{L}_{2}(q^{4})
by Lemma
7.4
, desired as in part (1) of this lemma.
Thus, by
[
31
, Table 4]
, we may let

|G_{1}:A_{1}|={1\over 2}q^{12}(q^{6}-1)(q^{4}+1)(q^{2}-1)
, and

B_{1}\cap\mathbf{soc}(G_{1})\cong[q^{12}]{:}\mathrm{GL}_{4}(q^{2})
or
\mathrm{GL}_{4}(4){.}2
.
Using Lemmas
7.1
and
7.2
, by a similar argument as in the proof of Lemma
7.5
, we conclude that
S\cong\mathrm{L}_{2}(q^{4})
or
\mathrm{L}_{3}(q^{2})
. Noting that

\pi(S)=\pi(B_{1}\cap\overline{Y})
and
|B_{1}\cap\overline{Y}|
is divisible by
|G_{1}:A_{1}|
, it follows that
q=2
and
S\cong\mathrm{L}_{2}(16)
, desired as in part (1) of this lemma.

Let
m\geq 10
. Then, by
[
31
, Theorem A and Proposition 2.5]
,
A_{1}
has type
\mathsf{N}_{1}
,
\mathsf{N}^{-}_{2}
or
{}^{\bf\hat{}}\mathrm{GU}_{m\over 2}(q^{2}){.}2
(with
{m\over 2}
even). For
\mathsf{N}^{-}_{2}
, by Lemma
7.5
,
S\cong\mathrm{L}_{m\over 2}(q^{2})
with

{m\over 2}
a prime and
mf
a power of
{m\over 2}
,
yielding
m=2
, a contradiction.
Assume that
A_{1}\cap\mathbf{soc}(G_{1})\cong{}^{\bf\hat{}}\mathrm{GU}_{m\over 2}(q^{2}){.}2
. By
[
31
, Table 1]
,
B_{1}
has type
\mathsf{P}_{1}
or
\mathsf{N}^{+}_{2}
. It follows that
(|B_{1}|,\Phi^{*}_{m}(q))=1
.
Clearly,
\Phi^{*}_{m}(q)\neq 1
.
Recalling that
\pi(S)=\pi(Y)
and
|Y|
is divisible by
\Phi^{*}_{m}(q)
, since
S\preccurlyeq B_{1}\cap\overline{Y}
, it follows that
(|B_{1}|,\Phi^{*}_{m}(q))\neq 1
, a contradiction.
Thus
A_{1}
is of type
\mathsf{N}_{1}
.
Then, by an induction on
m
, the lemma follows.
∎

Lemma 7.7
.

Let
S
be a nonabelian simple group with a subgroup of prime index
r
.
Let
q=2^{f}
,
L=\mathrm{Sp}_{2m}(q)
and
\mathbf{soc}(G_{0})=L
, where
m\geq 3
and
mf>3
.
Suppose that
G_{0}=A_{0}B_{0}
is a core-free maximal factorization,
A_{0}\cap L\cong\mathrm{GO}_{2m}^{-}(q)
and
G_{0}=A_{0}Y
for
Y\leq B_{0}
.
Suppose that
S
is the unique insolvable composition factor of
Y
, and
\pi(S)=\pi(Y)
.
Then
B_{0}
is given as in
[
31
, Table 1]
,
and one of the following holds.

(1)

S\cong\mathrm{L}_{d}(q^{m\over d})
, where
d
is a prime and
mf
is a power of
d
.

(2)

(S,m,q)
is one of
(\mathrm{A}_{7},6,2)
,
(\mathrm{L}_{3}(2),6,2)
and
(\mathrm{A}_{13},12,2)
.

(3)

B_{0}=\mathsf{P}_{m}
,
(S,m,q)
is one of
(\mathrm{A}_{7},4,2)
and
(\mathrm{L}_{5}(2),6,2)
.

Proof.

Assume that
B_{0}
is described as in
[
31
, Table 3]
.
Then
(m,q)=(4,2)
,
G_{0}=\mathrm{Sp}_{8}(2)
,
B_{0}\cong\mathrm{S}_{10}
and
A_{0}\cap B_{0}\cong\mathrm{S}_{3}\times\mathrm{S}_{7}
, see
[
31
, pp.97, 5.1.9]
. By
[
31
, Theorem D]
,
Y
is a primitive group of degree
10
. Noting that
\mathrm{A}_{10}\not\preccurlyeq Y
, it follows that
S=\mathbf{soc}(Y)\cong\mathrm{A}_{6}
or
\mathrm{A}_{5}
. Noting the limitations on
S
, we have
Y\cong\mathrm{A}_{5}
or
\mathrm{S}_{5}
. Then
Y\cong\mathrm{S}_{5}
as
|Y|
is divisible by
|G_{0}:A_{0}|=2^{3}\cdot 3\cdot 5
, and part (1) of this lemma follows.

Assume that
B_{0}
is described as in
[
31
, Table 2]
. Then
L\cap B_{0}\cong\mathrm{G}_{2}(q)
.
Noting that
B_{0}=(A_{0}\cap B_{0})Y
, by Lemma
2.6
, we have a core-free factorization
B_{1}=X_{1}Y_{1}
with
\mathbf{soc}(B_{1})\cong\mathrm{G}_{2}(q)
, and
S\preccurlyeq Y_{1}
. It follows from

[
21
]
that
q=4
and
S\cong\mathrm{J}_{2}
,
\mathrm{U}_{3}(4)
or
\mathrm{U}_{3}(3)
, which is impossible.

Assume next that
B_{0}
is given as in
[
31
, Table 1]
.
We discuss in several cases according the structure of
B_{0}
.
Note that
|Y|
is divisible by
|G_{0}:A_{0}|={1\over 2}q^{m}(q^{m}-1)
.

Case 1
.
Let
B_{0}=\mathsf{P}_{m}
,
\overline{B_{0}}=B_{0}/\mathbf{O}_{2}(B_{0})
and
\overline{Y}=Y\mathbf{O}_{2}(B_{0})/\mathbf{O}_{2}(B_{0})
.
Then
\overline{B_{0}}\cong\mathrm{GL}_{m}(q)
,
S\preccurlyeq\overline{Y}
and
|\overline{Y}|
is divisible by
q^{m}-1
.
Assume that
(m,q)\neq(6,2)
. Then, by Lemma
7.1
, either part (1) of this lemma occurs, or

(S,m,q)
is one of
(\mathrm{A}_{5},4,2)
,
(\mathrm{A}_{7},4,2)
,
(\mathrm{A}_{11},10,2)
,
(\mathrm{A}_{13},12,2)
,
(\mathrm{A}_{19},18,2)
,
(\mathrm{M}_{11},10,2)
,
(\mathrm{L}_{2}(11),10,2)
,
(\mathrm{L}_{2}(11),5,4)
and
(\mathrm{L}_{3}(3),12,2)
. For the latter case, considering
\pi(S)=\pi(Y)
, only
(\mathrm{A}_{5},4,2)
,
(\mathrm{A}_{7},4,2)
and
(\mathrm{A}_{13},12,2)
are left. Note the triple
(\mathrm{A}_{5},4,2)
is included in part (1) of this lemma.
Now let
(m,q)=(6,2)
. By Lemma
2.7
, there is a factorization
of
\mathrm{SL}_{6}(2)
with a factor having a composition factor
S
. Checking the subgroups of
\mathrm{SL}_{6}(2)
, we have

S\cong\mathrm{A}_{7}
,
\mathrm{SL}_{3}(2)
or
\mathrm{SL}_{5}(2)
, as in part (2) or (3) of this lemma.

Case 2
. Let
B_{0}=\mathrm{Sp}_{m}(q)\wr\mathrm{S}_{2}
.
Note that
A_{0}\cap B_{0}\cong(\mathrm{GO}^{-}_{m}(q)\times\mathrm{GO}^{+}_{m}(q))
, see
[
31
, pp. 50, 3.2.4(b)]
.
We have
A_{0}\cap B_{0}\leq B_{0}^{\prime}
and
B_{0}^{\prime}=(A_{0}\cap B_{0})(Y\cap B_{0}^{\prime})
. Let
N
be a direct factor of

B_{0}^{\prime}
such that
N
contains the direct factor
\mathrm{GO}^{-}_{m}(q)
of
A_{0}\cap B_{0}
. Let
\overline{B_{0}}=B_{0}^{\prime}/N
,
\overline{Y}=(Y\cap B_{0}^{\prime})N/N
and
\overline{H}=(A_{0}\cap B_{0})N/N
.
Then
\overline{B}\cong\mathrm{Sp}_{m}(q)
,
\overline{H}\cong\mathrm{GO}^{+}_{m}(q)
and
\overline{B}=\overline{H}\,\overline{Y}
, and thus

|\overline{Y}|
is divisible by
|\overline{B}:\overline{H}|={1\over 2}q^{m\over 2}(q^{m\over 2}+1)
.

Assume that
\overline{Y}
is insolvable. Then
S\preccurlyeq\overline{Y}
.
Applying Lemma
7.1
to the pair
(\overline{B},\overline{Y})
, by a similar argument as in Case 1, we have that either
S\cong\mathrm{L}_{2}(q^{m\over 2})
with
mf
a power of
2
, or
(S,m,q)
is one of
(\mathrm{A}_{5},4,2)
,
(\mathrm{A}_{7},6,2)
,
(\mathrm{L}_{3}(2),6,2)
,
(\mathrm{A}_{7},6,2)
and
(\mathrm{A}_{13},12,2)
.
Assume that
\overline{Y}
is solvable. It follows from
[
27
, Theorem 1.1]
that

(m,q)=(4,2)
or
(6,2)
, and
N\cong\mathrm{S}_{6}
or
\mathrm{Sp}_{6}(2){.}2
, respectively. Noting that
\overline{Y}=(Y\cap B_{0}^{\prime})N/N\cong(Y\cap B_{0}^{\prime})/(Y\cap N)
, we have

S\preccurlyeq Y\cap N
. Since
\pi(Y\cap N)\subseteq\pi(S)=\pi(Y)
, checking the subgroups of
N
, we have that

(S,m,q)
is one of
(\mathrm{A}_{5},4,2)
,
(\mathrm{A}_{7},6,2)
and
(\mathrm{L}_{3}(2),6,2)
.
Then our lemma holds in this case.

Case 3
. Let
B_{0}\cong\mathrm{GO}^{+}_{2m}(2)
,
m\geq 4
and
q=2
.
Then
A_{0}\cap B_{0}=\mathrm{Sp}_{2m-2}(2)\times 2
is maximal in both
A_{0}
and
B_{0}
, see
[
31
, pp. 52, 3.2.4(e)]
.
Noting that
B_{0}=(A_{0}\cap B_{0})Y_{0}
is a core-free factorization, by Lemma
2.7
, choose a core-free maximal factorization
G_{1}=A_{1}B_{1}
and a core-free factorization
G_{1}=A_{1}Y_{1}
such that

\mathbf{soc}(G_{1})\cong\Omega^{+}_{2m}(2)
,
A_{1}\gtrsim\mathrm{Sp}_{2m-2}(2)
and
S\preccurlyeq Y_{1}\leq B_{1}
.
Then
S
is described as in Lemma
7.6
. Recalling
|G_{\alpha}|
is divisible by
2^{m-1}(2^{m}-1)
, except for
S\cong\mathrm{L}_{3}(2)
and
m=6
, all
the triples in Lemma
7.6
(2) are easily excluded.
Thus
S\cong\mathrm{L}_{2}(2^{m\over 2})
, or
m=6
and
S\cong\mathrm{L}_{3}(2)
or
\mathrm{A}_{7}
, desired as part (1) or (2) of this lemma.

Case 4
. Let
B_{0}\cong\mathrm{Sp}_{2a}(q^{b}){.}b
, where
b
is a prime and
m=ab
. In this case,
A_{0}\cap B_{0}\cong\mathrm{GO}^{-}_{2a}(q^{b}){.}b
, which is maximal in
A_{0}
(if
a\neq 1
) and
B_{0}
.
Noting that
B_{0}=(A_{0}\cap B_{0})Y
, if
a\geq 3
then part (1) of this lemma occurs by an induction on
m
.

Assume that
a=1
. Then
m
is an odd prime and
B_{0}\cong\mathrm{L}_{2}(q^{m}){.}m
. Checking the subgroups of
\mathrm{L}_{2}(q^{m})
with order divisible by
q^{m}-1
, we conclude that
S\cong\mathrm{L}_{2}(q^{m})
. Then
r=q^{m}+1
, which is not a prime, a contradiction. Thus
a=2
,
{m\over 2}
is a prime and
B_{0}\cong\mathrm{Sp}_{4}(q^{m\over 2}){.}{m\over 2}
. By Lemmas
2.7
and
2.6
, since
B_{0}=(A_{0}\cap B_{0})Y
,
choose a core-free maximal factorization
G_{1}=A_{1}B_{1}
and a core-free factorization
G_{1}=A_{1}Y_{1}
such that

\mathbf{soc}(G_{1})\cong\mathrm{Sp}_{4}(q^{m\over 2})
,
A_{1}\gtrsim\Omega^{-}_{4}(q^{m\over 2})
and
S\preccurlyeq Y_{1}\leq B_{1}
.
By
[
31
, Theorem A]
, we conclude that
B_{1}^{\infty}\mathbf{rad}(B_{1})/\mathbf{rad}(B_{1})\cong\mathrm{L}_{2}(q^{m})
,
\mathrm{L}_{2}(q^{m\over 2})
or
\mathrm{L}_{2}(q^{m\over 2})\times\mathrm{L}_{2}(q^{m\over 2})
. It follows that
S\cong\mathrm{L}_{2}(q^{m})
or
\mathrm{L}_{2}(q^{m\over 2})
. This implies that
r=q^{m}+1
or
q^{m\over 2}+1
, and so
r
is not a prime, a contradiction.
∎

8.
The classical case

In this section, we will work under Hypothesis
6.1
. Let
A
and
B
be maximal subgroups of
G
such that one of them contains
T
and the other one contains
G_{\alpha}
.
Then

G=AB
, and both
A
and
B
are insolvable. Without loss of generality, we let
A
and
B
be described as in the third and the fourth columns of Tables 1-4 in
[
31
]
, respectively. For convenience, we define
X
and
Y
as follows:

\{X,Y\}=\{T,G_{\alpha}\},\,X\leq A\mbox{ and }Y\leq B.

Recall that
S=\mathbf{soc}(G_{\alpha}^{\Gamma(\alpha)})
.

8.1.
Unitary case

Let
G=\mathrm{U}_{2m}(q)
, where
m\geq 2
and
q=p^{f}
for some prime
p
. Then
A
and
B
are given
in
[
31
, Tables 1 and 3]
.

Lemma 8.1
.

A
and
B
are described as
in
[
31
, Table 1]
.

Proof.

Suppose that
A
,
B
are given as
in
[
31
, Table 3]
, and we list
A
and
B
as follows:

\tiny\begin{array}[]{l|l|l|l|l}(m,q)&A&|G:A|&B&|G:B|\\
\hline\cr(2,3)&\mathrm{L}_{3}(4)&2\cdot 3^{4}&\mathrm{PSp}_{4}(3)&2\cdot 3^{2}\cdot 7\\
\hline\cr(3,2)&\mathrm{U}_{5}(2)&2^{5}\cdot 3\cdot 7&\mathrm{U}_{4}(3).2&2^{7}\cdot 11\\
&\mathrm{U}_{5}(2)&2^{5}\cdot 3\cdot 7&\mathrm{M}_{22}&2^{8}\cdot 3^{4}\\
\hline\cr(6,2)&\mathrm{Suz}&\Phi_{22}^{*}(2)\Phi_{18}^{*}(2)\mid|G:A|&\mathrm{U}_{11}(2)&2^{11}\cdot 3\cdot 5\cdot 7\cdot 13\end{array}

Inspect the insolvable groups contained in
A
and
B
.
For
(m,q)=(2,3)
, combining Lemma
2.4
, we conclude that

\{T,G_{\alpha}\}=\{A,B\}
; however, neither
\mathrm{L}_{3}(4)
nor
\mathrm{PSp}_{4}(3)
has a subgroup of prime index. Similarly, the pair
(3,2)
is excluded for
(m,q)
.
Then only
(m,q)=(6,2)
is left.
By
[
32
, Table 10.1]
,
\mathrm{U}_{11}(2)
has no proper
subgroup with order divisible by
\Phi_{22}^{*}(2)\Phi_{18}^{*}(2)
.
By the Atlas
[
5
]
,
\mathrm{Suz}
has no maximal subgroup with order divisible by
2^{11}\cdot 3\cdot 5\cdot 7\cdot 13
. It follows that
\{T,G_{\alpha}\}=\{A,B\}
. By Lemma
4.2
, none of
\mathrm{U}_{11}(2)
and
\mathrm{Suz}
is a possible vertex-stabilizer of some symmetric graph of prime degree, a contradiction. This completes the proof.
∎

In the following we let
A=\mathsf{N}_{1}
. Then

B
and
|G:B|
are listed as follows:

\tiny\begin{array}[]{l|l|l|l}B&\mathsf{P}_{m}&\mathrm{PSp}_{2m}(q){.}{(2,q-1)(m,q+1)\over(2m,q+1)}&{}^{\bf\hat{}}\mathrm{SL}_{m}(4).2,q=2\\
\hline\cr|G:B|&\prod_{i=1}^{m}(q^{2i-1}+1)&{1\over(m,q+1)}q^{m(m-1)}\prod_{i=2}^{m}(q^{2i-1}+1)&{1\over 2}q^{m^{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{m}(q^{2i-1}+1)\\
\end{array}

By Lemma
2.4
,
|X|
is divisible by
|G:B|
.
Since
A
is insolvable,
(m,q)\neq(2,2)
.

Lemma 8.2
.

If
(m,q)=(3,2)
then
T\cong\mathrm{U}_{5}(2)
,
\mathrm{SL}_{3}(2)\unlhd G_{\alpha}^{\Gamma(\alpha)}
and
\mathbf{O}_{2}(G_{\alpha})\neq 1
.

Proof.

Let
(m,q)=(3,2)
.
Then
A\cong\mathrm{U}_{5}(2)
,
|G:A|={2^{5}\cdot 3\cdot 7}
, and
B
is listed as follows:

\tiny\begin{array}[]{l|l|l|l}B&2^{9}{:}\mathrm{L}_{3}(4)&\mathrm{PSp}_{6}(2)&\mathrm{L}_{3}(4).2\\
\hline\cr|G:B|&3^{4}\cdot 11&2^{6}\cdot 3^{2}\cdot 11&2^{8}\cdot 3^{4}\cdot 11\end{array}

Searching the subgroups of
\mathrm{U}_{5}(2)
with order divisible by
|G:B|
, we get
X=A\cong\mathrm{U}_{5}(2)
. Then
T=X\leq A
, and so
G_{\alpha}=Y\leq B
. Thus
|G_{\alpha}|
is divisible
|G:A|
.
For
B=\mathrm{PSp}_{6}(2)
, by calculation using GAP, we have
G_{\alpha}\cong 2^{3}{:}\mathrm{SL}_{3}(2)
or
2^{6}{:}\mathrm{L}_{3}(2)
.

Assume that
B\cong\mathrm{L}_{3}(4).2
or
2^{9}{:}\mathrm{L}_{3}(4)
.
Let
\overline{B}=B^{\prime}/\mathbf{rad}(B^{\prime})
and
\overline{Y}=(Y\cap B^{\prime})\mathbf{rad}(B^{\prime})/\mathbf{rad}(B^{\prime})
. Then
\overline{B}\cong\mathrm{L}_{3}(4)
,
S\preccurlyeq\overline{Y}
, and
|\overline{Y}|
has a divisor
21
. Checking the subgroups of
\mathrm{L}_{3}(4)
, we get
S\cong\overline{Y}\cong\mathrm{L}_{3}(2)
. By Lemma
4.2
,

|G_{\alpha}|
is a divisor of
6|\mathbf{O}_{2}(G_{\alpha})||\mathrm{GL}_{3}(2)|
. Then
2^{5}\leq|G_{\alpha}|_{2}\leq 2^{4}|\mathbf{O}_{2}(G_{\alpha})|
, yielding
\mathbf{O}_{2}(G_{\alpha})\neq 1
, and the lemma follows.
∎

Lemma 8.3
.

A\geq T\cong\mathrm{U}_{2m-1}(q)
.

Proof.

By Lemma
8.2
, our lemma holds if
(m,q)=(3,2)
.
Thus assume
(m,q)\neq(3,2)
. Let
\overline{A}=A/\mathbf{rad}(A)
and

\overline{X}=X\mathbf{rad}(A)/\mathbf{rad}(A)
. Then
\overline{A}\cong\mathrm{PGU}_{2m-1}(q)
, and
|\overline{X}|
is divisible by
\Phi_{2(2m-1)}^{*}(q)
. By Lemma
2.3
,
\Phi_{2(2m-1)}^{*}(q)\neq 1
as
(2(2m-1),q)\neq(6,2)
and
2(2m-1)\geq 6
.

Assume that
m\geq 3
. Then
\Phi_{2(2m-3)}^{*}(q)\neq 1
, and it is easily shown that
\overline{X}\cap\mathbf{soc}(\overline{A})
has order divisible by
\Phi_{2(2m-1)}^{*}(q)\Phi_{2(2m-3)}^{*}(q)
. By
[
32
, Theorem 4 and Table 10.1]
, we conclude that
\overline{X}\cap\mathbf{soc}(\overline{A})\cong\mathrm{U}_{2m-1}(q)
.
This implies that
T=X\cong\mathrm{U}_{2m-1}(q)
.

Let
m=2
. Then
A=[{q+1\over(4,q+1)}]{.}\mathrm{PGU}_{3}(q)
, and
|G:A|={q^{3}(q-1)(q^{2}+1)}
. Suppose that
\overline{X}\cap\mathbf{soc}(\overline{A})\not\cong\mathrm{U}_{3}(q)
. Let
M
be a maximal subgroup of
\mathbf{soc}(\overline{A})
with
\overline{X}\cap\mathbf{soc}(\overline{A})\leq M
.
Noting that
\Phi_{6}^{*}(q)\geq 7
,
|M|
is divisible by
\Phi_{6}^{*}(q)
and
M
is insolvable, by
[
3
, Tables 8.5 and 8.6]
,
q=p
and either
M\cong\mathrm{L}_{3}(2)
with
p\neq 5
or
M\cong\mathrm{A}_{7}
with
p=5
.
In both cases,
\Phi_{6}^{*}(q)=7
, and so
q\in\{3,5\}
by
[
20
, Theorem 3.9]
.
Assume that
q=3
. Then
A\cong\mathrm{U}_{3}(3)
,
X\cong\mathrm{L}_{3}(2)
, and
|G:X|=2^{4}\cdot 3^{5}\cdot 5
. Moreover,
B\cong 3^{4}{:}\mathrm{A}_{6}
or
\mathrm{PSp}_{4}(3)
. Since
G=XY
, by Lemma
2.4
,
|Y|
is divisible by
2^{4}\cdot 3^{5}\cdot 5
. It follows that
B\cong 3^{4}{:}\mathrm{A}_{6}
and
Y\cong 3^{4}{:}\mathrm{A}_{5}
. This forces
G_{\alpha}=Y\cong 3^{4}{:}\mathrm{A}_{5}
, which is impossible by Lemma
4.2
.
Assume that
q=5
. Then
A\cong 3{.}\mathrm{U}_{3}(5){.}3
,
X\lesssim 3{.}\mathrm{A}_{7}{.}3
, and
|G:X|
is divisible by
2^{4}\cdot 5^{5}\cdot 13
.
In this case,
|Y|
is divisible by
2^{4}\cdot 5^{5}\cdot 13
.
It follows that
B\cong 5^{4}{:}\mathrm{L}_{2}(25){:}[4]
,
and then
Y\cong 5^{4}{:}\mathrm{L}_{2}(25){:}[c]
, where
c
is a divisor of
4
. This implies that
G_{\alpha}=Y
, and so
r=25+1
, which contradicts that
r
is a prime. Therefore,

\overline{X}\cap\mathbf{soc}(\overline{A})\cong\mathrm{U}_{3}(q)
. Then
T=X\cong\mathrm{U}_{3}(q)
, and the lemma holds.
∎

Theorem 8.1
.

Assume that
Hypothesis
6.1
holds, and let
G=\mathrm{U}_{2m}(q)
, where
m\geq 2
,
(m,q)\neq(2,2)
and
q=p^{f}
for some prime
p
.
Then
T\cong\mathrm{U}_{2m-1}(q)
,
p=2
,
\mathbf{O}_{2}(G_{\alpha})\neq 1
and
one of the following holds.

(1)

(m,q)=(3,2)
and
\mathrm{SL}_{3}(2)\unlhd G_{\alpha}^{\Gamma(\alpha)}
.

(2)

mf
is a power of
2
and
\mathrm{SL}_{2}(q^{m})\unlhd G_{\alpha}^{\Gamma(\alpha)}
.

Proof.

By Lemmas
8.1
and
8.3
,
\mathrm{U}_{2m-1}\cong T\leq A=\mathsf{N}_{1}
, and so
G_{\alpha}=Y\leq B
.
Thus
S=\mathbf{soc}(G_{\alpha}^{\Gamma(\alpha)})
is described as in Lemma
7.3
.
If
(m,q)=(3,2)
then part (1) holds by Lemma
8.2
.
Next let
m>3
if
q=2
. Note that
|G:T|={(2m-1,q+1)\over(2m,q+1)}q^{2m-1}(q^{2m}-1)
.

Since
|G_{\alpha}|
is divisible by
|G:T|
, we have
|G_{\alpha}|_{p}\geq|G:T|_{p}
.
Suppose that
S
is given in Table
7.10
. Then
(S,2m,q)
is one of
(\mathrm{A}_{5},4,3)
,
(\mathrm{A}_{7},4,7)
,
(\mathrm{A}_{7},6,3)
,
(\mathrm{A}_{7},6,5)
,
(\mathrm{A}_{11},10,2)
,
(\mathrm{A}_{13},12,2)
,
(\mathrm{A}_{19},18,2)
,
(\mathrm{M}_{11},10,2)
,
(\mathrm{L}_{3}(2),6,3)
,
(\mathrm{L}_{3}(2),6,5)
,
(\mathrm{L}_{2}(11),10,2)
and
(\mathrm{L}_{3}(3),12,2)
.
For these triples, by Lemma
4.2
, calculation shows that
|G_{\alpha}|_{p}<|G:T|_{p}
, a contradiction. Therefore,

S\cong\mathrm{L}_{2}(q^{m})
,
r=q^{m}+1
,
p=2
and
mf
is a power of
2
. Suppose that
\mathbf{O}_{p}(G_{\alpha})=1
. Then, by Lemma
4.2
,
|G_{\alpha}|_{2}
is a divisor of

mfq^{m}
. Then
q^{2m-1}=|G:T|_{p}\leq|G_{\alpha}|_{2}|\leq mfq^{m}
, yielding
(m,q)=(2,2)
, a contradiction. Thus
\mathbf{O}_{p}(G_{\alpha})\neq 1
, and then our result follows.
∎

8.2.
Orthogonal case I

Let
G=\Omega_{2m+1}(q)
, where
m\geq 3
and
q=p^{f}
for some odd prime
p
.
Then
A
and
B
are given
in
[
31
, Tables 1-3]
. Clearly,
\Phi_{2m}^{*}(q)\neq 1\neq\Phi_{2m-2}^{*}(q)
.

Lemma 8.4
.

A
and
B
are described as
in
[
31
, Table 1]
.

Proof.

Suppose that the lemma is false. We deduce the contradiction in two cases.

Case 1
. Let
A
and
B
be as
in
[
31
, Table 2]
. We have the following three cases.

1.1
.
p=3
,
m=12
,
A\cong\mathrm{F}_{4}(q)
and
B\cong\Omega_{24}^{-}(q){.}2
. In this case,
|G:A|={q^{120}(q^{4}-1)(q^{10}-1)\over 2}\prod_{i=7}^{m}(q^{2i}-1)
, and
|G:B|={1\over 2}q^{12}(q^{12}-1)
. Clearly,
\Phi_{2m-4}^{*}(q)\neq 1
.

Recall that
X\leq A
and
Y\leq B
. Then
|Y|
is divisible by
\Phi_{2m}^{*}(q)\Phi_{2m-2}^{*}(q)\Phi_{2m-4}^{*}(q)
. By
[
32
, Theorem 4 and Table 10.1]
, since
q
is odd,
\mathrm{P\Omega}^{-}_{2m}(q)
has no proper subgroup
with order divisible by
\Phi_{2m}^{*}(q)\Phi_{2m-2}^{*}(q)\Phi_{2m-4}^{*}(q)
.
It follows that
Y\succcurlyeq\mathrm{P\Omega}^{-}_{2m}(q)
.
Then
Y\neq G_{\alpha}
by Lemma
4.2
. Therefore,
T=Y
and
G_{\alpha}=X\leq A
. Noting that
G=BG_{\alpha}
, we have
A=(A\cap B)G_{\alpha}
. By Lemma
4.2
,
G_{\alpha}\neq A
. Then we have a factorization of
\mathrm{F}_{4}(q)
,
which is impossible by
[
21
, Theorem 1]
as
q
is odd here.

1.2
.
p=3
,
m=6
,
A\cong\mathrm{PSp}_{6}(q).a
and
B\cong\Omega_{12}^{-}(q){.}2
, where
a\leq 2
. In this case,
|G:A|={1\over 2a}q^{27}\prod_{i=4}^{m}(q^{2i}-1)
, and
|G:B|={1\over 2}q^{6}(q^{6}-1)
.

Similarly as in
1.1
, we have
T\leq B
,
G_{\alpha}\leq A
, and so
|G_{\alpha}|
is divisible
q^{6}(q^{6}-1)
.
By Lemma
4.2
,
S\not\cong\mathrm{PSp}_{6}(q)
. It is easily shown that

S\preccurlyeq G_{\alpha}\cap A^{\prime}
, and
|G_{\alpha}\cap\mathrm{A}^{\prime}|
is divisible by
q^{6}\Phi_{6}^{*}(q)
, where
A^{\prime}
is the derived subgroup of
A
.
Clearly,
\Phi_{6}^{*}(q)\neq 1
. Noting that
q
is odd, it follows from Lemma
7.1
that

q\in\{3,5\}
and
S\cong\mathrm{A}_{7}
or
\mathrm{L}_{3}(2)
. By Lemma
4.2
,

|G_{\alpha}|
is indivisible by
3^{3}
; however, here,
|G_{\alpha}|
is divisible by
q^{6}
, a contradiction.

1.3
.
m=3
,
A\cong\mathrm{G}_{2}(q)
and
B
is one of
[q^{5}]{:}({q-1\over 2}\times\Omega_{5}(q)).2
,
\Omega_{6}^{+}(q){.}2
,
\Omega_{6}^{-}(q){.}2
,
({q-1\over 2}\times\Omega_{5}(q)).2^{2}
(with
q>3
) and
({q+1\over 2}\times\Omega_{5}(q)).2^{2}
.

Suppose that
X=G_{\alpha}
. Then
G=G_{\alpha}B
,
A=G_{\alpha}(A\cap B)
, and
|G_{\alpha}|
is divisible
|G:B|
; in particular,
|G_{\alpha}|
is divisible by
{1\over 2}q^{3}(q^{3}\pm 1)
or
{q^{6}-1\over 2(q\pm 1)}
.
Assume that
|G_{\alpha}|
is divisible by
{1\over 2}q^{3}(q^{3}\pm 1)
.
By
[
21
, Theorem 1]
, combining Lemma
4.2
, we have
\mathrm{SL}_{3}(q)\leq G_{\alpha}\leq\mathrm{SL}_{3}(q).2
and
p=3
. It follows that
r={q^{3}-1\over q-1}
, and so
f
is a power of
3
by Lemma
2.1
. In particular,
(4,q+1)=4
. Moreover, in this case,
|T|
is divisible by
|G:G_{\alpha}|={1\over 2a}q^{6}(q^{4}-1)(q^{3}+1)
, where
a\leq 2
. Then

T\leq B=\Omega_{6}^{-}(q){.}2
.
By
[
32
, Tables 10.3 and 10.4]
,
\mathrm{P\Omega}_{6}^{-}(q)
has no proper subgroup of order divisible by
q^{6}(q^{4}-1)(q^{3}+1)\over q-1
. It follows that

T=\mathrm{P\Omega}_{6}^{-}(q)
. This forces that
B
has trivial center, and then
(4,q+1)\leq 2
, a contradiction. Assume that
|G_{\alpha}|
is divisible by
{q^{6}-1\over 2(q\pm 1)}
. Then
|G_{\alpha}|
has a divisor
\Phi_{6}^{*}(q)\Phi_{3}^{*}(q)
. By
[
32
, Table 10.5]
, we have
G_{\alpha}\lesssim\mathrm{L}_{2}(13)
, forcing
G_{\alpha}\cong\mathrm{L}_{2}(13)
and
r=13+1
, which contradicts that
r
is a prime.

Now
G_{\alpha}=Y\leq B
and
T=X\leq A
. Then
G=AG_{\alpha}
, yielding

|G_{\alpha}:(A\cap G_{\alpha})|=|G:A|={1\over 2}q^{3}(q^{4}-1)
.
Assume that
B\succcurlyeq\mathrm{PSp}_{4}(q)
or

\mathrm{L}_{4}(q)
. Let
\overline{B}=B^{\prime}\mathbf{rad}(B)/\mathbf{rad}(B)
. Then
\overline{B}\cong\mathrm{PSp}_{4}(q)
or

\mathrm{L}_{4}(q)
. Note that
\Phi^{*}_{4}(q)\neq 1
.
Considering the preimage of
(G_{\alpha}\cap B^{\prime})\mathbf{rad}(B)/\mathbf{rad}(B)
in
\mathrm{GL}_{4}(q)
, by Lemma
7.1
,
we conclude that
q=3
or
7
, and
S\cong\mathrm{A}_{5}
or
\mathrm{A}_{7}
, respectively. By Lemma
4.2
,

|G_{\alpha}|
is indivisible by
3^{3}
, which is impossible as
|G_{\alpha}|
has a divisor
q^{3}
.

The left case is that
G_{\alpha}\leq B\cong\Omega_{6}^{-}(q){.}2
and
T\leq A\cong\mathrm{G}_{2}(q)
.
In particular,

|G_{\alpha}:(A\cap G_{\alpha})|=|G:A|={1\over 2}q^{3}(q^{4}-1)
and
|T:(B\cap T)|=|G:B|={1\over 2}q^{3}(q^{3}-1)
. It is easily shown that
S\preccurlyeq G_{\alpha}\cap B^{\prime}
. Since
|B:B^{\prime}|=2
, either
G_{\alpha}\leq B^{\prime}
or

B=G_{\alpha}B^{\prime}
, yielding
|G:(G_{\alpha}\cap B^{\prime})|=1
or
2
, respectively.
It follows that
|G_{\alpha}\cap B^{\prime}|
is divisible by
q^{3}\Phi_{4}^{*}(q)
.
Take a maximal subgroup
M
of
B^{\prime}
with

G_{\alpha}\cap B^{\prime}\leq M
. Then
|M|
is divisible by
q^{3}\Phi_{4}^{*}(q)
.
Checking the maximal subgroups of
\Omega_{6}^{-}(q)
, refer to
[
3
, Tables 8.33 and 8.34]
, we conclude that

M\cong\Omega_{5}(q){.}2\cong\mathrm{PSp}_{4}(q){.}2
.
This gives a similar contradiction as in the above paragraph.

Case 2
. Let
A
and
B
be as
in
[
31
, Table 3]
. Then
G=\Omega_{7}(3)
and, up to isomorphism,
A
and
B
are listed as follow:

\tiny\begin{array}[]{l|l|l|l|l|l|l|l}\hline\cr A&\mathrm{G}_{2}(3)&\mathrm{G}_{2}(3)&\mathrm{S}_{9}&\mathrm{S}_{9}&\mathrm{Sp}_{6}(2)&\mathrm{Sp}_{6}(2)&2^{6}{.}\mathrm{A}_{7}\\
\hline\cr|G:A|&2^{3}\cdot 3^{3}\cdot 5&2^{3}\cdot 3^{3}\cdot 5&2^{2}\cdot 3^{5}\cdot 13&2^{2}\cdot 3^{5}\cdot 13&3^{5}\cdot 13&3^{5}\cdot 13&3^{7}\cdot 13\\
\hline\cr\hline\cr B&\mathrm{Sp}_{6}(2)&\mathrm{S}_{9}&\mathrm{L}_{4}(3).2&[3^{6}]{:}\mathrm{L}_{3}(3)&\mathrm{L}_{4}(3).2&[3^{6}]{:}\mathrm{L}_{3}(3)&[3^{6}]{:}\mathrm{L}_{3}(3)\\
\hline\cr|G:B|&3^{5}\cdot 13&2^{2}\cdot 3^{5}\cdot 13&2\cdot 3^{3}\cdot 7&2^{5}\cdot 5\cdot 7&2\cdot 3^{3}\cdot 7&2^{5}\cdot 5\cdot 7&2^{5}\cdot 5\cdot 7\\
\hline\cr\end{array}

Note that
|X|
and
|Y|
are divisible by

|G:B|
and
|G:A|
, respectively. Inspecting simple subgroups contained in
A
and
B
, since neither
X
nor
Y
is an alternating group, we conclude that either
T=A\cong\mathrm{G}_{2}(3)
or
\mathrm{U}_{4}(2)\cong T\leq B\cong\mathrm{Sp}_{6}(2)
. Suppose that
T\cong\mathrm{G}_{2}(3)
. Then
G_{\alpha}\leq B\cong\mathrm{Sp}_{6}(2)
or
\mathrm{S}_{9}
and, since
G=TG_{\alpha}
, we know that
|G_{\alpha}|
is divisible by
2^{3}\cdot 3^{3}\cdot 5
.
By Lemma
4.2
,
G_{\alpha}\neq B
.
Let
M
be a maximal subgroup of
B
with
G_{\alpha}\leq M
.
Then, by the Atlas
[
5
]
,
M\cong\mathrm{U}_{4}(2).2
,
\mathrm{S}_{3}\times\mathrm{S}_{6}
or
(3\times\mathrm{A}_{6}){:}2
. However, such an
M
does not contain an insolvable subgroup satisfying Lemma
4.2
and having order divisible by
2^{3}\cdot 3^{3}\cdot 5
.
Thus
\mathrm{U}_{4}(2)\cong T\leq B\cong\mathrm{Sp}_{6}(2)
, and
G_{\alpha}\leq A\cong\mathrm{G}_{2}(3)
. Then
|G:T|=2^{3}\cdot 3^{5}\cdot 7\cdot 13
. Noting that

|G_{\alpha}|
is divisible by
|G:T|
, it follows that
G_{\alpha}=A\cong\mathrm{G}_{2}(3)
, which is impossible by Lemma
4.2
.
∎

Theorem 8.2
.

Assume that
Hypothesis
6.1
holds and
G=\Omega_{2m+1}(q)
, where
m\geq 3
and
q=p^{f}
for some odd prime
p
. Then
mf
is a power of some odd prime
d
,
T\cong\mathrm{P\Omega}^{-}_{2m}(q)
,
\mathbf{soc}(G_{\alpha}^{\Gamma(\alpha)})\cong\mathrm{L}_{d}(q^{m\over d})
,
p\equiv 1\,({\mathrm{mod}~}4)
and
r={q^{m}-1\over q^{m\over d}-1}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
8.4
, we let
A
and
B
be as
in
[
31
, Table 1]
. Then

A\cong\Omega^{-}_{2m}(q).2
,
B\cong[q^{m(m+1)\over 2}]{:}{1\over 2}\mathrm{GL}_{m}(q)
and
|G:B|=\prod_{i=1}^{m}(q^{i}+1)
.
In particular,
|G:B|
and hence
|X|
is divisible by
\Phi_{2m}^{*}(q)\Phi_{2m-2}^{*}(q)\Phi_{2m-4}^{*}(q)
.

Case 1
. Assume that
\Phi_{2m-4}^{*}(q)=1
. Then
m=3
and
q=p=2^{s}-1
for some prime
s\geq 2
. In this case, we have

A\cong\Omega_{6}^{-}(q){.}2\cong 2{.}\mathrm{U}_{4}(q){.}2
.

Suppose that
T=X\leq A
. Then
T\lesssim\Omega_{6}^{-}(q)
.
Noting that
(4,q+1)=4
, we have
T\not\cong\mathrm{U}_{4}(q)
.
Recall that
|X|
is divisible
by
(q+1)(q^{2}+1)(q^{3}+1)
, and hence
|T|
is divisible by
\Phi_{2m}^{*}(q)\Phi_{2m-2}^{*}(q)
. If
q\geq 5
and
|T|
is divisible by
p
then
T\cong\mathrm{U}_{4}(q)
by
[
32
, Table 10.3]
, which is impossible. If
q=3
then
|T|
is divisible by
35
, and so

T\cong\mathrm{U}_{4}(3)
by
[
32
, Table 10.4]
, a contradiction. Thus
we have
q\geq 5
and
|T|
is indivisible by
p
.
Note that
|T|
is divisible by
\Phi_{6f}^{*}(p)\Phi_{4}^{*}(q)
.
Considering the preimage of
T
in

\mathrm{GL}_{6}(q)
, by
[
2
, Theorem 3.1]
, we conclude that
T\leq\mathrm{GU}_{3}(q)
. Then
T\leq\mathrm{U}_{3}(q)
, and thus

|T|
is indivisible by
(q+1)(q^{2}+1)(q^{3}+1)
,
a contradiction.

Now
T\leq B\cong[q^{6}]{:}{1\over 2}\mathrm{GL}_{3}(q)
and
G_{\alpha}\leq A\cong\Omega_{6}^{-}(q){.}2
. Then
G=G_{\alpha}B
, and
|G_{\alpha}:(B\cap G_{\alpha})|=|G:B|=\prod_{i=1}^{3}(q^{i}+1)
. Thus
|G_{\alpha}|
is divisible

\Phi^{*}_{6}(q)
. By Lemma
7.1
, we conclude that

q=3
and
S\cong\mathrm{A}_{7}
or
\mathrm{L}_{3}(2)
. By Lemma
4.2
, calculation shows that
|G_{\alpha}|_{3}\leq 3^{3}
.
Then
3^{9}=|G|_{3}\leq|T|_{3}|G_{\alpha}|_{3}\leq 3^{6}
,
a contradiction.

Case 2
. Assume that
\Phi_{2m-4}^{*}(q)\neq 1
.
By
[
32
, Theorem 4 and Table 10.1]
, since
q
is odd,
\mathrm{P\Omega}^{-}_{2m}(q)
has no proper subgroup
with order divisible by
\Phi_{2m}^{*}(q)\Phi_{2m-2}^{*}(q)\Phi_{2m-4}^{*}(q)
. It follows that
X\succcurlyeq\mathrm{P\Omega}^{-}_{2m}(q)
.
Therefore,
T=X\cong\mathrm{P\Omega}^{-}_{2m}(q)
, and

G_{\alpha}=Y\leq B
. This implies that
(4,q^{m}+1)=2
and
|G:T|=q^{m}(q^{m}-1)
. In particular,

q^{m}(q^{m}-1)
is a divisor of
|G_{\alpha}|
, and so
|G_{\alpha}|
is divisible by
\Phi_{mf}^{*}(p)
.
Clearly,
\Phi_{mf}^{*}(p)\neq 1
.

Let
\overline{G_{\alpha}}=G_{\alpha}\mathbf{O}_{p}(B)/\mathbf{O}_{p}(B)
. Then
\overline{G_{\alpha}}\leq{1\over 2}\mathrm{GL}_{m}(q)
, and
|\overline{G_{\alpha}}|
is divisible by
q^{m}-1
. Note that
S\preccurlyeq\overline{G_{\alpha}}
.
By Lemma
7.1
,
either
S\cong\mathrm{L}_{d}(q^{m\over d})
for some divisor
d
of
m
, or

(S,m,q)
is one of
(\mathrm{A}_{5},4,3)
,
(\mathrm{A}_{7},6,3)
,
(\mathrm{A}_{7},6,5)
,

(\mathrm{A}_{7},4,7)
,
(\mathrm{A}_{7},3,25)
,
(\mathrm{L}_{3}(2),6,3)
,
(\mathrm{L}_{3}(2),6,5)
,
(\mathrm{L}_{3}(2),3,9)
and
(\mathrm{L}_{3}(2),3,25)
.
Since
|G_{\alpha}|
is divisible by
q^{m}
, the latter case is easily excluded by calculating
|G_{\alpha}|_{p}
. Assume that the former case occurs. If
(d,q^{m\over d},r)=(2,11,11)
then we have
|G_{\alpha}|_{11}=11
by Lemma
4.2
, which contradicts that
|G_{\alpha}|
is divisible by
q^{m}
. Thus we have
r={q^{m}-1\over q^{m\over d}-1}
, and so

d
is an odd prime
and
mf
is a power of
d
, see Lemma
2.1
. Recalling
(4,q^{m}+1)=2
, we have
p\equiv 1\,({\mathrm{mod}~}4)
.
∎

8.3.
Orthogonal case II

Let
G=\mathrm{P\Omega}^{-}_{2m}(q)
, where
m\geq 4
and
q=p^{f}
for some prime
p
. Then, by
[
31
, Theorem A]
,
m
is odd and
one of the following holds:

(1)

m=5
,
q=2
,
A\cong\mathrm{A}_{12}
and
B\cong 2^{8}{:}\Omega^{-}_{8}(2)
;

(2)

A\cong[q^{2m-2}]{:}[{q-1\over a}]{.}\mathrm{P\Omega}^{-}_{2m-2}(q){.}a
and
B\cong{q+1\over(4,q+1)}{.}\mathrm{PGU}_{m}(q)
, where
a=2
if
q\equiv 1\,({\mathrm{mod}~}4)
, or
a=1
otherwise;

(3)

A\cong\Omega_{2m-1}(q).{(4,q-1)\over 2}
(odd
q
) or
\mathrm{Sp}_{2m-2}(q)
(even
q
), and
B\cong{q+1\over(4,q+1)}{.}\mathrm{PGU}_{m}(q)
.

For case (1), we have
|G:B|=3^{2}\cdot 5\cdot 11
and
|G:A|=2^{11}\cdot 3\cdot 17
. Check the insolvable subgroups of
A
and
B
with order divisible by
|G:B|
and
|G:A|
, respectively. Recalling that
X
is not an alternating group, it follows that
X\cong\mathrm{M}_{11}
or
\mathrm{M}_{12}
, and
Y\gtrsim\Omega^{-}_{8}(2)
. Then the only possibility is that
T=Y\cong\Omega^{-}_{8}(2)
and
G_{\alpha}\cong\mathrm{M}_{11}
. Thus
|T|_{2}|G_{\alpha}|_{2}=2^{16}<|G|_{2}
, a contradiction.

Suppose that (2) or (3) occurs. It is easy to check that

|G:A|
is divisible by
q^{m-1}{q^{m}+1\over(4,q^{m}+1)}
, and then

|Y|
is divisible by
q^{m-1}\Phi^{*}_{m(2f)}(p)
. Since
m\geq 4
and
m
is odd,
\Phi^{*}_{m(2f)}(p)\neq 1
. By
[
2
, Theorem 3.1]
, one of the following holds:

Y\succcurlyeq\mathrm{U}_{m_{0}}(q^{m\over m_{0}})
for some divisor
m_{0}\geq 3
of
m
; or
m=5
,
q^{2}=4
and
Y\succcurlyeq\mathrm{L}_{2}(11)
; or
m=9
,
q^{2}=4
and
Y\succcurlyeq\mathrm{J}_{3}
or
\mathrm{L}_{2}(19)
. For the last two cases, we have
|Y|_{p}<q^{m-1}
, a contradiction. Thus
we have
T=Y\cong\mathrm{U}_{m_{0}}(q^{m\over m_{0}})
, and then
G_{\alpha}=X\leq A
.

Theorem 8.3
.

Assume that
Hypothesis
6.1
holds,
G=\mathrm{P\Omega}_{2m}^{-}(q)
for
m\geq 4
.
Then
m=5
,
q>2
,
T\cong\mathrm{U}_{5}(q)
,
\mathrm{SL}_{2}(q^{4})\unlhd G_{\alpha}^{\Gamma(\alpha)}
,
|\mathbf{O}_{p}(G_{\alpha})|=q^{8}
and
f
is a power of
2
.

Proof.

By the argument ahead of this lemma,
m
is odd,

\mathrm{U}_{m_{0}}(q^{m\over m_{0}})\cong T\leq B
,
G_{\alpha}\leq A
, where
m_{0}
is a divisor of
m
.
Note that
|G:B|=q^{m(m-1)\over 2}\prod_{i=1}^{m-1}(q^{i}-(-1)^{i-1})
. Then
|G_{\alpha}|
is divisible by
q^{m(m-1)\over 2}\Phi_{2(m-1)}^{*}(q)\Phi_{2(m-3)}^{*}(q)
.

Assume that
A\cong[q^{2m-2}]{:}[{q-1\over a}]{.}\mathrm{P\Omega}^{-}_{2m-2}(q){.}a
.
Let
\widehat{A}
and
\widehat{G_{\alpha}}
be the preimages of
A^{\prime}\mathbf{rad}(A)/\mathbf{rad}(A)
and
(A^{\prime}\cap G_{\alpha})\mathbf{rad}(A)/\mathbf{rad}(A)

in
\mathrm{GO}^{-}_{2(m-1)}(q)
, respectively. Then
S\preccurlyeq\widehat{G_{\alpha}}
, and
|\widehat{G_{\alpha}}|
is divisible by
\Phi_{2(m-1)f}^{*}(p)\Phi_{2(m-3)}^{*}(q)
. By Lemma
7.1
, either
S=\mathrm{L}_{d}(q^{2(m-1)\over d})
or

(S,2(m-1),q)
is one of
(\mathrm{A}_{13},12,2)
and
(\mathrm{L}_{3}(3),12,2)
.
For the latter case, by Lemma
4.2
, we conclude that
|G_{\alpha}|_{2}\leq 2^{11}
, which contradicts that
|G_{\alpha}|
is divisible by
q^{m(m-1)\over 2}
. Suppose that
S=\mathrm{L}_{d}(q^{2(m-1)\over d})
. Then
r={(q^{2(m-1)}-1)\over(q^{2(m-1)\over d}-1)}
. By Lemma
2.1
,
d=2
,
q
is even and
m-1
is a power of
2
. By the choice of
\widehat{G_{\alpha}}
, we conclude that

|G_{\alpha}|_{p}\leq|\mathbf{rad}(A)|_{p}|\widehat{G_{\alpha}}|_{p}
. Then, by Lemma
7.1
, we have
|G_{\alpha}|_{p}\leq|\mathbf{rad}(A)|_{p}2(m-1)|S|_{p}=2(m-1)q^{3(m-1)}
.
Thus
q^{m(m-1)\over 2}\leq|G_{\alpha}|_{p}\leq 2(m-1)q^{3(m-1)}
.
This implies that
m\leq 7
, yielding
m=5
as
m\geq 5
and
m-1
is a power of
2
. Then
T\cong\mathrm{U}_{5}(q)
.
Noting that
8q^{12}\geq|G_{\alpha}|_{p}\geq q^{10}
, by Lemma
4.2
, we have
|\mathbf{O}_{p}(G_{\alpha})|=q^{8}
.
To finish the proof, we next show that
A\not\cong\Omega_{2m-1}(q).{(4,q-1)\over 2}
or
\mathrm{Sp}_{2m-2}(q)
.

Suppose that
A\cong\Omega_{2m-1}(q).{(4,q-1)\over 2}
for odd
q
.
Recall that
|G_{\alpha}|
is divisible by
q^{m(m-1)\over 2}\Phi_{2(m-1)}^{*}(q)\Phi_{2(m-3)}^{*}(q)
, and
2(m-1)\geq 8
.
By Lemma
7.2
, the only possibility is that

S\cong\mathrm{L}_{d}(q^{2(m-1)\over d})
,
d
is a prime and
2(m-1)f
is a power of
d
. This implies that
d=2
, and so
r=q^{m-1}+1
. Then
q
is even by Lemma
2.1
, a contradiction.

Suppose that
A\cong\mathrm{Sp}_{2m-2}(q)
for even
q
. Clearly,
G_{\alpha}\neq A
. By Lemma
7.1
, we conclude that
either
S\cong\mathrm{L}_{2}(q^{m-1})
or

(S,2(m-1),q)
is one of
(\mathrm{A}_{13},12,2)
and
(\mathrm{L}_{3}(3),12,2)
.
For the latter case,
|G_{\alpha}|_{2}\leq 2^{11}
by Lemma
4.2
, and so
2^{21}=q^{m(m-1)\over 2}\leq|G_{\alpha}|_{2}\leq 2^{11}
, a contradiction. Then
S\cong\mathrm{L}_{2}(q^{m-1})
and, by Lemma
7.1
,
|G_{\alpha}|_{p}\leq 2(m-1)|S|_{p}
.
Thus
q^{m(m-1)\over 2}\leq 2(m-1)q^{m-1}\leq q^{2(m-1)}
, yielding
m\leq 4
, contradiction.
∎

8.4.
Orthogonal case III

Let
G=\mathrm{P\Omega}^{+}_{8}(q)
,
q=p^{f}
for some prime
p
.
Then
A
and
B
are as in
[
31
, Table 4]
.
Recall that
\{X,Y\}=\{T,G_{\alpha}\}
with
X\leq A
and
Y\leq B
.

Lemma 8.5
.

Either
q>3
, or one of the following holds.

(1)

q=2
,
A\cong\mathrm{Sp}_{6}(2)\cong B
,
T\cong\mathrm{Sp}_{6}(2)
and
G_{\alpha}\cong\mathrm{S}_{5}
.

(2)

q=3
,
T=A\cong\Omega^{+}_{8}(2)
,
G_{\alpha}\cong[3^{6}]{:}\mathrm{SL}_{3}(3)
and
r=13
.

Proof.

Let
q=2
or
3
. Then, up to isomorphism,
A
and
B
are listed as follows:

\tiny\begin{array}[]{l|l|l||l|l|l}X&A&|G:A|&|G:B|&B&Y\\
\hline\cr&\mathrm{A}_{9}&2^{6}\cdot 3\cdot 5&2^{3}\cdot 3\cdot 5&\mathrm{Sp}_{6}(2)&\\

&&&2^{3}\cdot 3\cdot 5&\mathrm{Sp}_{6}(2)&\\
&&&3^{3}\cdot 5&2^{6}{:}\mathrm{A}_{8}&\\
&&&2^{5}\cdot 5\cdot 7&(3{\times}\mathrm{U}_{4}(2)){:}2&\\
\hline\cr&(3{\times}\mathrm{U}_{4}(2)){:}2&2^{5}\cdot 5\cdot 7&3^{3}\cdot 5&2^{6}{:}\mathrm{A}_{8}&\\

Case 1
.
Let
q=2
. With the help of GAP, we search the subgroups of
A
with order divisible by
|G:B|
.
Up to isomorphism, either
A\cong\mathrm{A}_{9}
and
G_{\alpha}=X\cong\mathrm{S}_{5},\,2^{2}{\times}\mathrm{A}_{5},\,\mathrm{A}_{4}{\times}\mathrm{A}_{5},\,(2^{2}{\times}\mathrm{A}_{5}){.}2,\,(\mathrm{A}_{4}{\times}\mathrm{A}_{5}){.}2
or
\mathrm{S}_{7}
, or
X
and
Y
are listed as follows:

\tiny\begin{array}[]{l|l|l|l}A&X&B&Y\\
\hline\cr\mathrm{Sp}_{6}(2)&\mathrm{Sp}_{6}(2)&(\mathrm{A}_{5}\times\mathrm{A}_{5}){:}2^{4}&\mathrm{S}_{5},2^{2}{\times}\mathrm{A}_{5},(2^{2}{\times}\mathrm{A}_{5}){.}2,\mathrm{A}_{4}{\times}\mathrm{A}_{5},(\mathrm{A}_{4}{\times}\mathrm{A}_{5}){.}\\
&\mathrm{S}_{5},2^{4}{:}\mathrm{A}_{5},2^{4}{:}\mathrm{S}_{5},\mathrm{S}_{7}&\mathrm{Sp}_{6}(2)&\Omega_{5}(3),\mathrm{Sp}_{6}(2)\\
&\Omega_{5}(3),\mathrm{Sp}_{6}(2)&\mathrm{Sp}_{6}(2)&\mathrm{S}_{5},2^{4}{:}\mathrm{A}_{5},2^{4}{:}\mathrm{S}_{5},\mathrm{S}_{7}\\
&\Omega_{5}(3),\mathrm{Sp}_{6}(2)&2^{6}{:}\mathrm{A}_{8}&\mathrm{S}_{5},2^{2}{\times}\mathrm{A}_{5},(2^{2}{\times}\mathrm{A}_{5}){.}2,\mathrm{A}_{4}{\times}\mathrm{A}_{5},(\mathrm{A}_{4}{\times}\mathrm{A}_{5}){.}2\\
&&&2^{4}{:}\mathrm{A}_{5},2^{4}{:}\mathrm{S}_{5},\mathrm{A}_{4}\times 2^{4}{:}\mathrm{A}_{5},(\mathrm{A}_{4}\times 2^{4}{:}\mathrm{A}_{5}){.}2\\
&\mathrm{Sp}_{6}(2)&(3{\times}\mathrm{U}_{4}(2)){:}2&\mathrm{S}_{5},2^{4}{:}\mathrm{A}_{5},2^{4}{:}\mathrm{S}_{5}\\
\hline\cr(3{\times}\mathrm{U}_{4}(2)){:}2&\Omega_{5}(3)&2^{6}{:}\mathrm{A}_{8}&\mbox{No}\end{array}

Then the vertex-stabilizer
G_{\alpha}
is easily determined.
By calculation, we have a desired graph only if
A\cong\mathrm{Sp}_{6}(2)\cong B
,
T\cong\mathrm{Sp}_{6}(2)
and
G_{\alpha}\cong\mathrm{S}_{5}
.

Case 2
. Let
q=3
and
B\not\cong\Omega_{7}(3)
. Set

\overline{Y}=Y\mathbf{rad}(B)\mathbf{rad}(B)
and
\overline{B}=B/\mathbf{rad}(B)
.

Suppose that
B\cong 3^{6}{:}\mathrm{L}_{4}(3)
. Then
A\cong\Omega_{8}^{+}(2)
,

\Omega_{7}(3)
or
2^{.}\mathrm{U}_{4}(3){.}2^{2}
, and

|\overline{Y}|
has a divisor
5\cdot 13
. By
[
32
, Table 10.3]
, we have
\overline{Y}=\overline{B}\cong\mathrm{L}_{4}(3)
. Thus
Y\succcurlyeq\mathrm{L}_{4}(3)
. Noting that
3^{4}-1\over 3-1
is not a prime, we have
S\not\cong\mathrm{L}_{4}(3)
, and then
B>T\cong\mathrm{L}_{4}(3)
. Thus
|G:T|=|G:B||B:T|=2^{5}\cdot 5\cdot 7\cdot 3^{6}
, and so
G_{\alpha}
is a subgroup of
A
with order divisible by
2^{5}\cdot 3^{6}\cdot 5\cdot 7
. By
[
32
, Table 10.3]
, we conclude that
A\not\cong\Omega_{8}^{+}(2)
. Then
A\cong\Omega_{7}(3)
or
2^{.}\mathrm{U}_{4}(3){.}2^{2}
.
If
A\cong\Omega_{7}(3)
then, checking the maximal subgroup of
A
with order divisible by
|G:T|
, we conclude that

G_{\alpha}
is contained in a maximal subgroup
2^{.}\mathrm{U}_{4}(3){.}2^{2}
of
A
. Thus we may let
G_{\alpha}\leq A_{0}
, where
2^{.}\mathrm{U}_{4}(3){.}2^{2}\leq\mathrm{A}_{0}\leq A
. Considering
G_{\alpha}\mathbf{rad}(A_{0})/\mathbf{rad}(A_{0})
and
A_{0}/\mathbf{rad}(A_{0})
, by
[
32
, Table 10.4]
, we know that

G_{\alpha}\succcurlyeq\mathrm{L}_{3}(4)
or
\mathrm{A}_{7}
.
Then
S\cong\mathrm{L}_{3}(4)
or
\mathrm{A}_{7}
. Noting
4^{3}-1\over 4-1
is not a prime,
S\cong\mathrm{A}_{7}
. By Lemma
4.2
,
|G_{\alpha}|_{3}=3^{3}
, a contradiction.

Suppose that
B\cong 2^{.}\mathrm{U}_{4}(3){.}2^{2}
. Then
A\cong\Omega_{7}(3)
.
It is easily shown that
\overline{Y}
and
\overline{Y}\cap\overline{B}^{\infty}
have same insolvable composition factors, and
|\overline{Y}\cap\overline{B}^{\infty}|
is divisible by
3^{3}\cdot 5
. With the help of GAP, searching the subgroups of
\mathrm{U}_{4}(3)
with order divisible by
3^{3}\cdot 5
, we get
\overline{Y}\cap\overline{B}^{\infty}\cong 3^{4}{:}\mathrm{A}_{5}
,
\Omega_{5}(3)
,
3^{4}{:}\mathrm{A}_{6}
or
\mathrm{U}_{4}(3)
. Noting that

\overline{Y}=Y\mathbf{rad}(B)\mathbf{rad}(B)\cong Y/(Y\cap\mathbf{rad}(B))
, it follows that
Y
has a normal subgroup
R{.}(3^{4}{:}\mathrm{A}_{5})
,
R{.}\Omega_{5}(3)
,
R{.}(3^{4}{:}\mathrm{A}_{6})
or
R{.}\mathrm{U}_{4}(3)
, where
R=Y\cap\mathbf{rad}(B)
. Considering the limitations on
Y
, we conclude that
T=Y\cong\Omega_{5}(3)
, and
G_{\alpha}=X\leq A
. Noting that
|G:T|=2^{6}\cdot 3^{8}\cdot\cdot 5\cdot 7\cdot 13
, this leads to a similar contradiction as in the above paragraph.

Suppose that
B\cong(\mathrm{A}_{4}\times\mathrm{U}_{4}(2)){.}2
. Then
A\cong\Omega_{7}(3)
, and
|\overline{Y}\cap\overline{B}^{\infty}|
is divisible by
3^{2}\cdot 5
.
Searching the subgroups of
\mathrm{U}_{4}(2)
with order divisible by
3^{2}\cdot 5
, we conclude that
\overline{Y}\cap\overline{B}^{\infty}\cong\mathrm{A}_{6},\,\mathrm{S}_{6}
or
\Omega_{5}(3)
. It follows that
T=Y\cong\Omega_{5}(3)
, and
G_{\alpha}=X\leq A
, which leads to a contradiction.

Case 3
. Let
B\cong\Omega_{7}(3)
and
A\cong\Omega^{+}_{8}(2)
or
\Omega_{7}(3)
.
Suppose that
G_{\alpha}\lesssim\Omega^{+}_{8}(2)
.
Then, by Lemma
7.4
,
S\cong\mathrm{A}_{5}
or
\mathrm{A}_{7}
, which leads to a contradiction by calculating
|G_{\alpha}|_{3}
.
Thus
T\leq A\cong\Omega^{+}_{8}(2)
, and
G_{\alpha}\leq B
. In particular,
|G:A|=3^{7}\cdot 13
.
Then
G_{\alpha}
is a subgroup of
B
of order divisible by
3^{7}\cdot 13
. Checking the orders of maximal subgroups of
\Omega_{7}(3)
, we conclude that
\mathrm{L}_{3}(3)\lesssim G_{\alpha}\lesssim[3^{6}]{:}\mathrm{L}_{3}(3)\lesssim B
. Then
\mathbf{soc}(G_{\alpha}^{\Gamma(\alpha)}\cong\mathrm{L}_{3}(3)
,
r=13
and
|\mathbf{O}_{3}(G_{\alpha})|=3^{6}
by Lemma
4.2
. Thus
G_{\alpha}\cong[3^{6}]{:}\mathrm{SL}_{3}(3)
, and
|G:G_{\alpha}|=|G:B||B:G_{\alpha}|=2^{8}\cdot 3^{3}\cdot 5^{2}\cdot 7
. Since

|T|
is divisible by

, by
[
32
, Table 10.4]
, we have
