, and part (2) of this lemma follows.
∎

For
q>3
, we list
A
and
B
as follows:

\tiny\begin{array}[]{l|l|l|l}A&|G:A|&B&|G:B|\\
\hline\cr\Omega_{7}(q)&{1\over e}q^{3}(q^{4}-1)&\Omega_{7}(q)&{1\over e}q^{3}(q^{4}-1)\\
\Omega_{7}(q)&{1\over e}q^{3}(q^{4}-1)&\mathsf{P}_{1},\mathsf{P}_{3}\mbox{ or }\mathsf{P}_{4}&(q^{3}+1)(q^{2}+1)(q+1)\\
\Omega_{7}(q)&{1\over e}q^{3}(q^{4}-1)&{}^{\bf\hat{}}({q+1\over e}{\times}\Omega^{-}_{6}(q)){.}2^{e}&{1\over 2}q^{6}(q^{3}-1)(q^{2}+1)(q-1)\\
\Omega_{7}(q)&{1\over e}q^{3}(q^{4}-1)&{}^{\bf\hat{}}({q-1\over e}{\times}\Omega^{+}_{6}(q)){.}2^{e}&{1\over 2}q^{6}(q^{3}+1)(q^{2}+1)(q+1)\\
\Omega_{7}(q),q\mbox{ ood}&{1\over 2}q^{3}(q^{4}-1)&(\mathrm{PSp}_{2}(q)\otimes\mathrm{PSp}_{4}(q)){.}2&{1\over 2}q^{7}(q^{6}-1)(q^{2}+1)\\
\Omega_{7}(q)&{1\over e}q^{3}(q^{4}-1)&\Omega^{-}_{8}(\sqrt{q})&{1\over e}q^{6}(q^{4}-1)(q^{3}+1)(q+1)\\
\hline\cr{}^{\bf\hat{}}({q+1\over e}{\times}\Omega^{-}_{6}(q)){.}2^{e}&{1\over 2}q^{6}(q^{3}-1)(q^{2}+1)(q-1)&\mathsf{P}_{1},\mathsf{P}_{3}\mbox{ or }\mathsf{P}_{4}&(q^{3}+1)(q^{2}+1)(q+1)\end{array}

where
e=(2,q-1)
. Note that
\Omega_{m}(q)=\mathrm{Sp}_{m-1}(q)
for odd
m
and even
q
.

Lemma 8.6
.

Assume that
q>3
. Then
A\cong\Omega_{7}(q)
.

Proof.

Suppose that
A
is of type
{}^{\bf\hat{}}({q+1\over e}{\times}\Omega^{-}_{6}(q)){.}2^{e}
. Let
\overline{A}=A^{\infty}\mathbf{rad}(A)/\mathbf{rad}(A)
and
\overline{X}=(X\cap A^{\infty})\mathbf{rad}(A)/\mathbf{rad}(A)
. Then
\overline{A}\cong\mathrm{U}_{4}(q)
and
|\overline{X}|
is divisible by
\Phi^{*}_{6}(q)\Phi^{*}_{4}(q)
.
By
[
32
, Table 10.3]
, either
\overline{X}=\overline{A}\cong\mathrm{U}_{4}(q)
, or
p\geq 5
and
|\overline{X}|
is indivisible by
p
.
Assume that
\overline{X}\neq\overline{A}
.
Checking the orders of maximal subgroups of
\mathrm{U}_{4}(q)
(refer to
[
3
, Tables 8.10 and 8.11]
), we conclude that

\overline{X}\cong\mathrm{A}_{7}
. Considering the order of
\mathrm{A}_{7}
, it follows from Lemma
2.2
that
\Phi^{*}_{6}(q)=7
and
\Phi^{*}_{4}(q)=5
. Then
q=3
by
[
20
, Theorem 3.9]
, a contradiction. Thus
\overline{X}=\overline{A}\cong\mathrm{U}_{4}(q)
, and then
T=X\cong\mathrm{U}_{4}(q)
, yielding
(2,q-1)=1
and
|G:T|=q^{6}(q^{4}-1)(q^{3}-1)
.
Moreover,
G_{\alpha}=Y\leq B
.

Let
\overline{B}=B^{\infty}\mathbf{rad}(B)/\mathbf{rad}(B)
and
\overline{Y}=(Y\cap B^{\infty})\mathbf{rad}(B)/\mathbf{rad}(B)
. Then
\overline{B}\cong\mathrm{L}_{4}(q)
, and
|\overline{Y}|
has a divisor
\Phi^{*}_{3}(q)\Phi^{*}_{4}(q)
.
By
[
32
, Table 10.3]
, either
\overline{Y}=\overline{B}\cong\mathrm{L}_{4}(q)
, or
p\geq 5
and
|\overline{Y}|
is indivisible by
p
. For the former, we conclude that
G_{\alpha}=Y\succcurlyeq\mathrm{L}_{4}(q)
, a contradiction. Then
|\overline{Y}|
is indivisible by
p
.
Checking the orders of maximal subgroups of
\mathrm{L}_{4}(q)
(refer to
[
3
, Tables 8.10 and 8.11]
), we get

\overline{Y}\lesssim 2^{.}\mathrm{L}_{2}(7)
or
2^{.}\mathrm{A}_{7}
, and
q=p
.
Recalling
(2,q-1)=1
, we have
q=p=2
, a contradiction.
∎

Lemma 8.7
.

Assume that
q>3
. Then
T\lesssim\Omega_{7}(q)
.

Proof.

Suppose that
T\not\lesssim\Omega_{7}(q)
. Then, by Lemma
8.6
,
T\leq B\not\cong\Omega_{7}(q)
, yielding
G_{\alpha}\leq A\cong\Omega_{7}(q)
. Then
|G_{\alpha}|
is divisible by
|G:B|
.
It follows that either
T\lesssim\mathrm{U}_{4}(q)
or
|G_{\alpha}|
is divisible by
\Phi^{*}_{6}(q)\Phi^{*}_{4}(q)
.
Assume that the latter case occurs. Noting
6
is not a prime power, it follows from
Lemmas
7.1
and
7.2
that
q=5
and
S\cong\mathrm{A}_{7}
or
\mathrm{L}_{3}(2)
. Then
\Phi^{*}_{4}(q)=13\not\in\pi(S)
, which contradicts that
\pi(G_{\alpha})=\pi(S)
, see Lemma
4.2
. Thus we have
B\cong{}^{\bf\hat{}}({q+1\over e}{\times}\Omega^{-}_{6}(q)){.}2^{e}
, and

|G:B|={1\over 2}q^{6}(q^{3}-1)(q^{2}+1)(q-1)
, where
e=(2,q-1)
.
Note that
|G_{\alpha}|
is divisible by
|G:B|
.
Take a maximal subgroup
A_{0}
of
A
with
G_{\alpha}\leq A_{0}
.
Then both
|A_{0}|
and
|G_{\alpha}|
are divisible by
{1\over 2}q^{6}\Phi^{*}_{3}(q)\Phi^{*}_{4}(q)
.
Checking the orders of maximal subgroups of
\Omega_{7}(q)
and
\mathrm{Sp}_{6}(q)
, refer to

[
3
, Tables 8.28, 8.29, 8.39 and 8.40]
, we conclude that
A_{0}\cong\Omega^{+}_{6}(q){.}2\cong\mathrm{L}_{4}(q){.}2
.
It follows from
[
32
, Table 10.3]
that
\mathrm{L}_{4}(q)\preccurlyeq G_{\alpha}
, which is impossible by Lemma
4.2
.
∎

Theorem 8.4
.

Assume that
Hypothesis
6.1
holds and
G=\mathrm{P\Omega}^{+}_{8}(q)
, where
q=p^{f}
for some prime
p
. Then one of the following holds.

(1)

p=2
,
f
is a power of
2
,
T\cong\mathrm{Sp}_{6}(q)
,
\mathrm{SL}_{2}(q^{2})\unlhd G_{\alpha}^{\Gamma(\alpha)}
and
r=q^{2}+1
; in this case,
G_{\alpha}\cong\mathrm{S}_{5}
if
f=1
, and
\mathbf{O}_{2}(G_{\alpha})\neq 1
if
f>2
.

(2)

q=3
,
T\cong\Omega^{+}_{8}(2)
,
G_{\alpha}\cong[3^{6}]{:}\mathrm{SL}_{3}(3)
and
r=13
.

Proof.

If
q\leq 3
then our result holds by Lemma
8.5
.
Assume that
q>3
. By Lemmas
8.6
and
8.7
,
T\leq A\cong\Omega_{7}(q)
and
G_{\alpha}\leq B
.
Then
S
is given as in Lemma
7.4
.
Note that
|G_{\alpha}|
is divisible by
|G:A|={1\over(2,q-1)}q^{3}(q^{4}-1)
.
If
(S,q)=(\mathrm{A}_{7},7)
then
|G_{\alpha}|_{7}=7<|G:A|_{7}
, a contradiction.
Thus
S\cong\mathrm{L}_{2}(q^{2})
,
r=q^{2}+1
,
p=2
and
f
is a power of
2
. If
\mathbf{O}_{p}(G_{\alpha})=1
then, by Lemma
4.2
,
|G_{\alpha}|_{2}
is a divisor of

2fq^{2}
, and so
q^{3}\leq 2fq^{2}
, yielding
f\leq 2
. Since
q
is even,
A\cong\mathrm{Sp}_{6}(q)
.

By Lemma
4.2
,
|G_{\alpha}|
is a divisor of
4f^{2}q^{2}(q^{4}-1)(q^{2}-1)|\mathbf{O}_{2}(G_{\alpha})|
. It follows that
|G:G_{\alpha}|
is divisible by
(q^{4}-1)(q^{6}-1)
. Since
q>3
, by
[
32
, Table 10.1]
, we have
T=A\cong\mathrm{Sp}_{6}(q)
, and the result follows.
∎

8.5.
Orthogonal case IV

Let
G=\mathrm{P\Omega}^{+}_{2m}(q)
,
m\geq 5
and
q=p^{f}
for some prime
p
.

Lemma 8.8
.

A
and
B
are not described as in
[
31
, Table 3]
.

Proof.

Suppose that
G\cong\Omega^{+}_{24}(2)
,
A\cong\mathrm{Co}_{1}
and
B\cong\mathrm{Sp}_{22}(2)
.
Then
|G:B|=2^{11}(2^{12}-1)
and
|G:A|
is divisible by
\Phi^{*}_{22}(2)\Phi^{*}_{20}(2)\Phi^{*}_{18}(2)=683\cdot 41\cdot 19
.
Since
|Y|
is divisible by
|G:A|
, by
[
32
, Table 10.1]
, either
Y=B
or
Y
has a normal subgroup
\Omega^{-}_{22}(2)
, yielding
T=Y\cong\mathrm{Sp}_{22}(2)
or
\Omega^{-}_{22}(2)
. Then
G_{\alpha}\leq A
.
Checking the insolvable subgroups of
\mathrm{Co}_{1}
with order divisible by
2^{11}(2^{12}-1)
, we conclude that
G_{\alpha}\succcurlyeq\mathrm{G}_{2}(4)
or
\mathrm{Suz}
, which contradicts Lemma
4.2
.
∎

Lemma 8.9
.

A
and
B
are not described as in
[
31
, Table 2]
.

Proof.

Suppose that
G=\mathrm{P\Omega}^{+}_{16}(q)
,

A\cong\Omega_{9}(q){.}a
and

B=\mathsf{N}_{1}
, where
a\leq 2
.
Then
|G:B|
and
|G:A|
are divisible by
q^{7}{q^{8}-1\over(4,q^{8}-1)}

and
q^{40}{\prod_{i\in\{8,10,12,14\}}(q^{i}-1)\over a(4,q^{8}-1)}
.

Assume that
q
is even. Then
B\cong\mathrm{Sp}_{14}(q)
, and
|Y|
is
divisible by
\Phi^{*}_{14}(q)\Phi^{*}_{12}(q)\Phi^{*}_{7}(q)
. By
[
32
, Table 10.1]
, we have
Y=B\cong\mathrm{Sp}_{14}(q)
, and so
G_{\alpha}=X\leq A
.
Assume that
q
is odd. Then
B\cong\Omega_{15}(q){.}b
.
By Lemmas
2.6
and
2.7
, either
Y\succcurlyeq\Omega_{15}(q)
, or
there exists a core-free maximal factorization
B_{0}=HK
such that
K
contains a normal subgroup
N
of
Y
of index at most
2
.
The former cases yields that
T=Y=\Omega_{15}(q)
.
Suppose that the latter case occurs. It is easily shown that
|N|
is divisible by
\Phi^{*}_{14}(q)\Phi^{*}_{12}(q)\Phi^{*}_{10}(q)
.
Then, by
[
31
, Theorem A]
,
we conclude that
K
is the stabilizer of some
1
-dimensional nonsingular subspace, and so
N\leq K\cong\Omega_{14}^{-}(q){.}2
. By
[
32
, Table 10.1]
, we have
N\succcurlyeq\mathrm{P\Omega}_{14}^{-}(q)
. Then
Y\succcurlyeq\mathrm{P\Omega}_{14}^{-}(q)
, yielding
T=Y\cong\mathrm{P\Omega}_{14}^{-}(q)
.

All in all,
T\leq B=\mathsf{N}_{1}
. By Lemma
7.6
,

S\cong\mathrm{L}_{2}(q^{4})
,
p=2
and
f
is power of
2
.
Since
G_{\alpha}\leq A\cong\mathrm{Sp}_{8}(q){.}a
, we have
S\preccurlyeq G_{\alpha}\cap A^{\prime}
and
|G_{\alpha}:(G_{\alpha}\cap A^{\prime})|\leq a
. By Lemma
7.1
,
|G_{\alpha}\cap A^{\prime}|_{2}\leq 4|S|_{2}=4q^{4}
, and so
|G_{\alpha}|_{2}\leq 2^{3}q^{4}
. Recalling that
|G_{\alpha}|
is divisible by
|G:B|
, we have
q^{7}\leq|G_{\alpha}|_{2}\leq 2^{3}q^{4}
, yielding
q=2
and
|G_{\alpha}|_{2}=2^{7}
. Then
\mathbf{O}_{2}(G_{\alpha})=1
, otherwise,
|G_{\alpha}|_{2}\geq q^{8}
by Lemma
4.2
, a contradiction. Thus
G_{\alpha}\lesssim\mathrm{P\Gamma L}_{2}(2^{4})
, and then
|G_{\alpha}|_{2}\leq 2^{6}
, a contradiction.
∎

We next deal with the case that
A
and
B
are described as in
[
31
, Table 1]
. In particular, either
m
is even, or
|G:B|=\prod_{i=1}^{m-1}(q^{i}+1)
.

Lemma 8.10
.

Assume that
A
and
B
are described as in
[
31
, Table 1]
, and
(m,q)=(6,2)
. Then one of the following holds.

(1)

T=\Omega^{-}_{10}(2)
or
\mathrm{Sp}_{10}(2)
, and
\mathrm{SL}_{5}(2)\leq G_{\alpha}\leq[2^{20}]{:}\mathrm{SL}_{5}(2)
;

(2)

T=\mathrm{Sp}_{10}(2)
,
G_{\alpha}^{\Gamma(\alpha)}\cong\mathrm{SL}_{3}(2)
and
\mathbf{O}_{2}(G_{\alpha})\neq 1
.

Proof.

Let
(m,q)=(6,2)
. Then
A
and
B
are listed as follows:

\tiny\begin{array}[]{l|l|l|l|l|l}X^{\infty}&&A&|G:A|&B&|G:B|\\
\hline\cr\Omega^{-}_{10}(2)&\mathrm{SO}^{-}_{10}(2)&\mathrm{Sp}_{10}(2)&2^{5}\cdot 3^{2}\cdot 7&2^{15}{:}\mathrm{GL}_{6}(2)&3^{4}\cdot 5\cdot 11\cdot 17\\
\Omega^{+}_{10}(2)&\mathrm{SO}^{+}_{10}(2)&&&3{.}\mathrm{U}_{6}(2){.}[6]&2^{14}\cdot 5\cdot 7\cdot 17\cdot 31\\
&\mbox{No}&&&\mathrm{Sp}_{2}(2)\times\mathrm{Sp}_{6}(2)&2^{20}\cdot 3^{3}\cdot 5\cdot 7\cdot 11\cdot 17\cdot 31\\
\Omega^{-}_{10}(2)&\mathrm{SO}^{-}_{10}(2)&&&\mathrm{SL}_{6}(2){.}2&2^{14}\cdot 3^{4}\cdot 5\cdot 11\cdot 17\\
&\mbox{No}&&&\Omega^{+}_{6}(4){.}2^{2}&2^{16}\cdot 3^{4}\cdot 7\cdot 11\cdot 31\\
\hline\cr&\mbox{No}&(3\times\Omega^{-}_{10}(2)){.}2&2^{9}\cdot 3\cdot 7\cdot 31&\mathrm{SL}_{6}(2){.}2&2^{14}\cdot 3^{4}\cdot 5\cdot 11\cdot 17\\
&\mbox{No}&&&2^{15}{:}\mathrm{GL}_{6}(2)&3^{4}\cdot 5\cdot 11\cdot 17\\
\hline\cr&\mbox{No}&2^{10}{:}\Omega^{+}_{10}(2)&3^{3}\cdot 7\cdot 11&3{.}\mathrm{U}_{6}(2){.}[6]&2^{14}\cdot 5\cdot 7\cdot 17\cdot 31\end{array}

If
A^{\infty}\leq\mathbf{rad}(A)X
then we have
X\cong\mathrm{PSp}_{10}(2)
,
\Omega^{-}_{10}(2)
or
\Omega^{+}_{10}(2)
.
Suppose that
A^{\infty}\not\leq\mathbf{rad}(A)X
. Then
\mathbf{rad}(A)X\cap A^{\infty}
is properly contained in
A^{\infty}
, and
X^{\infty}<A^{\infty}
.
It is easily shown that every odd prime divisor is also a divisor of
|\mathbf{rad}(A)X\cap A^{\infty}|
. Check the maximal subgroups of
A^{\infty}
with order divisible by these primes, refer to
[
3
, Tables 8.64-8.69]
. Combining

[
32
, Table 10.4]
, we list
X^{\infty}
and
those maximal subgroups of
A
which possibly contain
X^{\infty}
in the first and second columns of the above table, respectively. Then
X\cong\Omega^{-}_{10}(2)
or
\Omega^{+}_{10}(2)
. Therefore,
X\cong\mathrm{PSp}_{10}(2)
or
\Omega^{\pm}_{10}(2)
, and so
T=X<A
and
G_{\alpha}\leq B
.

Assume that
T\cong\Omega^{-}_{10}(2)
. Then
|G:T|=2^{10}\cdot 3^{2}\cdot 7\cdot 31
, which is a divisor of
|G_{\alpha}|
and
|B|
. It follows that
B\cong 2^{15}{:}\mathrm{GL}_{6}(2)
or
\mathrm{SL}_{6}(2){.}2
. Since
2^{6}-1
is not a prime, by Lemma
4.2
,
\mathrm{SL}_{6}(2)\not\preccurlyeq G_{\alpha}
. Thus
G_{\alpha}\mathbf{rad}(B)/\mathbf{rad}(B)
is a proper subgroup of
B/\mathbf{rad}(B)
. It is easy to see that
|G_{\alpha}\mathbf{rad}(B)/\mathbf{rad}(B)|
is divisible by
3^{2}\cdot 7\cdot 31
. By
[
32
, Table 10.4]
,
G_{\alpha}\mathbf{rad}(B)/\mathbf{rad}(B)\lesssim\mathrm{SL}_{5}(2)
or

2^{5}{:}\mathrm{SL}_{5}(2)
.
By the Atlas
[
5
]
,
\mathrm{SL}_{5}(2)
has
no proper insolvable subgroup with order divisible by
7\cdot 31
.
It follows that
\mathrm{SL}_{5}(2)\lesssim G_{\alpha}\mathbf{rad}(B)/\mathbf{rad}(B)
.
Thus we have
\mathrm{SL}_{5}(2)\lesssim G_{\alpha}\lesssim[2^{20}]{:}\mathrm{SL}_{5}(2)
.

Assume next that
T\cong\mathrm{Sp}_{10}(2)
or
\Omega^{+}_{10}(2)
. Then
|G:T|=2^{5}\cdot 3^{2}\cdot 7
or
2^{10}\cdot 3^{3}\cdot 7\cdot 11
, respectively. Suppose that
11\in\pi(G_{\alpha})
.
Then
7,\,11\in\pi(G_{\alpha})
. Checking those
B
with order divisible by
77
, we have
B\cong 3{.}\mathrm{U}_{6}(2){.}[6]
.
By
[
32
, Table 10.4]
,
G_{\alpha}\mathbf{rad}(B)/\mathbf{rad}(B)
has a normal subgroup
\mathrm{U}_{6}(2)
or
\mathrm{M}_{22}
, which is impossible by Lemma
4.2
. Thus
11\not\in\pi(G_{\alpha})
.
Recalling that
|G_{\alpha}|
is divisible by
|G:T|
, we have
T\cong\mathrm{Sp}_{10}(2)
. If
31\in\pi(G_{\alpha})
then
\mathrm{SL}_{5}(2)\lesssim G_{\alpha}\lesssim[2^{20}]{:}\mathrm{SL}_{5}(2)
by a similar argument as in above paragraph. Thus we assume that neither
11
nor
31
is a divisor of
|G_{\alpha}|
. Moreover, for
B\cong\Omega^{+}_{6}(4){.}2^{2}\cong\mathrm{SL}_{4}(4){.}2^{2}
,
by
[
32
, Table 10.3]
,
B
has no proper subgroup of order divisible by
7\cdot 17
, and thus
17\not\in\pi(G_{\alpha})
. Then the prime divisors of
|G_{\alpha}|
are
2
,
3
,
7
or and
5
.
By Lemma
4.2
, we conclude that
(G_{\alpha}\mathbf{rad}(B)/\mathbf{rad}(B))^{\infty}
is a proper subgroup of
(B/\mathbf{rad}(B))^{\infty}
. Checking the maximal subgroups of
\mathrm{SL}_{6}(2)
,
\mathrm{U}_{6}(2)
and
\mathrm{SL}_{4}(4)
(refer to
[
5
]
and
[
3
, Tables 8.3 and 8.4]
), we conclude that
(G_{\alpha}\mathbf{rad}(B)/\mathbf{rad}(B))^{\infty}\succcurlyeq\mathrm{SL}_{3}(2)
or
\mathrm{A}_{7}
. For
\mathrm{A}_{7}
, we have
G_{\alpha}\lesssim\mathrm{S}_{7}
by Lemma
4.2
, and thus
|G_{\alpha}|
is indivisible by
2^{5}
, a contradiction.
Thus
G_{\alpha}\succcurlyeq\mathrm{SL}_{3}(2)
.
If
\mathbf{O}_{2}(G_{\alpha})=1
then
|G_{\alpha}|_{2}\leq 2^{3}
by Lemma
4.2
, and so

|G_{\alpha}|_{2}|T|_{2}\leq 2^{28}<|G|_{2}
, a contradiction. Thus part (2) of this lemma follows.
∎

Lemma 8.11
.

Let
(m,q)\neq(6,2)
and
A=\mathsf{N}_{1}
. Then
T\leq A
and
G_{\alpha}\leq B
.

Proof.

By the assumption,

|G:A|={1\over(2,q-1)}q^{m-1}(q^{m}-1)
, and
|G:B|
is listed as follows:

\tiny\begin{array}[]{l|l|l}B&|G:B|&\\
\hline\cr\mathsf{P}_{m}\mbox{ or }\mathsf{P}_{m-1}&\prod_{i=1}^{m-1}(q^{i}+1)&\\
\hline\cr{}^{\bf\hat{}}\mathrm{GU}_{m}(q){.}2&{1\over 2}q^{m(m-1)\over 2}\prod_{i=1}^{m-1}(q^{i}-(-1)^{i-1})&m\mbox{ even}\\
\hline\cr(\mathrm{PSp}_{2}(q){\times}\mathrm{PSp}_{m}(q)).c&{1\over c(q^{2}-1)}q^{(3m+2)(m-2)\over 4}\prod_{i={m\over 2}+1}^{m-1}(q^{2i}-1)&m\mbox{ even},c\leq 2,c=2\mbox{ for odd $q$ and }4\,\,\big|\,\,m\\
\hline\cr{}^{\bf\hat{}}\mathrm{GL}_{m}(q){.}2&{1\over 2}q^{m(m-1)\over 2}\prod_{i=1}^{m-1}(q^{i}+1)&m\mbox{ even}\\
\hline\cr\Omega^{+}_{m}(4).2^{2}&q^{(m+2)(m-2)\over 2}\prod_{i=1}^{m\over 2}(q^{2(2i-1)}-1)&q=2,m\mbox{ even}\end{array}

Clearly,
|G:B|
is divisible by
\Phi^{*}_{2(m-1)}(q)
or
\Phi^{*}_{2(m-2)}(q)
.
Moreover,
A^{\prime}=\mathbf{soc}(A)\cong\Omega_{2m-1}
,
|A:A^{\prime}|\leq 2
, and
|A:A^{\prime}|=2
if and only if both
q
and
{m(q-1)\over 2}
are odd.

Suppose that
T\leq B
. Then
|G:T|=|G:B||B:T|
, and thus

|G:T|
is divisible by
{1\over(2,p)}q^{m(m-1)\over 2}
.
Since
G=TG_{\alpha}
, by Lemma
2.4
,

|G_{\alpha}|_{p}\geq{1\over(2,p)}q^{m(m-1)\over 2}
, and
|G_{\alpha}|
is divisible by
\Phi^{*}_{2(m-1)}(q)
or
\Phi^{*}_{2(m-2)}(q)
.
Noting
G=G_{\alpha}B
and
A=G_{\alpha}(A\cap B)
is core-free, next we deduce the contradiction in two cases.

Case 1
. Let
q
be odd. By Lemma
2.7
, choose a core-free maximal factorization
A_{0}=A_{1}B_{1}
such that
A_{0}=X_{1}B_{1}
with

A_{0}=A
or
A^{\prime}
,

G_{\alpha}^{\infty}\leq X_{1}=A_{1}\cap G_{\alpha}
and
|G_{\alpha}:X_{1}|
is divisor of
|A:A_{0}|
.
Noting that
2m-1\geq 9
, by
[
31
, Theorem A]
,

A_{1}
and
B_{1}
are given in
[
31
, Tables 1 and 2]
.
On the other hand, it is easy to see
|X_{1}|
is divisible by
\Phi^{*}_{2(m-1)}(q)
or
\Phi^{*}_{2(m-2)}(q)
. Checking the orders of
A_{1}
and
B_{1}
, we conclude that
A_{1}\cong\Omega^{-}_{2(m-1)}(q){.}[2|A_{0}:A^{\prime}|]
.
Note that
\Omega^{-}_{2(m-1)}(q)\not\cong S\preccurlyeq X_{1}
.
By
[
32
, Table 10.1]
,
|X_{1}|
and so
|G_{\alpha}|
is indivisible by

\Phi^{*}_{2(m-1)}(q)\Phi^{*}_{2(m-2)}(q)\Phi^{*}_{2(m-3)}(q)
.
Then
|G:B|
is indivisible by
(q^{m-1}+1)(q^{m-2}+1)(q^{m-3}+1)
. It follows that

B\cong{}^{\bf\hat{}}\mathrm{GU}_{m}(q){.}2
for some even
m
, and then
|G_{\alpha}|
divisible by
q^{m(m-1)\over 2}\Phi^{*}_{2(m-2)f}(p)
.
Since
m
is even, we have
A=A^{\prime}=A_{0}
, and so
G_{\alpha}=X_{1}\leq A_{1}\cong\Omega^{-}_{2(m-1)}(q){.}2
.

It is easily shown that
S\preccurlyeq G_{\alpha}\cap A_{1}^{\prime}
, and

|G_{\alpha}\cap A^{\prime}_{1}|
is divisible by
q^{m(m-1)\over 2}\Phi^{*}_{2(m-2)f}(p)
.
Combining Lemma
4.2
, it follows from
[
2
, Theorem 3.1]
that
G_{\alpha}\cap A^{\prime}_{1}\lesssim\mathrm{GO}^{+}_{2}(q)\times\mathrm{GO}^{-}_{2m-4}(q)
,
[q^{2m-4}].(q-1\times\mathrm{GO}^{-}_{2m-4}(q))
,

\mathrm{GO}_{2m-3}(q)
,
\mathrm{GO}^{-}_{2{m-1\over d}}(q^{d}){.}[d]
,
\mathrm{GU}_{m-1}(q){.}2
or
\mathrm{GO}_{m-1}(q^{2}){.}2
, where
d>1
is a divisor of
m-1
. The last three groups are excluded as their orders are indivisible by
q^{m(m-1)\over 2}
.
If
G_{\alpha}\cap A^{\prime}_{1}\lesssim\mathrm{GO}_{2m-3}(q)
then, by Lemma
7.2
,
we have
(S,2m-3,q)=(\mathrm{A}_{7},7,3)
or
(\mathrm{A}_{7},7,5)
, which yields that
|G_{\alpha}|
is indivisible by
q^{m(m-1)\over 2}
, a contradiction. Therefore,
G_{\alpha}\cap A^{\prime}_{1}\lesssim\mathrm{GO}^{+}_{2}(q)\times\mathrm{GO}^{-}_{2m-4}(q)
or
[q^{2m-4}].(q-1\times\mathrm{GO}^{-}_{2m-4}(q))
.
It follows from Lemma
7.1
that
S\cong\mathrm{L}_{2}(q^{m-2})
, and so
r=q^{m-2}+1
. By Lemma
2.1
,
q
is even, a contradiction.

Case 2
. Let
q
be even. Then
A\cong\mathrm{Sp}_{2m-2}(q)
.
Since
m\geq 5
, we have
\Omega^{-}_{2(m-1)}(q)\not\preccurlyeq G_{\alpha}
. Suppose that
(m,q)=(5,2)
. Then

|G:A|=2^{4}\cdot 31
, and
T\leq B\cong 2^{10}{:}\mathrm{GL}_{5}(2)
.
Recalling
G=TA
, by Lemma
2.4
,
|T|
is divisible
31
, yielding
T=\mathrm{GL}_{5}(2)
. Thus
|G:T|=2^{10}\cdot 3^{3}\cdot 5\cdot 17
, which is a divisor of
|G_{\alpha}|
. Checking the maximal subgroups of
\mathrm{Sp}_{8}(2)
, we conclude that
G_{\alpha}\lesssim\mathrm{SO}^{-}_{8}(2)
. Checking the maximal subgroups of

\mathrm{SO}^{-}_{8}(2)
, we get
\Omega^{-}_{8}(2)\unlhd G_{\alpha}
, which is impossible by Lemma
4.2
. Then
m\geq 7
if
q=2
.

Assume that
|G_{\alpha}|
is divisible by
\Phi^{*}_{2m-2}(q)
.
Recall that
|G_{\alpha}|_{2}\geq{1\over 2}q^{m(m-1)\over 2}
.
Then
|G_{\alpha}|
is divisible by
{1\over 2}q^{m(m-1)\over 2}\Phi^{*}_{2m-2}(q)
. Applying Lemma
7.1
to
A
and
G_{\alpha}
, either
S\cong\mathrm{L}_{2}(q^{m-1})
and
|G_{\alpha}|_{2}\leq(m-1)|S|_{2}
with
(m-1)f
a power of
2
, or
(S,2m-2,q)
is one of

(\mathrm{A}_{13},12,2)
,
(\mathrm{A}_{19},18,2)

and
(\mathrm{L}_{3}(3),12,2)
.
The former case implies that
{1\over 2}q^{m(m-1)\over 2}\leq(m-1)q^{m-1}
, which is impossible as
m\geq 5
. For the latter case, we have
|G_{\alpha}|_{2}<2^{m-2}
by Lemma
4.2
, again a contradiction.

Assume that
|G_{\alpha}|
is indivisible by
\Phi^{*}_{2m-2}(q)
. Then

B=^{\bf\hat{}}\mathrm{GU}_{m}(q){.}2
,
m
is even and
|G_{\alpha}|
is divisible by
{1\over 2}q^{m(m-1)\over 2}\Phi^{*}_{2m-4}(q)
. Applying
[
2
, Theorem 3.1]
to
A
and
G_{\alpha}
, we have
G_{\alpha}\leq A_{1}\cong[q^{2m-3}]{:}((q-1){\times}\mathrm{Sp}_{2m-4}(q))
or
\mathrm{Sp}_{2}(q)\times\mathrm{Sp}_{2m-4}(q)
.
If
S\lesssim\mathrm{Sp}_{2}(q)
then, by Lemma
4.2
,
|G_{\alpha}|_{2}
is a divisor of
q^{4}f^{2}
, and so
{1\over 2}q^{m(m-1)\over 2}\leq(m-1)q^{m-1}\leq|G_{\alpha}|_{2}\leq q^{5}
,
yielding
m\leq 4
, a contradiction.
Let
N\unlhd A_{1}
with
A_{1}/N\cong\mathrm{Sp}_{2m-4}(q))
.
Then
S\preccurlyeq G_{\alpha}N/N
, and
|G_{\alpha}N/N|
is divisible by
\Phi^{*}_{2m-4}(q)
.
By Lemma
7.1
, either
S\cong\mathrm{L}_{2}(q^{m-2})
and
|G_{\alpha}N/N|_{2}\leq(m-2)|S|_{2}
with
(m-2)f
a power of
2
, or
(S,2m-4,q)
is one of

(\mathrm{A}_{13},12,2)
and
(\mathrm{L}_{3}(3),12,2)
.
For the latter case,
m=8
and
|G_{\alpha}|_{2}\geq 2^{27}
; however, by Lemma
4.2
,
|G_{\alpha}|_{2}\leq 2^{10}
, a contradiction. The former case implies that
{1\over 2}q^{m(m-1)\over 2}\leq|G_{\alpha}|_{2}\leq|G_{\alpha}N/N|_{2}|N|_{2}\leq(m-2)q^{3m-5}
, yielding
(m,q)=(6,2)
, again a contradiction.
∎

Lemma 8.12
.

Let
(m,q)\neq(6,2)
and
A=\mathsf{N}_{1}
.
Then one of the following holds.

(1)

T\cong\mathrm{Sp}_{2m-2}(q)
,
\mathrm{SL}_{2}(q^{m\over 2})\unlhd G_{\alpha}^{\Gamma(\alpha)}
,
\mathbf{O}_{2}(G_{\alpha})\neq 1
,
m\geq 8
,
p=2
and
mf
is a power of
2
.

(2)

T\cong\mathrm{Sp}_{2m-2}(q)
or
\Omega^{-}_{2m-2}(q)
,
\mathrm{L}_{d}(q^{m\over d})\unlhd G_{\alpha}^{\Gamma(\alpha)}
,
p=2
,
d
is an odd prime and
mf
is a power of
d
.

(3)

T\cong\Omega_{2m-1}(q)
,
\mathrm{L}_{d}(q^{m\over d})\unlhd G_{\alpha}^{\Gamma(\alpha)}
,
p
and
d
are odd, and
mf
is a power of
d
.

Proof.

By Lemma
8.11
,
T\leq A
and
G_{\alpha}\leq B
, and then Lemma
7.6
works.
Note that
|G:A|={1\over(2,q-1)}q^{m-1}(q^{m}-1)
. In particular,

|G_{\alpha}|_{p}\geq q^{m-1}
. For those triples in Lemma
7.6
(2), by Lemma
4.2
, we have
|G_{\alpha}|_{p}\leq q^{m-2}
, and thus they are excluded.
Thus
S\cong\mathrm{L}_{d}(q^{m\over d})
,
d
is a prime and
mf
is a power of
d
. In particular,
m
is even if and only if
d=p=2
.

Assume that
d=2
. Then
p=2
and
m
is a power of
2
, and so
A\cong\mathrm{Sp}_{2m-2}(q)
and
m\geq 8
.
Noting that
|G_{\alpha}|
is a divisor
of
|\mathbf{O}_{p}(G_{\alpha})|\mathrm{GL}_{2}(q^{m\over 2})|{m^{2}f^{2}\over 4}
, it follows that
(|G_{\alpha}|,\Phi^{*}_{2m-2}(q)\Phi^{*}_{2m-4}(q)\Phi^{*}_{m-1}(q))=1
.
Since
G=TG_{\alpha}=TB
, by Lemma
2.4
,
|T|
is divisible by
\Phi^{*}_{2m-2}(q)\Phi^{*}_{2m-4}(q)\Phi^{*}_{m-1}(q)
. By
[
32
, Table 10.1]
, we have
T=A\cong\mathrm{Sp}_{2m-2}(q)
.
Suppose that
\mathbf{O}_{p}(G_{\alpha})=1
. Then
|G_{\alpha}|_{p}
is a divisor of
{mf\over 2}q^{m\over 2}
. It follows that
q^{m-1}\leq|G_{\alpha}|_{p}\leq{mf\over 2}q^{m\over 2}<q^{{3m\over 4}+1}
, yielding
m<8
, a contradiction. Thus
\mathbf{O}_{p}(G_{\alpha})\neq 1
. Then part (1) of this lemma follows.

Assume
d
is odd.
Then
m
is odd, and
B=\mathsf{P}_{m}
or
\mathsf{P}_{m-1}
. Suppose that
q
is even. Then
A\cong\mathrm{Sp}_{2m-2}(q)
.
Noting that
|T|
is divisible by
\Phi^{*}_{2m-2}(q)\Phi^{*}_{2m-4}(q)\Phi^{*}_{2m-6}(q)
, by
[
32
, Table 10.1]
, we have
T=\mathrm{Sp}_{2m-2}(q)
or
\Omega^{-}_{2m-2}(q)
, and part (2) of this lemma follows.
Suppose that
q
is odd. Then
A\cong\Omega_{2m-1}(q){.}{4\over(4,m(q-1))}
.
Noting that
|G_{\alpha}|
is a divisor
of
|\mathbf{O}_{p}(G_{\alpha})|\mathrm{GL}_{d}(q^{m\over d})|{m^{2}f^{2}\over d^{2}}
,
it follows that
|B:G_{\alpha}|
is divisible by
\prod_{m>i\geq 2,(i,d)=1}\Phi^{*}_{i}(q)
. In particular,
|B:G_{\alpha}|
is divisible by

\Phi^{*}_{m-1}(q)
. Noting that
|G:G_{\alpha}|=|G:B||B:G_{\alpha}|
, it follows that
|G:G_{\alpha}|
is divisible by
\Phi^{*}_{m-1}(q)\prod_{i=1}^{m-1}(q^{i}+1)
.
Then
|T|
is divisible by
\Phi^{*}_{m-1}(q)\prod_{i=1}^{m-1}(q^{i}+1)
, and thus

|T|
has a divisor
(\Phi^{*}_{m-1}(q))^{2}\Phi^{*}_{2m-2}(q)\Phi^{*}_{2m-4}(q)
.
Since
m\geq 5
and
q
is odd,
\Phi^{*}_{m-1}(q)\neq 1
.
By
[
32
, Table 10.2]
,

T\cong\Omega_{2m-1}
, desired as in part (3) of this lemma.
∎

Lemma 8.13
.

Let
(m,q)\neq(6,2)
and
A=\mathsf{N}_{2}^{-}
. Then

T\cong\mathrm{P\Omega}^{-}_{2m-2}(q)
,
\mathrm{L}_{m}(q)\unlhd G_{\alpha}^{\Gamma(\alpha)}
,
m
is an odd prime and
f
is a power of
m
.

Proof.

In this case,
|G:A|=q^{2m-2}{(q^{m}-1)(q^{m-1}-1)\over 2(q+1)}
,
and
B
is
\mathsf{P}_{m}
,
\mathsf{P}_{m-1}
or
{}^{\bf\hat{}}\mathrm{GL}_{m}(2){.}2
(with
q=2
and
m
even). Then
|G:B|
is divisible by
\prod_{i=1}^{m-1}(q^{i}+1)
.
Let
\overline{A}=A/\mathbf{rad}(A)
and
\overline{X}=X\mathbf{rad}(A)/\mathbf{rad}(A)
. It is easily shown that

\mathbf{soc}(\overline{A})\cong\mathrm{P\Omega}^{-}_{2m-2}(q)
and
|\overline{X}|
is divisible by
\Phi^{*}_{2m-2}(q)\Phi^{*}_{2m-4}(q)\Phi^{*}_{2m-6}(q)
.
Noting that
(m,q)\neq(6,2)
, it follows from
[
32
, Table 10.1]
that either
\mathrm{P\Omega}^{-}_{2m-2}(q)\lesssim\overline{X}
, or
(m-1,q)=(4,2)
and
\Omega^{-}_{8}(2)\not\lesssim\overline{X}
. Suppose that the latter case occurs. Checking the maximal subgroups of
\Omega^{-}_{8}(2)
, we get
\overline{X}\lesssim\mathrm{L}_{2}(16){.}2
. Since
\mathrm{L}_{2}(16){.}2
has no proper subgroup of order divisible by
\Phi^{*}_{8}(2)\Phi^{*}_{4}(2)
, we have
\mathrm{L}_{2}(16)\lesssim\overline{X}
. If
T=X\cong\mathrm{L}_{2}(16)
then
|T|
is indivisible by
|G:B|
, a contradiction. Thus

X=G_{\alpha}
, and
T\leq B
, yielding
T\lesssim\mathrm{SL}_{5}(2)
.
By Lemma
2.10
, it is easy to show that
|G_{\alpha}|_{3}\leq 9
, and so
|T|_{3}|G_{\alpha}|_{3}\leq 3^{4}<3^{5}=|G|_{3}
, a contradiction. Therefore, we have
\mathrm{P\Omega}^{-}_{2m-2}(q)\lesssim\overline{X}
. This implies that
A\geq T\cong\mathrm{P\Omega}^{-}_{2m-2}(q)
. Then our result follows from Lemma
7.5
.
∎

Theorem 8.5
.

Assume that
Hypothesis
6.1
holds and
G=\mathrm{P\Omega}_{2m}^{+}(q)
, where
m\geq 5
and
q=p^{f}
. Then one of the following holds.

(1)

(m,q)=(6,2)
,
T=\Omega^{-}_{10}(2)
or
\mathrm{Sp}_{10}(2)
, and
\mathrm{SL}_{5}(2)\leq G_{\alpha}\leq[2^{20}]{:}\mathrm{SL}_{5}(2)
;

(2)

(m,q)=(6,2)
,
T=\mathrm{Sp}_{10}(2)
,
G_{\alpha}^{\Gamma(\alpha)}\cong\mathrm{SL}_{3}(2)
and
\mathbf{O}_{2}(G_{\alpha})\neq 1
.

(3)

T\cong\mathrm{Sp}_{2m-2}(q)
,
\mathrm{SL}_{2}(q^{m\over 2})\unlhd G_{\alpha}^{\Gamma(\alpha)}
,
\mathbf{O}_{2}(G_{\alpha})\neq 1
,
m\geq 8
,
p=2
and
mf
is a power of
2
.

(4)

T\cong\mathrm{Sp}_{2m-2}(q)
or
\Omega^{-}_{2m-2}(q)
,
\mathrm{L}_{d}(q^{m\over d})\unlhd G_{\alpha}^{\Gamma(\alpha)}
,
p=2
,
d
is and odd prime and
mf
is a power of
d
.

(5)

T\cong\Omega_{2m-1}(q)
,
\mathrm{L}_{d}(q^{m\over d})\unlhd G_{\alpha}^{\Gamma(\alpha)}
,
p
and
d
are odd, and
mf
is a power of
d
.

(6)

T=\mathrm{P\Omega}^{-}_{2m-2}(q)
,
\mathrm{L}_{m}(q)\unlhd G_{\alpha}^{\Gamma(\alpha)}
,
m
is an odd prime and
f
is a power of
m
.

Proof.

By Lemmas
8.10
,
8.12
and
8.13
, we assume that
A=\mathsf{P}_{1}
and
(m,q)\neq(6,2)
.
In this case,
m
is even,
|G:A|={(q^{m}-1)(q^{m-1}+1)\over(q-1)}
,
B={}^{\bf\hat{}}\mathrm{GU}_{m}(q){.}2
and
|G:B|={1\over 2}q^{m(m-1)\over 2}\prod_{i=1}^{m-1}(q^{i}+(-1)^{i})
. We next deduce a contradiction.

Suppose that
X=G_{\alpha}
.
Let
\overline{A}=A/\mathbf{rad}(A)
and
\overline{X}=X\mathbf{rad}(A)/\mathbf{rad}(A)
. Then
|\mathbf{rad}(A)|_{p}=q^{2m-2}
,

\mathbf{soc}(\overline{A})\cong\mathrm{P\Omega}^{+}_{2m-2}(q)
,
|\overline{A}:\mathbf{soc}(\overline{A})|\leq 2
and
|\overline{X}|
is divisible by
{1\over 2}q^{(m-4)(m-1)\over 2}\prod_{i=2}^{m-1}(q^{i}+(-1)^{i})
.
By Lemma
2.6
,
\overline{A}=\overline{A}\,\overline{A\cap B}
is core-free, where
\overline{A\cap B}=(A\cap B)\mathbf{rad}(A)/\mathbf{rad}(A)
.
By Lemma
2.7
, we have a core-free maximal factorization
G_{0}=A_{0}B_{0}
such that
\overline{A}\geq G_{0}=X_{0}B_{0}
,
X_{0}=A_{0}\cap\overline{X}
,
\mathbf{soc}(G_{0})\cong\mathrm{P\Omega}^{+}_{2m-2}(q)
,
\overline{X}^{\infty}\leq X_{0}
and
|X_{0}|
has a divisor
{1\over o}|\overline{X}|
, where
o\leq 2
.
In particular,
|X_{0}|
is divisible by
{1\over(4,p^{2})}q^{(m-4)(m-1)\over 2}\Phi^{*}_{2m-4}(q)\Phi^{*}_{m-1}(q)\Phi^{*}_{m-3}(q)
.
Noting that
m-1
is odd and
|A_{0}|
is divisible by
\Phi^{*}_{2m-4}(q)
, by
[
31
, Theorem A and Proposition 2.5]
,
A_{0}
has type
\mathsf{N}_{1}
or
\mathsf{N}^{-}_{2}
.
However, in this case,
|A_{0}|
is indivisible by
\Phi^{*}_{m-1}(q)
, a contradiction.

Now, we have
T\leq A
and
G_{\alpha}\leq B\cong{}^{\bf\hat{}}\mathrm{GU}_{m}(q){.}2
.
Then
|G_{\alpha}|
is divisible by
|G:A|
; in particular,
|G_{\alpha}|
is divisible by
\Phi^{*}_{m}(q)\Phi^{*}_{2m-2}(q)
. Applying Lemma
7.2
to the preimages of
G_{\alpha}\cap B^{\prime}
and
B^{\prime}
in
\mathrm{GL}_{m}(q^{2})
, we conclude that
(m,q^{2})=(4,9),\,(4,25)
or
(6,4)
. Then
m<5
or
(m,q)=(6,2)
, a contradiction.
∎

8.6.
Symplectic case

Let
G=\mathrm{PSp}_{2m}(q)
, where
m\geq 2
,
(m,q)\neq(2,2)
and
q=p^{f}
for some prime
p
. Then
A
and
B
are described as in
[
31
, Tables 1-3]
.

Lemma 8.14
.

A
and
B
are not in
[
31
, Table 3]
.

Proof.

Suppose that
A
and
B
are described as in
[
31
, Table 3]
. Since
A
and
B
are insolvable, they are listed as follows:

\tiny\begin{array}[]{l|l|l|l|l}G&A&|G:A|&B&|G:B|\\
\hline\cr\mathrm{PSp}_{6}(3)&\mathrm{L}_{2}(13)&2^{7}\cdot 3^{8}\cdot 5&[3^{5}]{:}2\mathrm{U}_{4}(2)&2^{2}\cdot 7\cdot 13\\
\hline\cr\mathrm{PSp}_{8}(2)&\Omega^{-}_{8}(2){.}2&2^{3}\cdot 3\cdot 5&\mathrm{S}_{10}&2^{8}\cdot 3\cdot 17\\
&\mathrm{L}_{2}(17)&2^{12}\cdot 3^{3}\cdot 5^{2}\cdot 7&\Omega^{+}_{8}(2){.}2&2^{3}\cdot 17\end{array}

Assume that
G=\mathrm{PSp}_{6}(3)
. Then
X=A\cong\mathrm{L}_{2}(13)
. Let
\overline{B}=B/\mathbf{rad}(B)
and
\overline{Y}=Y\mathbf{rad}(B)/\mathbf{rad}(B)
.
Then
\overline{B}\cong\mathrm{U}_{4}(2)
and
|\overline{Y}|
has a divisor
2^{6}\cdot 3^{3}\cdot 5
. Searching the subgroups of
\mathrm{U}_{4}(2)
, we conclude that
\overline{Y}\succcurlyeq\mathrm{U}_{4}(2)
or
\Omega_{5}(3)
.
By Lemma
4.2
, neither
X
nor
Y
plays the role of
G_{\alpha}
, a contradiction.
Thus
G=\mathrm{PSp}_{8}(2)
.

Suppose that
A\cong\mathrm{L}_{2}(17)
and
B\cong\Omega^{+}_{8}(2){.}2
.
It is easily shown that
X=A\cong\mathrm{L}_{2}(17)
, and
|Y\cap B^{\prime}|
has a divisor
5^{2}\cdot 7
. By
[
32
, Table 10.4]
,
Y\cap B^{\prime}\cong\Omega^{+}_{8}(2)
. Then
X\neq G_{\alpha}
and
Y\neq G_{\alpha}
, a contradiction.
Suppose that
A\cong\Omega^{-}_{8}(2){.}2
. Then

X\cong\Omega^{-}_{8}(2)
due to searching the subgroups of
\Omega^{-}_{8}(2)
with order divisible by
2^{7}\cdot 3\cdot 17
.
Thus
X=T
, and
G_{\alpha}=Y\leq B
,
which contradicts Lemma
7.7
.
∎

Lemma 8.15
.

If
A
and
B
are as in
[
31
, Table 2]
, then
p=2
,
f>2
is a power of
2
,
G=\mathrm{Sp}_{6}(q)
,
T\cong\mathrm{G}_{2}(q)
,
\mathrm{SL}_{2}(q^{2})\unlhd G_{\alpha}^{\Gamma(\alpha)}
and
\mathbf{O}_{2}(G_{\alpha})\neq 1
.

Proof.

Suppose that
A
and
B
are described as in
[
31
, Table 2]
. Then
q
is even, and
(G,A)
is one of
(\mathrm{Sp}_{4}(q),\mathrm{Sz}(q))
and
(\mathrm{Sp}_{6}(q),\mathrm{G}_{2}(q))
.

Assume that
G=\mathrm{Sp}_{4}(q)
and
A\cong\mathrm{Sz}(q)
. Then
f>1
is odd. Noting that
A=X(A\cap B)
, by
[
21
]
, we have
A=X
. Thus
T=X\cong\mathrm{Sz}(q)
, and
G_{\alpha}=Y\leq B\cong\mathrm{GO}^{+}_{4}(q)
. Since
G=TG_{\alpha}
and
|G:T|=q^{2}(q^{2}-1)(q+1)
, by Lemma
2.4
,
|G_{\alpha}|
divisible by
q^{2}(q^{2}-1)(q+1)
. Then
|B:G_{\alpha}|
is a divisor of
2(q-1)
. This implies that
G_{\alpha}
has a normal subgroup
\mathrm{L}_{2}(q)\times\mathrm{L}_{2}(q)
, which is impossible by Lemma
4.2
.

Assume that
G=\mathrm{Sp}_{6}(q)
and
A\cong\mathrm{G}_{2}(q)
. Then

|G:A|=q^{3}(q^{4}-1)
and
B\in\{\mathrm{GO}^{\pm}_{6}(q),\mathsf{P}_{1},\mathsf{N}_{2}\}
. Suppose that
X\neq A
. Noting that
A=X(A\cap B)
, by
[
21
]
,
q=4
and
X\cong\mathrm{J}_{2}
,
\mathrm{SU}_{3}(4)
or
\mathrm{SU}_{3}(4){.}2
.
Then
T=X\cong\mathrm{J}_{2}
or
\mathrm{U}_{3}(4)
, and
G_{\alpha}=Y\leq B
. Noting that
|T|
is divisible by
|G:B|
, the factorization
G=TB
is listed as follows:

\tiny\begin{array}[]{l|l|l|l}T&|G:T|&B&|G:B|\\
\hline\cr\mathrm{J}_{2}&2^{11}\cdot 3\cdot 5\cdot 13\cdot 17&\mathrm{U}_{4}(4){.}2&2^{5}\cdot 3^{3}\cdot 7\\
\hline\cr\mathrm{U}_{3}(4)&2^{12}\cdot 3^{3}\cdot 5\cdot 7\cdot 17&\mathrm{L}_{4}(4){.}2&2^{5}\cdot 5\cdot 13\\
\end{array}

Clearly,
S\preccurlyeq Y\cap B^{\prime}
, and then
\pi(Y)=\pi(S)=\pi(Y\cap B^{\prime})
. Then, by
[
32
, Table 10.3]
, we get

Y\cap B^{\prime}\cong\mathrm{U}_{4}(4)
or
\mathrm{L}_{4}(4)
. Thus
G_{\alpha}=Y\succcurlyeq\mathrm{U}_{4}(4)
or
\mathrm{L}_{4}(4)
, a contradiction.
Therefore,
X=A\cong\mathrm{G}_{2}(q)
, and so
T\cong\mathrm{G}_{2}(q)
for
q>2
.

Let
\overline{B}=B^{\infty}\mathbf{rad}(B)/\mathbf{rad}(B)
and
\overline{Y}=(Y\cap B^{\infty})\mathbf{rad}(B)/\mathbf{rad}(B)
.
Then
|\overline{Y}|
has a divisor
\Phi^{*}_{4}(q)
, and
S\preccurlyeq\overline{Y}
.
If
\overline{B}\cong\mathrm{L}_{4}(q)
,
\mathrm{PSp}_{4}(q)
or
\mathrm{PSp}_{2}(q)\times\mathrm{PSp}_{4}(q)
then, by Lemma
7.1
, we conclude that

S\cong\mathrm{L}_{2}(q^{2})
.
Assume that
\overline{B}\cong\mathrm{U}_{4}(q)
. Then
|\overline{Y}|
has a divisor
{1\over 2}q^{3}\Phi^{*}_{4}(q)
. Checking the maximal subgroups
\mathrm{U}_{4}(q)
, we have
\overline{Y}\lesssim q^{4}{:}([a]{.}\mathrm{L}_{2}(q^{2}){.}[b])
, where
ab={(q-1)(2,q^{2}-1)\over(4,q+1)}
. This implies that
S\cong\mathrm{L}_{2}(q^{2})
. All in all,

r=q^{2}+1
, and so
p=2
and
f
is a power of
2
by Lemma
2.1
. If
\mathbf{O}_{2}(G_{\alpha})=1
then
q^{3}\leq|G_{\alpha}|_{2}\leq q^{2}2f
,
yielding
q=4
. For
q=4
, confirmed by GAP, there exists no a desired graph. Then the lemma follows.
∎

Assume that
A
and
B
are as in
[
31
, Table 1]
.
For
(m,q)=(3,2)
or
(2,4)
,
A
and
B
are listed as follows:

\tiny\begin{array}[]{l|l|l|l|l}(m,q)&A&|G:A|&B&|G:B|\\
\hline\cr(3,2)&\mathrm{Sp}_{2}(8){.}3&2^{6}\cdot 3\cdot 5&2^{5}{:}\mathrm{S}_{6}&3^{2}\cdot 7\\
&&&\mathrm{S}_{8}&2^{2}\cdot 3^{2}\\
&&&\Omega^{-}_{6}(2){.}2&2^{2}\cdot 7\\
&\Omega^{-}_{6}(2){.}2&2^{2}\cdot 7&2^{6}{:}\mathrm{L}_{3}(2)&3^{3}\cdot 5\\
&&&\mathrm{S}_{8}&2^{2}\cdot 3^{2}\\
\hline\cr(2,4)&\mathrm{Sp}_{2}(16){.}2&2^{3}\cdot 3\cdot 5&2^{6}{:}(3\times\mathrm{Sp}_{2}(4))&5\cdot 17\\
&&&\Omega^{+}_{4}(4){.}2&2^{3}\cdot 17\\
&&&\Omega^{-}_{4}(4){.}2&2^{3}\cdot 3\cdot 5\\
&\Omega^{-}_{4}(4){.}2&2^{3}\cdot 3\cdot 5&2^{6}{:}\mathrm{GL}_{2}(4)&5\cdot 17\\
&&&(\mathrm{Sp}_{2}(4)\times\mathrm{Sp}_{2}(4)){:}2&2^{3}\cdot 17\end{array}

We first search the insolvable subgroups of
A
and
B
with orders divisible by
|G:B|
and
|G:A|
, respectively.
It follows that
X^{\prime}=A^{\prime}
, and either
Y^{\prime}=B^{\prime}
or

G_{\alpha}=Y
, and then all possible pairs
(T,G_{\alpha})
are easily determined. Calculation shows that no desired graph arises for
(m,q)=(3,2)
or
(4,4)
.

Lemma 8.16
.

Let
A
and
B
be as in
[
31
, Table 1]
.
Then
(m,q)\neq(3,2),\,(2,4)
.

For convenience, we list

A
,
B
and their indices in
G
as follows:

\tiny\begin{array}[]{l|l|l|l|l}A&|G:A|&B&|G:B|&\\
\hline\cr\mathrm{PSp}_{2a}(q^{b}){.}b&{1\over b}q^{m(m-a)}\prod_{(i,b)=1}(q^{2i}-1)&\mathsf{P}_{1}&{q^{2m}-1\over q-1}&\\
&&\mathrm{GO}^{\pm}_{2m}(q)&q^{m}{q^{m}\pm 1\over 2}&p=2\\
&&\mathsf{N}_{2}&q^{2(m-1)}{q^{2m}-1\over q^{2}-1}&b=q=2,2\,\,\big|\,\,m\\
\hline\cr\mathrm{GO}^{-}_{2m}(q)&q^{m}{q^{m}-1\over 2}&\mathsf{P}_{m}&\prod_{i=1}^{m}(q^{i}+1)&p=2\\
&&\mathrm{Sp}_{m}(q)\wr\mathrm{S}_{2}&{1\over 2}q^{m^{2}\over 2}\prod_{i=1}^{m\over 2}{q^{m+2i}-1\over q^{2i}-1}&2\,\,\big|\,\,m,p=2\\
&&\mathrm{GO}^{+}_{2m}(2)&q^{m}{q^{m}+1\over 2}&q=2\par\end{array}

where
b
is a prime such that
m=ab
.

Lemma 8.17
.

Let
A
and
B
be as in
[
31
, Table 1]
, and
m=2
. Then
p=2
,
f>2
is a power of
2
,
T\cong\mathrm{L}_{2}(q^{2})
, and either
G_{\alpha}\cong\mathrm{L}_{2}(q^{2})
or
\mathrm{SL}_{2}(q)\unlhd G_{\alpha}^{\Gamma(\alpha)}
and
\mathbf{O}_{2}(G_{\alpha})\neq 1
.

Proof.

Note that
A^{\prime}\cong\mathrm{L}_{2}(q^{2})
and
q>2
.
Assume that
B\cong\mathrm{GO}^{-}_{4}(q)
. Then
A\cong\mathrm{Sp}_{2}(q^{2}){.}2
,

B^{\prime}\cong\mathrm{L}_{2}(q^{2})
for even
q
, and
both
|X\cap A^{\prime}|
and
|Y\cap B^{\prime}|
are divisible by
{1\over 4}q^{2}(q^{2}-1)
. Checking the insolvable subgroups of
\mathrm{L}_{2}(q^{2})
, it follows that either
X\cap A^{\prime}\cong Y\cap B^{\prime}\cong\mathrm{L}_{2}(q^{2})
, or one of
X\cap A^{\prime}
and
Y\cap B^{\prime}
is isomorphic to
\mathrm{A}_{5}
if
q=4
.
By Lemma
8.16
,
q\neq 4
.
Thus
X\cap A^{\prime}\cong Y\cap B^{\prime}\cong\mathrm{L}_{2}(q^{2})
. It follows that
T\cong\mathrm{L}_{2}(q^{2})
,
G_{\alpha}\cong\mathrm{L}_{2}(q^{2}){.}o
and
r=q^{2}+1
, where
o\leq 2
.
Then, by Lemma
2.1
,
f
is a power of
2
. In particular,
f\geq 4
.
Suppose that
G_{\alpha}\cong\mathrm{L}_{2}(q^{2}){.}2
.
Then
|T:(T\cap G_{\alpha})|=|G:G_{\alpha}|={1\over 2}q^{2}(q^{2}-1)
, and thus

T_{\alpha}=T\cap G_{\alpha}\cong\mathrm{D}_{2r}
, a maximal subgroup of
T
.
Thus both
T
and
G
are primitive on
V
, and
T_{\alpha}
and
G_{\alpha}

have a common orbit on
V
of size
r
, which is impossible by
[
33
, Theorem 1 and Table 7.1]
.
Thus
G_{\alpha}\cong\mathrm{L}_{2}(q^{2})
.

Assume next that
B\not\cong\mathrm{GO}^{-}_{4}(q)
. Then
|X\cap A^{\prime}|
is divisible by
\Phi^{*}_{2}(q^{2})
. Note that
X\cap A^{\prime}
is insoluble. Checking the subgroups of
\mathrm{L}_{2}(q^{2})
, it follows that either
X\cap A^{\prime}=A^{\prime}
, or
q=p\equiv\pm 3\,({\mathrm{mod}~}10)
and
X\cap A^{\prime}\cong\mathrm{A}_{5}
.
For the latter, we have
\Phi^{*}_{2}(q^{2})=5
or
15
, which is impossible by
[
20
, Theorem 3.9]
. Thus
X=A^{\prime}
or
A
.

Let
\overline{B}=B^{\infty}\mathbf{rad}(B)/\mathbf{rad}(B)
and
\overline{Y}=(Y\cap B^{\infty})\mathbf{rad}(B)/\mathbf{rad}(B)
. Then
\overline{B}\cong\mathrm{L}_{2}(q)
or
\mathrm{L}_{2}(q)\times\mathrm{L}_{2}(q)
,
\overline{Y}
is insolvable and has order divisible by
\Phi^{*}_{2}(q)
. Clearly, if
q\neq p
then
\Phi^{*}_{2}(q)\neq 1
.
Checking the insolvable subgroups of
\mathrm{L}_{2}(q)
,
we conclude that either
\mathrm{L}_{2}(q)\preccurlyeq\overline{Y}
, or
\mathrm{A}_{5}\preccurlyeq\overline{Y}
and
q=p\equiv\pm 1\,({\mathrm{mod}~}10)
. Then
Y\succcurlyeq\mathrm{L}_{2}(q)
or
\mathrm{A}_{5}
.

If
T=Y
, recalling
T\not\cong\mathrm{A}_{5}
, then
q^{4}=|G|_{p}\leq|T|_{p}|G_{\alpha}|=|T|_{p}|X|\leq q(2,p)q^{2}
, which is impossible as
q>2
.
Thus we have
Y=G_{\alpha}
,
X=T
and
S=\mathbf{soc}(G_{\alpha}^{\Gamma(\alpha)})\cong\mathrm{L}_{2}(q)
or
\mathrm{A}_{5}
. If
S\cong\mathrm{A}_{5}
, recalling
q=p\equiv\pm 1\,({\mathrm{mod}~}10)
, then
r=5
and
|G_{\alpha}|_{p}=1
by Lemma
4.2
, and so
q^{4}=|G|_{p}\leq|T|_{p}|G_{\alpha}|_{p}=q^{2}
, a contradiction.
If
q=11
then
r=11
and
|G_{\alpha}|_{p}=q
, so
q^{4}=|G|_{p}\leq|T|_{p}|G_{\alpha}|_{p}=q^{3}
, a contradiction.
Therefore,
r=q+1
,
p=2
and
f
is power of
2
.
If
\mathbf{O}_{2}(G_{\alpha})=1
then
q^{4}=|G|_{2}\leq|G_{\alpha}|_{2}|T|_{2}\leq|\mathrm{\Gamma L}_{2}(q)|_{2}|T|_{2}=q^{3}f
, yielding
q\leq f
, a contradiction.
Thus
\mathbf{O}_{2}(G_{\alpha})\neq 1
, and the lemma follows.
∎

Lemma 8.18
.

Let
A
and
B
be as in
[
31
, Table 1]
, and
m\geq 3
. Assume that
A\cong\mathrm{GO}^{-}_{2m}(q)
for even
q
. Then
one of the following holds.

(1)

T\cong\Omega^{-}_{2m}(q)
and
S\cong\mathrm{L}_{d}(q^{m\over d})
,
d
is an odd prime and
mf
is a power of
d
.

(2)

T\cong\Omega^{-}_{2m}(q)
and
S\cong\mathrm{L}_{2}(q^{m\over 2})
,
\mathbf{O}_{2}(G_{\alpha})\neq 1
and
mf
is a power of
2
.

(3)

q=2
,
T\cong\Omega^{+}_{2m}(2)
,
S\cong\mathrm{L}_{2}(2^{m})
and
\mathbf{O}_{2}(G_{\alpha})=1
, where
m
is a power of
2
.

(4)

G=\mathrm{Sp}_{12}(2)
,
T\cong\Omega^{-}_{12}(2)
, either
S\cong\mathrm{L}_{3}(2)
and
\mathbf{O}_{2}(G_{\alpha})\neq 1
or
S\cong\mathrm{L}_{5}(2)
.

Proof.

Recall that
\{X,Y\}=\{T,G_{\alpha}\}
with
X\leq A
and
Y\leq B
.
We discuss in three cases according the structure of
B
.

Case 1
.
Let
B=\mathsf{P}_{m}
. Then
|G:B|
has a divisor
\Phi^{*}_{2m}(q)\Phi^{*}_{2m-2}(q)\Phi^{*}_{2m-4}(q)
. Since
|X|
is divisible by
|G:B|
, it is easily shown that

|X\cap A^{\prime}|
is divisible by
\Phi^{*}_{2m}(q)\Phi^{*}_{2m-2}(q)\Phi^{*}_{2m-4}(q)
. By
[
32
, Tables 10.1 and 10.3]
, either
q=2
and
m\in\{4,5\}
or

X\cap A^{\prime}=A^{\prime}\cong\Omega^{-}_{2m}(q)
. For the former,

|G:B|=3^{3}\cdot 5\cdot 17
or
3^{4}\cdot 5\cdot 11\cdot 17
, by
the Atlas
[
5
]
and
[
32
, Table 10.4]
, also
X\cap A^{\prime}=A^{\prime}\cong\Omega^{-}_{2m}(q)
. Since
m\geq 3
,
it follows that
T=X\cong\Omega^{-}_{2m}(q)
, and
G_{\alpha}=Y\leq B
.
Then
S
is described as in Lemma
7.7
.

If
S\cong\mathrm{L}_{d}(q^{m\over d})
for some prime
d
then
part (1) or (2) of this lemma occurs.
For the triples given in Lemma
7.7
(2) and (3), by Lemma
4.2
and calculating
|G_{\alpha}|_{2}
, we conclude that
(S,m,q)
is one of
(\mathrm{A}_{7},4,2)
,
(\mathrm{L}_{3}(2),6,2)
and
(\mathrm{L}_{5}(2),6,2)
.
Suppose that
(S,m,q)=(\mathrm{A}_{7},4,2)
. By Lemma
4.2
, noting
|G|_{2}\leq|T|_{2}|G_{\alpha}|_{2}
, we have
G_{\alpha}\cong\mathrm{S}_{7}
.
Then
\mathrm{S}_{7}\cong G_{\alpha}\mathbf{O}_{2}(B)/\mathbf{O}_{2}(B)\leq\overline{B}\cong\mathrm{A}_{8}
, which is impossible.
Thus
T
and
S
are desired as in part (4) of this lemma.

Case 2
. Let
B\cong\mathrm{Sp}_{m}(q)\wr\mathrm{S}_{2}
.
Suppose that
T=Y\leq B
. Then
G_{\alpha}=X\leq A
,
T\lesssim\mathrm{Sp}_{m}(q)
, and
|G:T|

is divisible by
q^{3m^{2}\over 4}\prod_{i={m\over 2}+1}^{m}(q^{2i}-1)
. Since
|G_{\alpha}|
is divisible by
|G:T|
,
it is easily shown that

|G_{\alpha}\cap A^{\prime}|
is divisible
{1\over 2}q^{3m^{2}\over 4}\prod_{i={m\over 2}+1}^{m}(q^{2i}-1)
. Checking
[
32
, Tables 10.1, 10.3 and 10.4]
, we conclude that

G_{\alpha}\cap A^{\prime}=A^{\prime}\cong\Omega^{-}_{2m}(q)
, which is impossible by Lemma
4.2
. Then
T=X\lesssim\Omega^{-}_{2m}(q)
and
G_{\alpha}=Y\leq B
.
By a similar argument as in Case 1,
T
and
S
are described as in part (2) or (4) of this lemma.

Case 3
. Let
B\cong\mathrm{GO}^{+}_{2m}(2)
,
m\geq 4
and
q=2
.
If
G_{\alpha}=Y\leq B
then Lemma
7.7
works, and
T
and
S
are described as in part (2) or (4) of this lemma.

Suppose next that
G_{\alpha}=X\leq A
. Then
T\leq B^{\prime}\cong\Omega^{+}_{2m}(2)
, and

|G:T|
is divisible by
2^{m}(2^{m}+1)
.
Considering
G_{\alpha}\cap A^{\prime}
, by Lemma
7.1
, either
S\cong\mathrm{L}_{2}(2^{m})
with
m
a power of
2
, or
(S,2m,q)
is one of
(\mathrm{A}_{11},10,2)
,
(\mathrm{A}_{13},12,2)
,
(\mathrm{A}_{19},18,2)
,
(\mathrm{M}_{11},10,2)
,
(\mathrm{L}_{2}(11),10,2)
and
(\mathrm{L}_{3}(3),12,2)
. The triples
(\mathrm{M}_{11},10,2)
and
(\mathrm{L}_{2}(11),10,2)
are excluded by calculation of
|G_{\alpha}|_{2}
. If
(S,2m,q)=(\mathrm{L}_{3}(3),12,2)
then

5\not\in\pi(G_{\alpha})
by Lemma
4.2
,
which contradicts that
|G_{\alpha}|
is divisible by
2^{m}+1
.
Assume that
(S,2m,q)=(\mathrm{A}_{11},10,2)
,
(\mathrm{A}_{13},12,2)
or
(\mathrm{A}_{19},18,2)
.
By
[
31
, pp. 52, 3.2.4(e)]
,
A\cap B\cong\mathrm{Sp}_{2m-2}(2)\times 2
, which is maximal in both
A
and
B
.
Since
A=G_{\alpha}(A\cap B)
, we have a core-free maximal factorization of
A
with a factor
\mathrm{Sp}_{2m-2}(2)\times 2
and the other factor containing
\mathrm{A}_{2m+1}
, which leads to a contradiction by checking the maximal factorizations of
\mathrm{GO}^{-}_{2m}(2)
.

Now
S\cong\mathrm{L}_{2}(2^{m})
, where
m
is a power of
2
.
By Lemma
7.1
,
|G_{\alpha}\cap A^{\prime}|_{2}
is a divisor of
m|S|_{2}
.
Then
|G_{\alpha}|
is a divisor of
2m2^{m}
.
If
\mathbf{O}_{2}(G_{\alpha})\neq 1
then
|G_{\alpha}|_{2}\geq 2^{2m}
, and so
2^{m}\leq 2m
, yielding
m\leq 2
, a contradiction. Thus
\mathbf{O}_{2}(G_{\alpha})=1
.
It is easily shown that
|G:G_{\alpha}|
has a divisor
{2^{m(m-1)}\over 4m(2^{m}-1)}\prod_{i=1}^{m-1}(2^{2i}-1)
, and then
|T|
is divisible by
{2^{m(m-1)}\over 4m(2^{m}-1)}\prod_{i=1}^{m-1}(2^{2i}-1)
.
If
m>4
then, by
[
32
, Table 10.1]
,
T\cong\mathrm{Sp}_{2m-2}(2)
or
\Omega^{+}_{2m}(2)
.
Assume that
m=4
. Then
|T|
is divisible by
2^{8}\cdot 3^{3}\cdot 7
. Checking the subgroups of
\Omega^{+}_{8}(2)
, we have
T\cong\Omega^{+}_{8}(2)
or
\mathrm{Sp}_{6}(2)
.
Suppose that
T\cong\mathrm{Sp}_{2m-2}(2)
.
Then
|G:T|
is divisible by
2^{2m-1}
, which is a divisor of
|G_{\alpha}|
. Since
\mathbf{O}_{2}(G_{\alpha})=1
, we have
|G_{\alpha}|_{2}\leq 2^{m}mf
.
It follows that
2^{2m-1}\leq 2^{m}m
, and so
2^{m-1}\leq m
, which is impossible as
m>2
. Thus
T\cong\Omega^{+}_{2m}(2)
, and part (3) of this lemma occurs.
∎

Lemma 8.19
.

Let
m\geq 3
and
A\cong\mathrm{PSp}_{2a}(q^{b}){.}b
, where
b
is a prime and
m=ab
. Assume that
B\not\cong\mathrm{GO}^{-}_{2m}(q)
. Then
p=2
and one of the following holds.

(1)

T\cong\Omega^{+}_{2m}(q)
,
S\cong\mathrm{L}_{2}(q^{m})
,
\mathbf{O}_{2}(G_{\alpha})=1
and
mf
is a power of
2
.

(2)

m=3
,
q>2
,
T\cong\mathrm{L}_{2}(q^{3})
,
S\cong\mathrm{SL}_{2}(q^{2})
,
\mathbf{O}_{2}(G_{\alpha})\neq 1
and
f
is a power of
2
.

Proof.

Assume that
X=G_{\alpha}
. Then
T\leq B
, and so
T\lesssim\mathrm{PSp}_{2m-2}(q)
or
\Omega^{+}_{2m}(q)
. In particular,
|G:T|
has a divisor
q^{m}(q^{m}+1)
. Then
|X\cap A^{\prime}|
is divisible by
{1\over(b,p)}q^{m}\Phi^{*}_{2m}(q)
. Since
(m,q)\neq(3,2)
, we have
\Phi^{*}_{2m}(q)\neq 1
.
Clearly,
S\preccurlyeq X\cap A^{\prime}
.
Applying Lemma
7.1
to

X\cap A^{\prime}
and
A^{\prime}
, we conclude

S\cong\mathrm{L}_{d}(q^{b{2a\over d}})
, where
d
is a prime divisor of
2a
and
mf
is power of
d
. By Lemma
2.1
,
we have
d=b=p=2
. Again by Lemma
7.1
,
|X\cap A^{\prime}|_{2}
is a divisor of
q^{m}a
, and so
|G_{\alpha}|_{2}
is a divisor of
q^{m}m
. Thus
q^{m}m\geq|G:T|_{2}
as
|G_{\alpha}|
is divisible by
|G:T|
. It follows that
T\leq B\cong\mathrm{GO}^{+}_{2m}(q)
.
It is easily shown that
|G:G_{\alpha}|
is divisible by

{1\over m}q^{m^{2}-m}\Phi^{*}_{2m-2}(q)\Phi^{*}_{2m-4}(q)\Phi^{*}_{m-1}(q)
, which is a divisor of
|T|
. By
[
32
, Table 10.1]
,
we conclude that either
T=B^{\prime}\cong\Omega^{+}_{2m}(q)
, or
(m,q)=(4,2)
. For the latter case, noting that
|T|
is divisible by
2^{10}\cdot 5\cdot 7
, we have
T\cong\Omega^{+}_{8}(2)
by
[
32
, Table 10.4]
.
Then part (1) of this lemma occurs.

We assume next that
X=T
. Then
T\lesssim\mathrm{PSp}_{2a}(q^{b})
,
G_{\alpha}=Y\leq B
, and
|G_{\alpha}|
is divisible by
q^{m(m-a)}\prod_{(i,b)=1}(q^{2i}-1)
.
Let
\overline{B}=B^{\prime}\mathbf{rad}(B)/\mathbf{rad}(B)
and
\overline{Y}=(Y\cap B^{\prime})\mathbf{rad}(B)/\mathbf{rad}(B)
. Then
S\preccurlyeq\overline{Y}
and
|\overline{Y}|
is divisible by
\prod_{(i,b)=1,i>1}(q^{2i}-1)
. In particular,
|\overline{Y}|
is divisible by

\prod_{(i,b)=1,i>1}\Phi^{*}_{2i}(q)
.

Suppose that
B=\mathsf{P}_{1}
or
\mathsf{N}_{2}
. Then
\overline{B}\cong\mathrm{PSp}_{2m-2}(q)
.
By Lemma
7.1
, either
S\cong\mathrm{L}_{2}(q^{m-1})
,

p=2
and
(m-1)f
is a power of
2
, or
(S,2(m-1),q)
is one of the triples

(\mathrm{A}_{5},4,3)
,
(\mathrm{A}_{7},4,7)
,
(\mathrm{A}_{7},6,3)
,
(\mathrm{A}_{7},6,5)
,
(\mathrm{A}_{11},10,2)
,
(\mathrm{A}_{13},12,2)
,
(\mathrm{A}_{19},18,2)
,
(\mathrm{M}_{11},10,2)
,
(\mathrm{L}_{3}(2),6,3)
,
(\mathrm{L}_{3}(2),6,5)
,
(\mathrm{L}_{2}(11),10,2)
,
(\mathrm{L}_{3}(3),12,2)
.
Recalling that
|G_{\alpha}|
is divisible by
q^{m(m-a)}\prod_{(i,b)=1}(q^{2i}-1)
, the latter case is excluded by Lemma
4.2
and calculating
|G_{\alpha}|_{p}
or
\pi(G_{\alpha})
.
Then
S\cong\mathrm{L}_{2}(q^{m-1})
. Assume
m>3
. Then
|G:G_{\alpha}|
has a divisor
\prod_{i={m+1\over 2}}^{m-2}\Phi^{*}_{2i}(q)
, and so

|T|
is divisible by
\prod_{i={m+1\over 2}}^{m-2}\Phi^{*}_{2i}(q)
. Considering
\pi(\mathrm{PSp}_{2a}(q^{b}))
, we have
b\geq{m+1\over 2}
, yielding
b=m
and
T\lesssim\mathrm{PSp}_{2}(q^{m})
. Since
\Phi^{*}_{2m-4}(q)
is a divisor of
|T|
, it implies that
(m,q)=(5,2)
and
S\cong\mathrm{L}_{2}(16)
. In this case,
q^{m(m-a)}\prod_{(i,b)=1}(q^{2i}-1)
has a divisor
7
, and so
7\in\pi(G_{\alpha})=\pi(S)=\{2,3,5,17\}
, a contradiction.
Thus
m=3
,

T\lesssim\mathrm{L}_{2}(q^{3})
. Then
T\cong\mathrm{L}_{2}(q^{3})
by checking the subgroups of
\mathrm{L}_{2}(q^{3})
. If
\mathbf{O}_{2}(G_{\alpha})=1
then

q^{6}\leq|G_{\alpha}|_{2}\leq|\mathrm{P\Gamma L}_{2}(q^{2})|_{2}
, yielding
q^{4}\leq 2f
, a contradiction. Therefore, part (2) of this lemma occurs.

Now let
B\cong\mathrm{GO}^{+}_{2m}(q)
. Recall that
|G_{\alpha}|
is divisible by
q^{m(m-a)}\prod_{(i,b)=1}(q^{2i}-1)
. Assume that
(m,q)=(4,2)
. Then
|G_{\alpha}|
is divisible by
2^{8}\cdot 3^{3}\cdot 7
. Checking the maximal subgroups of
\mathrm{GO}^{+}_{8}(2)
, we have
G_{\alpha}\lesssim\mathrm{Sp}_{6}(2)\times 2
. Searching subgroups of
\mathrm{Sp}_{6}(2)
with order divisible by
3^{3}\cdot 7
, we conclude that
\mathrm{U}_{3}(3)\preccurlyeq G_{\alpha}
, a contradiction.
Then
(m,q)\neq(4,2)
, and
\Phi^{*}_{(2m-2)f}(q)\neq 1
.
Recalling the limitations on
G_{\alpha}
, by
[
2
, Theorem 3.1]
, we conclude that
G_{\alpha}\lesssim\mathrm{GO}^{-}_{2}(q)\times\mathrm{GO}^{-}_{2m-2}(q)
, or
\mathrm{Sp}_{2m-2}(q)
.
Then Lemma
7.1
works for

G_{\alpha}
modulo a suitable solvable group.
Arguing similarly as above, we have part (2) of this lemma.
∎

Theorem 8.6
.

Assume that
Hypothesis
6.1
holds and
G=\mathrm{PSp}_{2m}(q)
, where
m\geq 2
and
q=p^{f}
. Then
q
is even and one of the following holds.

(1)

m=2
,
f>2
is a power of
2
,
T\cong\mathrm{L}_{2}(q^{2})
, and either
G_{\alpha}\cong\mathrm{L}_{2}(q^{2})
or
\mathrm{SL}_{2}(q)\unlhd G_{\alpha}^{\Gamma(\alpha)}
and
\mathbf{O}_{2}(G_{\alpha})\neq 1
.

(2)

m=3
,
q>2
,
f
is a power of
2
,
T\cong\mathrm{G}_{2}(q)

(
with
f>2
)
or
\mathrm{L}_{2}(q^{3})
,
\mathrm{SL}_{2}(q^{2})\unlhd G_{\alpha}^{\Gamma(\alpha)}
and
\mathbf{O}_{2}(G_{\alpha})\neq 1
.

(3)

(m,q)=(6,2)
,
T\cong\Omega^{-}_{12}(2)
, either
S\cong\mathrm{L}_{3}(2)
and
\mathbf{O}_{2}(G_{\alpha})\neq 1
or
S\cong\mathrm{L}_{5}(2)
.

(4)

m\geq 5
,
T\cong\Omega^{-}_{2m}(q)
,
S\cong\mathrm{L}_{d}(q^{m\over d})
,
d
is an odd prime and
mf
is a power of
d
.

(5)

m\geq 4
,
T\cong\Omega^{-}_{2m}(q)
and
S\cong\mathrm{L}_{2}(q^{m\over 2})
,
\mathbf{O}_{2}(G_{\alpha})\neq 1
and
mf
is a power of
2
.

(6)

T\cong\Omega^{+}_{2m}(q)
,
S\cong\mathrm{L}_{2}(q^{m})
,
\mathbf{O}_{2}(G_{\alpha})=1
,
m\geq 4
and
mf
is a power of
2
.

Proof.

Based on the previous discussion, we assume that

A\cong\mathrm{PSp}_{2a}(q){.}b
and
B\cong\mathrm{GO}^{-}_{2m}(q)
, where
q
is even,
m\geq 3
,
(m,q)\neq(3,2)
, and
b
is a prime divisor of
m
with
m=ab
.
If
G_{\alpha}\leq B
then part (2) of this lemma occurs by a similar argument as in the last paragraph of the proof of Lemma
8.19
.

Now Let
G_{\alpha}\leq A
and
T\leq B
. By Lemma
7.7
and Case 4 in its proof, we conclude that
a\geq 3
,

S\cong\mathrm{L}_{d}(q^{m\over d})
,
d
is a prime divisor of
a
and
mf
is a power of
d
. Assume that
m
is even. Then
d=b=2
,
a\geq 4
and
S\cong\mathrm{L}_{2}(q^{m\over 2})
. It is easily shown that
|G:G_{\alpha}|
is divisible by
\prod_{i={m+2\over 2}}^{m}\Phi^{*}_{2i}(q)
. By
[
32
, Table 10.1]
,
T\cong\Omega^{-}_{2m}(q)
, and then
T
and
S
are desired as in part (5) of this lemma. Now let
m
be odd; in particular,
a,\,b
and
d
are odd.
Then
A\cap B\cong\mathrm{GO}_{2a}^{-}(q^{b}){.}b
, which is maximal in
B
. Suppose that
T\not\cong\Omega_{2m}^{-}(q)
.
Noting
B=X(A\cap B)
, by Lemma
2.6
, we have a core-free maximal factorization
G_{0}=A_{0}B_{0}
with
\mathbf{soc}(G_{0})\cong\Omega_{2m}^{-}(q)
and
\Omega^{-}_{2a}(q^{b})\lesssim B_{0}
.
It follows from
[
31
, Theorem A]
that
q\in\{2,4\}
and
b=2
, a contradiction. Thus
T\cong\Omega_{2m}^{-}(q)
, and
T
and
S
are desired as in part (4) of this lemma.
∎

8.7.
Linear case

Let
G=\mathrm{L}_{n}(q)
, where
n\geq 2
and
q=p^{f}
for some prime
p
. Since
A
and
B
are insolvable, by
[
31
, Theorem A]
and Corollary
5.1
,
n
is not a prime,
A\cong{}^{\bf\hat{}}\mathrm{GL}_{a}(q^{b}){.}b
or
\mathrm{PSp}_{n}(q){.}[c]
, and
B\cong[q^{n-1}]{:}({q-1\over(n,q-1)}{.}\mathrm{PGL}_{n-1}(q))
, where
a\geq 2
,
b
is a prime with
n=ab
, and
c={(2,q-1)({n\over 2},q-1)\over(n,q-1)}
.
Recalling
G\not\cong\mathrm{A}_{8}
, we have
(m,q)\neq(4,2)
.

Lemma 8.20
.

If
G_{\alpha}\leq A
then
T\cong\mathrm{L}_{n-1}(q)
,
S\cong\mathrm{L}_{d}(q^{n\over d})
, where
d
is an odd prime and
nf
is a power of
d
.

Proof.

Let
G_{\alpha}\leq A
. Then
T\lesssim\mathrm{L}_{n-1}(q)
, and
|G:T|={(n-1,q-1)\over(n,q-1)}q^{n-1}(q^{n}-1)
.
Let
X_{0}=G_{\alpha}\cap A^{\prime}
. Then
S\preccurlyeq X_{0}
and
|X_{0}|
is divisible by
{1\over(e,p)}q^{n-1}(q^{n}-1)
, where
e=b
or
c
.
Suppose that
(n,q)=(6,2)
. Then
A^{\prime}\cong\mathrm{GL}_{3}(4)
or
\mathrm{Sp}_{6}(2)
, and
|G_{\alpha}|
is divisible by
2^{4}\cdot 3^{2}\cdot 7
.
Checking the subgroups of
A^{\prime}
, we conclude that

X_{0}
has a composition factor
\mathrm{L}_{3}(4)
,
\mathrm{U}_{3}(3)
,
\mathrm{A}_{8}
or
\mathrm{Sp}_{6}(2)
, which is impossible by Lemma
4.2
. Thus
(n,q)\neq(6,2)
, and
\Phi^{*}_{nf}(p)\neq 1
. Then Lemma
7.1
works here.
By calculation of
\pi(S)
or
|G_{\alpha}|_{p}
, we conclude that
A\cong{}^{\bf\hat{}}\mathrm{GL}_{a}(q^{b}){.}b
,
S\cong\mathrm{L}_{d}(q^{n\over d})
,
d
is an odd prime divisor of
a
and
nf
is a power of
d
.
In particular,
a\geq 3
and
n
is odd.
Then
|G:A|
has a divisor
(q^{n-1}-1)(q^{n-2}-1)
. By
[
32
, Table 10.1]
, we have
T\cong\mathrm{L}_{n-1}(q)
, and the lemma holds.
∎

Lemma 8.21
.

If
G_{\alpha}\leq B
then one of the following holds.

(1)

(n,q)=(4,4)
,
T\cong\mathrm{Sp}_{4}(4)
,
S\cong\mathrm{L}_{3}(2)
and
\mathbf{O}_{2}(G_{\alpha})\neq 1
.

(2)

S\cong\mathrm{L}_{n-1}(q)
,
n-1
is an odd prime and
f
is a power of
n-1
.

Proof.

Let
G_{\alpha}\leq B
, set

\overline{B}=B/\mathbf{rad}(B)
and
\overline{Y}=Y\mathbf{rad}(B)/\mathbf{rad}(B)
. Then
S\preccurlyeq\overline{Y}
. Note that
|G:A|
has a divisor
q^{n-1}-1\over q-1
, which is also a divisor of
|G_{\alpha}|
. Then
|\overline{Y}|
is divisible by
q^{n-1}-1\over q-1
, and hence divisible by
\Phi^{*}_{(n-1)f}(p)
.

Assume that
(n,q)=(4,4)
. Then
\overline{B}\cong\mathrm{PGL}_{3}(4)
.
Checking the subgroups of
\mathrm{PGL}_{3}(4)
, we conclude that
S\cong\overline{Y}\cong\mathrm{L}_{3}(2)
. Note that
T\lesssim\mathrm{L}_{2}(16)
or
\mathrm{Sp}_{4}(4)
. Checking the subgroups of
\mathrm{L}_{2}(16)
and
\mathrm{Sp}_{4}(4)
, we conclude that
T\cong\mathrm{L}_{2}(16)
or
\mathrm{Sp}_{4}(4)
.
By Lemma
4.2
,
|G_{\alpha}|_{5}=1
. If
T\cong\mathrm{L}_{2}(16)
then
5^{2}=|G|_{5}\leq|T|_{5}|G_{\alpha}|_{5}=5
, a contradiction.
Then
T\cong\mathrm{Sp}_{4}(4)
, and
|G:T|=2^{4}\cdot 3^{2}\cdot 7
. By
Lemma
4.2
,
\mathbf{O}_{2}(G_{\alpha})\neq 1
, and part (1) of this lemma follows.

Now
(n,q)\neq(4,4)
, and
\Phi^{*}_{(n-1)f}(p)\neq 1
. By Lemma
7.1
, one of the following holds:

(i)

S\cong\mathrm{L}_{d}(q^{n-1\over d})
,
d
is a prime and
(n-1)f
is a power of
d
;

(ii)

(S,n-1,q)
is one of
(\mathrm{A}_{7},3,25)
,
(\mathrm{L}_{3}(2),3,25)
,
(\mathrm{L}_{3}(2),3,9)
and
(\mathrm{L}_{2}(11),5,4)
.

The case (ii) is easily excluded by calculating
|G_{\alpha}|_{p}
and
|G:A|_{p}
. Thus (i) occurs.

Suppose that
A\cong\mathrm{PSp}_{n}(q){.}[c]
. Then
T\lesssim\mathrm{PSp}_{n}(q)
,
n
is even
and
|G:T|={(2,q-1)\over(n,q-1)}q^{n(n-2)\over 4}\prod_{i=2}^{n\over 2}(q^{2i-1}-1)
. In particular,
|G_{\alpha}|
is divisible by
\Phi^{*}_{n-3}(q)
. Clearly,
\Phi^{*}_{n-3}(q)\neq 1
. Since
\pi(G_{\alpha})=\pi(S)
, we have

(\Phi^{*}_{n-3}(q),|S|)\neq 1
. By Lemma
2.2
,

n-3
is a divisor
of
(n-1)i\over d
for some
2\leq i\leq d
. It follows that
d=n-1
, and so
S\cong\mathrm{L}_{n-1}(q)
.

Suppose that
A\cong{}^{\bf\hat{}}\mathrm{GL}_{a}(q^{b}){.}b
.
If
b=2
or
b\geq 5
then
|G_{\alpha}|
is divisible by
\Phi^{*}_{n-3}(q)
, and so
d=n-1
by a similar argument as above. Now let
b=3
. Then
|G_{\alpha}|
is divisible by
\Phi^{*}_{n-2}(q)
.
Clearly,
n\neq 8
and
n-2>2
. Then
\Phi^{*}_{n-2}(q)\neq 1
, this also yields
d=n-1
. Thus the lemma follows.
∎

Theorem 8.7
.

Assume that
Hypothesis
6.1
holds and
G=\mathrm{L}_{n}(q)
, where
n\geq 4
and
q=p^{f}
. Then one of the following holds.

(1)

T\cong\mathrm{L}_{n-1}(q)
,
S\cong\mathrm{L}_{d}(q^{n\over d})
,
d
is an odd prime and
nf
is a power of
d
.

(2)

(n,q)=(4,4)
,
T\cong\mathrm{Sp}_{4}(4)
,
S\cong\mathrm{L}_{3}(2)
and
\mathbf{O}_{2}(G_{\alpha})\neq 1
.

(3)

S\cong\mathrm{L}_{n-1}(q)
,
n-1
is an odd prime,
f
is a power of
n-1
, and

T\cong\mathrm{L}_{m}(q^{n\over m})
,
m
is a proper divisor of
n
; or

T\cong\mathrm{PSp}_{m}(q^{n\over m})
,
m\geq 4
is even, and
q
is even if
m=n
; or

T\cong\mathrm{P\Omega}^{-}_{m}(q^{n\over m})
,
4\,\,\big|\,\,m
,
m\geq 8
, and
q
is even if
m=n
; or

T\cong\mathrm{G}_{2}(q^{n\over 6})
,
6\,\,\big|\,\,n
,
q
is even; or

T\cong\mathrm{U}_{3}(3)
and
(n,q)=(6,2)
.

Proof.

By Lemmas
8.20
and
8.21
, we assume that

T\leq A
,
S\cong\mathrm{L}_{n-1}(q)
and
f
is a power of
n-1
.
We shall show that
T
is described as in part (3) of lemma.

Since
G=TB
, by Lemma
2.4
,
|T|
is divisible by
q^{n}-1\over q-1
.
Assume that
(n,q)=(6,2)
. Then
T\lesssim\mathrm{L}_{2}(8)
,
\mathrm{L}_{3}(4)
or
\mathrm{Sp}_{6}(2)
. Checking the subgroups of
\mathrm{L}_{2}(8)
,
\mathrm{L}_{3}(4)
and
\mathrm{Sp}_{6}(2)
, it follows that

T\cong\mathrm{L}_{2}(8),\,\mathrm{U}_{3}(3)
or
\mathrm{Sp}_{6}(2)
, desired as in part (3). Thus we assume next that
(n,q)\neq(6,2)
. Then
\Phi^{*}_{nf}(p)\neq 1
.

Case 1
. Assume that
A\cong{}^{\bf\hat{}}\mathrm{GL}_{a}(q^{b}){.}b
. Then
T\lesssim\mathrm{L}_{a}(q^{b})
.
Choose a minimal divisor
m
of
n
such that
T\lesssim\mathrm{L}_{m}(q_{0})
, where
q_{0}=q^{n\over m}
. Then
m\leq a
,
|T|
is divisible by
q_{0}^{m}-1\over q-1
and
\Phi^{*}_{m{nf\over m}}(p)
.
If
m=2
then
T\lesssim\mathrm{L}_{2}(q^{n\over 2})
and
q_{0}
is not a prime, and so
T\cong\mathrm{L}_{2}(q^{n\over 2})
by checking the subgroups of
\mathrm{L}_{2}(q^{n\over 2})
. Thus we let
m\geq 3
, and so
a\geq 3
.
Noting that
T
is not an alternating group, by
[
2
, Theorem 3.1]
, one of the following holds:

(i)

T\cong\mathrm{P\Omega}^{-}_{m}(q_{0})
,
\mathrm{PSp}_{m}(q_{0})
or
\mathrm{L}_{m}(q_{0})
;

(ii)

T\cong\mathrm{U}_{m}(\sqrt{q_{0}})
,
q_{0}
is a square and
m
is odd;

(iii)

(T,m,q_{0})
is one of the following triples:

(\mathrm{J}_{3},9,4)
,

(\mathrm{L}_{3}(2),3,9)
,
(\mathrm{L}_{3}(2),3,25)
,

(\mathrm{L}_{2}(11),5,4)
,

(\mathrm{L}_{2}(13),6,4)
,
(\mathrm{L}_{2}(19),9,4)
,

(\mathrm{Suz}(q_{0}),4,2^{nf\over m})
and
(\mathrm{G}_{2}(q_{0}),6,2^{nf\over m})
.

We only need deal with cases (ii) and (iii).

Suppose that (ii) occurs. Since
m\geq 3
is odd, by Lemma
2.1
,

\Phi^{*}_{m}(\sqrt{q_{0}})\neq 1
. Since
m
is odd, it is easy to see that
|\mathrm{U}_{m}(\sqrt{q_{0}})|
is indivisible by
\Phi^{*}_{m}(\sqrt{q_{0}})
; however,
|T|
is divisible by
q_{0}^{m}-1\over q-1
, a contradiction.
Suppose that (iii) occurs. For the first six triples, we have
b={n\over m}=2
and
q=p
, and then they are excluded by calculating
|T|
and
{q^{n}-1\over q-1}
. Similarly, the triple

(\mathrm{Suz}(q_{0}),4,2^{nf\over m})
is excluded as
|\mathrm{Suz}(q_{0})|
is indivisible by
q_{0}+1
. Thus
T\cong\mathrm{G}_{2}(q_{0})
, desired as in part (3).

Case 2
. Assume that
A\cong\mathrm{PSp}_{n}(q){.}[c]
. Then
T\lesssim\mathrm{PSp}_{n}(q)
for even
n
.
Noting that
T
is not an alternating group,
n-1
is an odd prime and
f
is a power of
n-1
, by
[
2
, Theorem 3.1]
, one of the following holds:

(iv)

T\cong\Omega^{-}_{n}(q)
(with
q
even) or
\mathrm{PSp}_{n}(q)
;

(v)

T\lesssim\mathrm{PSp}_{m}(q^{n\over m})
, where
m
is a proper divisor of
n
;

(vi)

T\lesssim\mathrm{U}_{n\over 2}(q)
, where
{n\over 2}q
is odd;

(vii)

(T,n,q)
is one of the following triples:

(\mathrm{J}_{2},6,5)
,
(\mathrm{L}_{3}(2),6,3)
,
(\mathrm{L}_{3}(2),6,5)
,
(\mathrm{L}_{2}(13),6,3)
,
(\mathrm{L}_{2}(13),6,4)
,
(\mathrm{L}_{2}(17),8,2)
,

(\mathrm{L}_{2}(25),12,2)
,
(\mathrm{L}_{2}(37),18,2)
,
(\mathrm{L}_{2}(41),20,2)
,
(\mathrm{L}_{3}(3),12,2)
,
(\mathrm{L}_{3}(4),6,3)
,

(\mathrm{PSp}_{4}(5),12,2)
,

(\mathrm{U}_{3}(9),6,5)
,
(\mathrm{Suz}(q),4,2^{f})
and
(\mathrm{G}_{2}(q),6,2^{f})
.

Recall that
|T|
is divisible by
q^{n}-1\over q-1
.
For
T\lesssim\mathrm{U}_{n\over 2}(q)
, since
{n\over 2}q
is odd,
\Phi^{*}_{n\over 2}(q)\neq 1
; however, it is easy to see that
|\mathrm{U}_{n\over 2}(q)|
is indivisible by
\Phi^{*}_{n\over 2}(q)
, a contradiction.

For case (vii),
only the triples
(\mathrm{L}_{2}(13),6,3)
and
(\mathrm{G}_{2}(q),6,2^{f})
are left. If
(n,q)=(6,3)
then
r={3^{5}-1\over 3-1}
, which is not a prime, a contradiction.
Thus
T\cong\mathrm{G}_{2}(q)
, desired as in part (3) of this lemma.
For case (iv), either
T
is desired in part (3) of this lemma, or
T\cong\Omega^{-}_{n}(q)
with odd
{n\over 2}
; the latter does occur as
|T|
has a divisor
q^{n\over 2}-1\over q-1
.

The left case is that
T\lesssim\mathrm{PSp}_{m}(q^{n\over m})
. We choose
m
minimal as possible.
If
m=2
then
T\lesssim\mathrm{PSp}_{2}(q^{n\over 2})
, yielding
T\cong\mathrm{PSp}_{2}(q^{n\over 2})
, desired as in part (3) of this lemma. Thus we may let
m\geq 4
.
Clearly
q^{n\over m}
is not a prime, and
m-1
is odd.
Using
[
2
, Theorem 3.1]
, by a similar argument as above,
T
is described as in part (3).
∎

9.
The proof of Theorem
2

Let
\Gamma=(V,E)
be a connected
X
-symmetric Cayley
graph of a finite simple group
T

of prime valency
r\geq 7
, where
T\leq X\leq\mathbf{Aut}(\Gamma)
. Assume that
T
is not normal in
X
, and

X_{\alpha}
is insolvable, where
\alpha\in V
. By Theorem
1
(2), we choose a characteristic subgroup
G
of
X
such that
that
G^{\infty}=G\gneq\mathbf{rad}(G)T
,
G/\mathbf{rad}(G)
is simple,
r\not\in\pi(\mathbf{rad}(G))
,
\mathbf{rad}(G)
is intransitive on
V
and
\Gamma
is
G
-symmetric.
By Lemma
2.8
,
\mathbf{rad}(G)
is semiregular on
V
, and so
|\mathbf{rad}(G)|
is a proper divisor of
|V|=|T|
.

Set
\overline{G}=G/\mathbf{rad}(G)
and
\overline{T}=T\mathbf{rad}(G)/\mathbf{rad}(G)
, and
identify
\overline{G}
with a subgroup of

\mathbf{Aut}(\Gamma_{G/\mathbf{rad}(G)})
. Then

\Gamma_{G/\mathbf{rad}(G)}
is
\overline{G}
-symmetric, and
\overline{G}=\overline{T}\,\overline{G}_{\overline{a}}
, where
\overline{\alpha}=\alpha^{\mathbf{rad}(G)}
. Thus
(\overline{G},\overline{T},\overline{G}_{\overline{a}})
is one of the triples satisfying Hypothesis
4.1
.
Since
G\unlhd X
, we have
G_{\alpha}\unlhd X_{\alpha}
, and so
G_{\alpha}^{\Gamma(\alpha)}\unlhd X_{\alpha}^{\Gamma(\alpha)}
. Since
X_{\alpha}
is insolvable,
X_{\alpha}^{\Gamma(\alpha)}
is almost simple, and then
G_{\alpha}^{\Gamma(\alpha)}
is almost simple. In particular,
G_{\alpha}
is insolvable.
Note that
\overline{G}_{\overline{a}}\cong G_{\overline{a}}/\mathbf{rad}(G)=\mathbf{rad}(G)G_{\alpha}/\mathbf{rad}(G)\cong G_{\alpha}
. Then
\overline{G}_{\overline{a}}
is insolvable. Thus
(\overline{G},\overline{T},\overline{G}_{\overline{a}})
can be read out from Theorems
6.1
,
6.2
and

8.1
-
8.7
.

Note that
T\cong\overline{T}
and
{|T||G_{\alpha}|\over|\mathbf{rad}(G)|}=|G:\mathbf{rad}(G)|=|\overline{G}|=|\overline{T}||\overline{G}_{\overline{a}}:(\overline{G}_{\overline{a}}\cap\overline{T})|
. Then
|\mathbf{rad}(G)|=|\overline{G}_{\overline{a}}\cap\overline{T}|={|G_{\alpha}|\over|\overline{G}:\overline{T}|}
, and so
|\overline{G}:\overline{T}|_{r}=r
. Thus we only need to deal with those triples
(\overline{G},\overline{T},\overline{G}_{\overline{a}})
with
|\overline{G}:\overline{T}|_{r}=r
.
For further argument, we need more precise structural information about
G
. Note that
G
is perfect, i.e.,
G^{\prime}=G
.

Lemma 9.1
.

Let
L=\overline{G}
and
R_{s}=\min\{m\mid L\lesssim\mathrm{PGL}_{m}(q_{0}),q_{0}\mbox{ a power of }s\}
, where
s
is a prime. Then either
G
is a perfect central extension of
L
, or
|\mathbf{rad}(G)|_{s}\geq q_{0}^{R_{s}}
for some prime
s
and its a power
q_{0}
with
L\lesssim\mathrm{PGL}_{m}(q_{0})
. For the latter case, if
L\cong\mathrm{A}_{n}
for
n\geq 9
then
n
is a power of
2
and
|\mathbf{rad}(G)|_{2}\geq 2^{n-2}
.

Proof.

Assume that
G
is not a perfect central extension of
L
.
Recalling
G^{\infty}=G
, it is easily shown that
G/K
is perfect for each normal subgroup
K
of
G
with
K\leq\mathbf{rad}(G)
.
Choose a minimal
K
such that
G/K
is a perfect central extension of
L
. Then
K\neq 1
. Choose
J\unlhd G
with
J\leq K
such that

K/J
is a minimal normal subgroup of
G/J
. Since
K
is solvable, we set

K/J\cong\mathbb{Z}_{s}^{l}
for some prime
s
and integer
l\geq 1
.

Since
G/J=(K/J){.}(G/K)
and
G/J
is perfect, by the choice of
K
,
G/J=(K/J){.}(G/K)
is not a central extension; otherwise,
G/J
should be a perfect central extension of
L
, refer to
[
1
, pp. 167, (33.5)]
. Thus
G/J
acts nontrivially on
K/J
induced by the conjugation of
G
on
K
, and the kernel is
\mathbf{C}_{G/J}(K/J)
. Set
C/J=\mathbf{C}_{G/J}(K/J)
. Then
C\leq\mathbf{rad}(G)
, and

G/C\cong(G/J)/(C/J)\lesssim\mathrm{GL}_{l_{0}}(q_{0})
, where
s^{l}=q_{0}^{l_{0}}
. It follows that
l_{0}\geq R_{s}
, refer to
[
25
, pp.184, Proposition 5.3.2]
.
Then
|\mathbf{rad}(G)|_{s}\geq q_{0}^{l_{0}}\geq q_{0}^{R_{s}}
.

Suppose that
L\cong\mathrm{A}_{n}
for some
n\geq 9
. Then, by
[
25
, Propositions 5.3.2 and 5.3.7]
,
l\geq R_{s}\geq n-2
. Thus
|\mathrm{A}_{n}|_{s}\geq s^{n-2}
, and then

n-2+(2,s)\leq\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\lfloor{n\over s^{i}}\rfloor
. The only possibility is that
s=2
and
n
is a power of
2
. Then the lemma follows.
∎

We next finish the proof of Theorem
2
by checking one by one the possible candidates for
(\overline{G},\overline{T},\overline{G}_{\overline{a}})
described in Theorems
6.1
,
6.2
and

8.1
-
8.7
. Recall that
|\mathbf{rad}(G)|=|\overline{G}_{\overline{a}}\cap\overline{T}|
and
|\overline{G}:\overline{T}|_{r}=r
.

Assume that
L\cong\mathrm{A}_{n}
. Then, by Theorem
6.1
, either
T\cong\mathrm{A}_{n-1}
as at Line 1 of Table
1.1
, or

\overline{G}\cong\mathrm{A}_{r+1}
for
r\geq 7
,
\overline{G}_{\overline{a}}\cong\mathrm{A}_{r}
,
\Gamma_{G/\mathbf{rad}(G)}\cong\mathsf{K}_{r+1}
and
T\cong\overline{T}
is isomorphic to a transitive subgroup of
\mathrm{A}_{r+1}
. Since
|\overline{G}:\overline{T}|_{r}=r
, we have
r\not\in\pi(T)
. Checking the subgroups of
\mathrm{A}_{8}
, we conclude that
\mathrm{A}_{8}
has no insolvable transitive subgroup with order indivisible by
7
. Thus we have
r>7
, and
r+1>9
. By Lemma
9.1
either
G
is a perfect central extension of
\mathrm{A}_{r+1}
, or
|\mathbf{rad}(G)|_{2}\geq 2^{r-1}
. Recalling
|\mathbf{rad}(G)|
is a divisor of
|\overline{G}_{\overline{a}}|
, if
|\mathbf{rad}(G)|_{2}\geq 2^{r-1}
then

|\mathrm{A}_{r}|_{2}\geq 2^{r-1}
, which is impossible. Thus
G
is a perfect central extension of
\mathrm{A}_{r+1}
, and so
|\mathbf{rad}(G)|\leq 2
, refer to
[
1
, pp. 170, (33.15)]
. If
|\mathbf{rad}(G)|=2
then
\Gamma
is bipartite by
[
10
, Theorem 1.1]
, which is not the case. Then
\mathbf{rad}(G)=1
, and

T
is desired at Line
2
of Table
1.1
.

Assume that
L
is a sporadic simple group. By Theorem

6.2
,
\overline{G}\cong\mathrm{M}_{n}
,
\overline{G}_{\overline{a}}\cong\mathrm{M}_{n-1}
,
\Gamma_{G/\mathbf{rad}(G)}\cong\mathsf{K}_{n}
,
n=12
or
24
and
\overline{T}\cong\mathrm{A}_{5}
or
\mathrm{L}_{3}(2)
. In this case,
|\mathbf{rad}(G)|=|\overline{G}_{\overline{a}}\cap\overline{T}|=s\in\{5,7\}
.
It follows that
G\cong\mathbb{Z}_{s}\times\mathrm{M}_{n}
, which contradicts the choice of
G
.

Assume that
L
is a classical simple group. Then Theorems
8.1
-
8.7
work here, and either the pair
(L,T)
is desired as in Table
1.1
or
(\overline{G},\overline{T},S,r)
is listed as follows:

\tiny\begin{array}[]{l|l|l|l|l}|\overline{G}:\overline{T}|&\overline{G}&\overline{T}&\overline{G}_{\overline{\alpha}}&r\\
\hline\cr 3^{7}\cdot 13&\mathrm{P\Omega}^{+}_{8}(3)&\Omega^{+}_{8}(2)&\overline{G}_{\overline{a}}\cong[3^{6}]{:}\mathrm{SL}_{3}(3)&13\\
\hline\cr 2^{10}\cdot 3^{2}\cdot 7\cdot 31&\Omega^{+}_{12}(2)&\Omega^{-}_{10}(2)&\mathrm{SL}_{5}(2)\preccurlyeq\overline{G}_{\overline{\alpha}}&31\\
\hline\cr 2^{4}\cdot 3^{2}\cdot 7&\mathrm{L}_{4}(4)&\mathrm{Sp}_{4}(4)&\mathrm{SL}_{3}(2)\preccurlyeq\overline{G}_{\overline{\alpha}},\,\mathbf{O}_{p}(\overline{G}_{\overline{\alpha}})\neq 1&7\\
\hline\cr 2^{10}\cdot 3\cdot 5\cdot 7\cdot 31&\mathrm{L}_{6}(2)&\mathrm{U}_{3}(3)&\mathrm{SL}_{5}(2)\preccurlyeq\overline{G}_{\overline{\alpha}}&31\\
\hline\cr 2^{mf}(2^{mf}+1)&\mathrm{Sp}_{2m}(2^{f}),m\geq 4&\Omega^{+}_{2m}(2^{f})&\mathrm{SL}_{2}(2^{mf})\lesssim\overline{G}_{\overline{a}}\lesssim\mathrm{\Gamma L}_{2}(2^{mf})&2^{mf}+1\\
\end{array}

Recall that
|\mathbf{rad}(G)|=|\overline{G}_{\overline{a}}\cap\overline{T}|={|\overline{G}_{\overline{\alpha}}|\over|\overline{G}:\overline{T}|}
.

Suppose that
\overline{G}\cong\mathrm{Sp}_{2m}(2^{f})
and
\overline{T}\cong\Omega^{+}_{2m}(2^{f})
, where
m\geq 4
and
mf
is a power of
2
.
Then
|\mathbf{rad}(G)|
is a divisor of
mf(2^{mf}-1)^{2}
and divisible by
2^{mf}-1
. If
G
is a perfect central extension of
\mathrm{Sp}_{2m}(2^{f})
then
|\mathbf{rad}(G)|\leq(2,2^{f}-1)=1
by
[
25
, pp. 173, Theorem 5.1.4]
, a contradiction. Thus, by Lemma
9.1
,

|\mathbf{rad}(G)|_{s}\geq q_{0}^{R_{s}}
, where
s\in\pi(\mathbf{rad}(G))
,
R_{s}
is defined as in Lemma
9.1
,
q_{0}
is a power of
s
and
q_{0}=2^{f}
if
s=2
. Note that
R_{s}
is given as in
[
25
, Theorem 5.3.9 and Proposition 5.4.13]
. If
s=2
then
R_{s}=2m
, and so

mf\geq|\mathbf{rad}(G)|_{s}\geq(2^{f})^{R_{s}}=2^{2mf}
, which is impossible.
Thus
s
is odd, and so
R_{s}\geq{1\over 2}2^{(m-1)f}(2^{(m-1)f}-1)(2^{f}-1)
by
[
25
, Theorem 5.3.9]
.
It follows that
\log_{s}(2^{mf}-1)^{2}\geq\log_{s}|\mathbf{rad}(G)|_{s}\geq R_{s}\geq{1\over 2}2^{(m-1)f}(2^{(m-1)f}-1)(2^{f}-1)
.
Then
4\log_{s}(2^{mf}-1)\geq 2^{(m-1)f}(2^{(m-1)f}-1)(2^{f}-1)
, and so

\log_{s}(2^{mf}-1)>2^{(m-1)f}-1
as
m\geq 4
.
We have
mf>\log_{s}(2^{mf}-1)>2^{(m-1)f}-1
, yielding
mf+1>2^{(m-1)f}>2(m-1)f
. This implies that
m\leq 2
, a contradiction.

Suppose that
\overline{G}\cong\Omega^{+}_{12}(2)
and
\overline{T}\cong\Omega^{-}_{10}(2)
. By Lemma
4.2
, either
|\mathbf{O}_{2}(\overline{G}_{\overline{\alpha}})|=2^{30}

or
|\mathbf{O}_{2}(\overline{G}_{\overline{\alpha}})|\leq 2^{10}
. Noting that
2^{30}=|\Omega^{+}_{12}(2)|\geq|\mathbf{O}_{2}(\overline{G}_{\overline{\alpha}})||\mathrm{SL}_{5}(2)|_{2}
, we have
|\mathbf{O}_{2}(\overline{G}_{\overline{\alpha}})|\leq 2^{20}
, and so
|\mathbf{O}_{2}(\overline{G}_{\overline{\alpha}})|\leq 2^{10}
. Then
|\overline{G}_{\overline{\alpha}}|
is a divisor of
2^{10}|\mathrm{SL}_{5}(2)|
and divisible by
|\mathrm{SL}_{5}(2)|
.
It follows that
|\mathbf{rad}(G)|={|\overline{G}_{\overline{\alpha}}|\over|\overline{G}:\overline{T}|}=2^{i}\cdot 3\cdot 5
for some
i\leq 10
.
By
[
25
, Theorem 5.3.9 and Proposition 5.4.13]
, we have
|\mathbf{rad}(G)|_{s}<s^{R_{s}}
for
s\in\{2,3,5\}
, and then
G
is a perfect central extension of
\mathrm{P\Omega}^{+}_{12}(2)
by Lemma
9.1
.
Thus
|\mathbf{rad}(G)|\leq(4,2^{6}-1)=1
by
[
25
, pp. 173, Theorem 5.1.4]
, a contradiction.

Finally, if
(\overline{G},\overline{T})
is one of
(\mathrm{P\Omega}^{+}_{8}(3),\mathrm{P\Omega}^{+}_{8}(2))
,
(\mathrm{L}_{4}(4),\mathrm{Sp}_{4}(4))
and
(\mathrm{L}_{6}(2),\mathrm{U}_{3}(3))
then a similar contradiction arises as above, we omit the details here. This completes the proof of Theorem
2
.
